PLAN
====

PART 1: OPEN ISSUES (cleaned 2026-11-06: the open items first, the done ones kept below)
-------------------
- DONE 2026-11-12: the tiles feature labeled beta 0.5 in the app (the Options tab's "Keep backgrounds as tiles (tilemap, beta 0.5)" on DPC+ and standard, the Playfield tab's "Tiles (beta 0.5)" section header).
- DONE 2026-11-12: Scene card: the offset chips were right under the Remove trigger button (a trigger's fields have no space below); now 12 pixels apart, also the hint line under an offset area's fields.
- DONE 2026-11-12 Scene triggers: a "Trigger function/subroutine" checkbox on a selected trigger; when on, a dropdown on a row under it picks a Subroutine or a Function of the Blocks tab, and a Function shows a number field for each argument it reads. The trigger then calls it the frame it fires (also with no On trigger block in the project). Kept beside the values as the card's triggerCalls (utils/scene.js); the build puts a Call block (subroutine_call or function_call_statement with number arguments) inside the trigger_check block, run the frame it fires; a call to a block no longer on the Blocks tab is left out. Tested in the emulator (tests/projects/mktrigcall.cjs): a Subroutine and a Function with 2 arguments set the score on standard, DPC+ and PXE.
- DONE 2026-11-12 4-pixel tiles: a "Try 4-pixel tiles" checkbox in the Playfield to tiles popup; when on, the tiles are made 4 pixels wide instead when that takes fewer bytes (the map has 8 tile numbers a row). The build follows the Tiles section's width: DPC+ (armprog.c, the descriptor's bit 6: two tiles a byte, two rows of a tile a byte), the standard kernel (the 6502 ORs the two halves; Set playfield tile, columns 0 to 7, writes only its half). Done that way, with: the 4-pixel reading in separate coprocessor programs (letter n, only with t; 178 bytes more, the 254 others byte for byte the same); the standard tall scroll keeps 8-pixel tiles (a scroll step builds a row in the game's time and 4-pixel rows took about 150 cycles more: the picture started 2 lines late with Superchip); a DPC+ build with no Set playfield tile block now follows the section's width too. Tested in the emulator (tests/projects/mknarrow.cjs, tests/hooks/hook_narrow.cjs for the standard kernel): the scroll projects (DPC+ plain and tall, standard fit and tall, also with Superchip) the same 600 frames as rows; Set playfield tile at columns 2, 4, 5, 7 (DPC+, also flipped) and 5 (standard, also Superchip) the same as the background drawn so; 8-pixel output the same as before (40 random layouts, both kernels).
- 2026-11-12 Shrink the DPC+ coprocessor program (armprog.c): looked at. Each set alone: o 780, b 532, c 416, p 1142, f 764, a 256, t 450 bytes (the biggest functions: bouncepix 670, firemove 540, box 448, findpixel 376, circle 348, pass 308). Compiler options save at most 16 bytes (-fno-tree-loop-optimize; -O2 is 1260 more); reading CF and K once (not volatile) at most 4 to 48 a set. More would take rewriting the bounce, fire and box code (and testing them all again): not done, asked.
- 2026-11-12 Test on DPC+ with a high playfield row count: collision, the box collision platformer, bounce, fire, gravity; check for screen rolling (the frame not 262 lines). tests/projects/mkdpcstress.cjs (48, 96, 192 rows), tests/emulator/rollcheck.cjs (the lines of each frame with joystick input). Found: 192 rows (one line a row) did not build: the Bounce, Find playfield pixel and Fire (coprocessor) steps gave the coprocessor a row increment of 256 ("lda #256"); now 0, which armprog.c reads as 256. (The programs with p or f rebuilt: 20 and 8 bytes more; 48 and 96 rows the same 900 frames as before.) No rolling at 48, 96 or 192 rows: every frame the same length over 1500 frames with 3 input patterns; Player 0 lands on the floor, the ball moves. Not checked by eye in the app.
- DONE 2026-11-12 The DPC+ high row count test also at 64 rows (3 scanlines a row) and 32 rows (6): both build, no rolling over 1500 frames with 3 input patterns, Player 0 lands on the floor. (There the 6502 does the Bounce: whole-line rows, so no coprocessor row step is worked out for it.)
- DONE 2026-11-12 Bounce on the coprocessor also with rows a whole number of lines that does not divide 256 (3, 5, 6, 7... lines: 64 and 32 rows), which the 6502 did; with it Find playfield pixel and Fire's playfield check too. Later maybe: Inertia objects, bouncing players, backgrounds with different row heights. Done: the row height is given with a divide bit (bit 7 of the directions byte for Bounce and Find pixel, bit 6 of Fire's flags) and armprog.c divides (rowat); the coprocessor Bounce now also looks at whether the step back changes the row, as the 6502's reflect does (it only looked at the column). Programs with p 132 bytes more (1162 to 1294), with f 28 more. Tested (mkdpcstress.cjs, now with Fire's playfield check and _fc variants with Fire on the coprocessor): 48, 96, 192 rows the same 1200 frames as before; 32 and 64 rows the same headings in the same order as the 6502, but around a bounce the position is a pixel apart for a few frames (not found why; at 48 rows the two are the same frames). FOUND: at 32 and 64 rows the frames roll (1 to 6 lines long) with Fire's playfield check done by the 6502 (it divides by 3 or 6 with a loop, several times a step); also before these changes. With Fire on the coprocessor (allowed there now) no rolling. OPEN: a faster 6502 division for those rows (or Fire on the coprocessor by itself there). Also: tools/armprog/build.sh twice made one program 4 or 8 bytes different from a compile of it alone (obpfat, obfatn): look at why.
- DONE 2026-11-12 Looked into tools/armprog/build.sh making a program a few bytes different from a compile of it alone (obpfat once, obfatn once): not found. The compiler gives the same bytes every time (300 compiles of obfatn), two whole runs of build.sh came out the same as each other and as the programs in the repo, and the odd files were not another program nor the source before the last change. build.sh now deletes each step's file first, checks the program is not empty, and compiles each program twice: when the two differ it compiles a third time, keeps the one two agree on and prints a warning (stops when all three differ).
- 2026-11-12 IDEA (asked whether possible, not to do yet): store backgrounds as runs of rows (a byte and how many rows, as Pitfall II only changes the playfield where it needs to), compressed when the ROM is compiled, the rows per background staying variable as now and drawn as now; DPC+: the coprocessor expands the runs into the column queues at load (like the tilemap's function 106), so the kernel, collision, offset, pixel blocks and scrolling are unchanged; kept only when smaller than rows or tiles; the row colors can be done the same way. Standard kernel: little to gain (12 or 24 rows). PXE: check its playfield memory first. Saves ROM, not cycles.
- 2026-11-12 Runs measured on Bricks of Olympus (5 x 22 rows) and Passage 0-0-7 (DPC+, 9 x 21 rows), counting the coprocessor program (tile loader +426 bytes, +604 with 4-pixel tiles; a run loader +132, packed runs +156): Bricks rows 440, tiles 655, runs 451; Passage rows 756, tiles 969, runs 603 (153 less than rows). FOUND: on DPC+ the build keeps tiles whenever the option is on, without weighing them (and their program) against the rows as the standard kernel does: in both projects tiles take more ROM than rows. To do when asked: weigh the program in; runs as an option kept when smallest.

Options tab "Keep backgrounds as runs" (DPC+, not PXE); planRuns, runsLoadLines, runsDataLines in pf-tiles.js (the data between the tiles' labels, moved into the graphics bank); armprog.c function 112 (runsload, repeats the runs down the rows to fill for scrolling); programs with r (new letter, never with t: 128 new, 510 in all, the others the same; r alone 208 bytes, +184). Kept when runs + 184 < rows and < tiles + their program; never with a Set playfield tile block; the build log says which and why. - 2026-11-12 FOUND while testing runs with Passage 0-0-7: it did not build ("Unknown Mnemonic lda gState"): a variable used only by bit (or nibble) blocks was never given a byte, as those blocks pick it in a dropdown, not a variable field, and allUsedVarModels does not see it. Fixed: such variables are added (and counted as read).
- DONE 2026-11-12 Looked at PXE support for runs: the PXE ARM driver is fixed (no program of the project; its functions are called with jsr: pfsetup1 to pfsetup15 copy Y bytes of ROM into that many columns from each column's PF_WRITE_OFFSET, the buffers 256 rows a column), with no fill. Runs there: the 6502 calls pfsetup1 for each run from a ROM strip of that byte as long as its longest run (one strip a value, shared). Estimated: Bricks 527 against 440 as rows, Passage 787 against 756: no gain (only tall flat backgrounds would gain), and a PXE ROM is up to 60 KB without banks. Not done. Tiles fit PXE better: a tile column is already its bytes in ROM, so a cell is one pfsetup1 call (Set playfield tile too); flipped tiles need separate copies. Not done; asked.
- LATER (decided 2026-11-12: leave PXE as it is for now): PXE tiles (a cell a pfsetup1 call from the tile's bytes in ROM, Set playfield tile the same at its row; flipped tiles as separate copies; the wider PF_MODE widths as more tile columns) and PXE runs (pfsetup1 from a ROM strip a value: only worth it for tall flat backgrounds). See the entry above for the estimates.
- DONE 2026-11-13 Fix: on DPC+ the tiles are kept whenever the option is on, also when they and their part of the coprocessor program take more than the rows: weigh them as the standard kernel does (rows when smaller; tiles always with a Set playfield tile block). planBackgroundTilemap weighs tiles + 426 bytes (+178 with 4-pixel tiles) against the rows; the build log says why rows were kept; the Options hint says so. Tested (tests/projects/mktileweigh.cjs): 6 backgrounds of 96 rows from the same blocks keep the tiles, the same 300 frames as rows; 1 background of 24 rows: the same ROM with the option on as off; narrow_dpc_plain_t4 now rows (781 against 240); narrow_set_dpc_tiles (Set playfield tile) keeps its tiles.
- 2026-11-13 DPC+ stack, fuller support (asked; https://www.randomterrain.com/atari-2600-memories-batari-basic-commands.html#dpc_stack): push/pull take several variables at once, ranges (push j-m) and any variable (player0x, missile0x...), not only one user variable; save/restore at a place (stack 200, push; stack 199, pull) for 256 bytes of storage. FOUND: the docs say nothing else uses the stack, but the build pushes there too (Function argument snapshots, the box test's scratch byte), so a value saved near the pointer can be written over. Waiting for which parts to build.
- DONE 2026-11-13 DPC+ stack, decided: option 4 only, automatic (the user does not manage it): the build keeps variables in the stack's 256 bytes by itself (as extra variable storage), not push/pull blocks that take lists or slot blocks. Done: the variables that do not fit in the RAM go first to the stack's bytes (STACKbegin+n, from its top down; area 6 of displayRamVars, spill.js displayRamSlotAddress), then, with 127 rows or fewer and no scrolling, the tables' upper halves as before; a part of the stack's bottom is left for pushes (32 bytes, 8 more for each push or keep safe block), and none of it is used when the project sets the stack position itself. So DPC+ projects with more than 127 rows or a scrolling background now have 224 more bytes for variables (before: "too many variables"). The clears at the start, the ROM capacity list ("DPC+ stack byte n") and the debug overlay's addresses know the stack bytes. Tested (tests/projects/mkstackvars.cjs: 80 variables, each up by 1 a frame and checked against the first): 96 and 192 rows build, the score counts (137 at frame 400, 225 at frame 1000) and never shows the error; Passage 0-0-7 (its overflow now in the stack, 378 uses) the same 1500 frames as before.

- DONE 2026-11-13 Share the animation code and tables between the players that play the same animation (DPC+): do not duplicate code that does not need to be duplicated (asked). Done: poolAnimationGroups now also groups the virtual sprites (Players 2 to 9) that are not in a Sprite pool and play the same animations with the same settings (the signature the pools use): the animation code, its picture tables and the color tables are made once (the pool's shared code, each sprite running it with its state in temp bytes through a short step routine). The shared color code has a table for each member's default color, and their color variables start at those defaults (sharedColorStarts), as a sprite with code for itself falls back on its default. Not shared: Player 1 (the hardware sprite) and Player 0, whose code reads their variables by name.

- DONE, checked 2026-11-07 (not played by hand): Fire and Bounce vertical stretch on DPC+: a Bricks build rolled the screen when the paddle moved and let the ball through the playfield (found 2026-11-06). Cause: with the stretch the ball was moved by the 6502 pixel by pixel instead of by the coprocessor (the stretch had turned the Enable coprocessor movement off), too slow and wrong. Fixed 2026-11-06 (uncommitted): firemove in custom/armprog.c does the stretch itself (the 125 files of custom/prog rebuilt with C:/tmp/arm/buildprogs6.py; the Bricks program is 116 bytes smaller), and a playfield-checked object is stretched only where the coprocessor moves it. Checked in the emulator on Bricks 0-0-20: every frame 262 lines but a few, ball goes 2 lines a frame up and down, bounces off the paddle. Checked 2026-11-07 (all committed): the coprocessor firemove run on the emulated coprocessor against a JavaScript port of the same function (C:/tmp/arm/fuzz2.cjs, 4 builds of the program, 9000 random calls, speeds 1 to 255): no differences and no traps. Bricks 0-0-20 played by script (fire, then the ball): the ball goes 1 pixel across and about 1.6 lines up or down a frame, bounces off the top and right walls and the bricks, no roll, never through the playfield. Not played by hand. The speed above 14 overflow is not reachable: Fire and Set speed hold the speed to 7 (and a speed of 7 gives 119 in the byte arithmetic).
- Flash Score color block (asked 2026-11-07, built, committed 096a41e): "Flash Score [digits | bars | digits and bars] color to [color] every [frames] frames, [times] times" (score.js: blocks, generator, generateScoreFlashChecks; 5 bytes of state for the digits and 5 for the bars, only when a block uses them). The color shows for the frames, then the old color for as many frames, a flash being one of each, and the score ends in the color it had; started again while it runs it starts over (the old color is kept). The digits flash scorecolor (on DPC+ the 8 line bytes too: the old line colors come back when the Score tab gave the lines different colors); the bars flash pfscorecolor over the color they follow every frame. Bars follow the digits unless a Score bars set color block is used, so flashing the digits flashes the bars too in a project without that block (flash the bars alone, or use the block to flash the digits alone). Checked in the emulator on the standard kernel and DPC+ (digits, bars, both), and on DPC+ with Score tab line colors (the lines go all one color while it flashes and the Score tab colors come back; the bars flash alone with the lines kept). With "Score bars use the line colors" on (DPC+, no Score bars set color block) the bars take the colors of the score lines, which the digits' flash changes, so a flash of the bars alone flashes the digits too (a note goes to the build log); checked in the emulator. Does nothing on PXE.
- Score color swatch (asked 2026-11-07, built, committed 096a41e): the Score tab has a swatch "Score color" (config key scoreColor, scoreColorByteOf in utils/palette.js, $08 until one is picked) that is the color the score, the bars that follow it and on DPC+ the 8 lines (unless the Score tab gave the lines different colors) start in, in place of the fixed $08; a Score color block still changes it. Checked in the emulator on the standard kernel and DPC+ (red swatch, no blocks). Not done: the digits in the font editor are still drawn in orange, not in the Score color.
- Score tab: the checkbox "Use the line colors" (done 2026-11-06, key dpcPlusScoreLineColorsOff) turns the score line colors off and on without losing them; not played in the emulator yet.
- Sprite pool, asked 2026-11-08 (stop leaving features out, implement the pool properly), built, committed b389f1d: (a) a pool inside a custom function is no longer copied for each sprite: one subroutine of the blocks, and one exchange subroutine for each function (_poolswap_A_B_in_<function>), both kept in the function's bank (the HOST field of the internal blocks, Blockly.BBasic.functionHostedSubroutines, computeFunctionFamilies in hooks/rom.js unions them with the function); measured on the emulator the stack is 2 bytes deeper than the copy version (10 against 8), the price of one gosub; (b) the sprite overlap event has pool sprite / any / all pool sprites, each pool sprite keeping separate overlap bits (spriteFlagBits from overlapSpriteFlagBits, the blocks of the copies tagged through a <data> name); found on the way: the box test answers of the pairs of sprites (the Collided block with players 2 and up) were not exchanged, so a Collided in a pool gave every sprite the answer of the first one: now exchanged; (c) a block outside a pool that names one pool sprite (Gravity start on Player 3 ...) gets a copy kept as a shadow for the other sprites of the pool, so all of them have the same state (the compile log note about different state no longer comes up for it); (d) a pool with Return (with a value), Go to label, Change state or Return from a subroutine in it is a subroutine too: the block leaves its number in temp1 (the value in temp6) and returns, and the block runs after the call. Checked on DPC+ in the emulator against the same blocks on plain Players 2 and 3 (identical frames): Change state to Gameover from a pool (the Gameover start event runs on the same frame as with plain sprites; only which of the two flickering sprites is drawn on a given frame differs), Collided, overlap starts, overlap with any pool sprite, Go to label, Return with a value from a pool inside a function, a pool in a function called from an event in another bank, and the five earlier pool tests (Platform, Scroll, Bounce, Fire, Fire 2). PXE finished 2026-11-08: the Distance and Find playfield pixel blocks list Players 2 to 16 on PXE (spriteOptionsWithPool, spriteCollisionCoords in generators/bbasic/background.js use EXTRA_PLAYER_NAME); Distance X of Players 5 and 12 against Player 0 gives the right 70 on PXE and DPC+; Find playfield pixel column of Players 5 and 12 on PXE gives the same column as Players 1 and 5 on DPC+ at two x positions (5 at x 40, 20 at x 100). The PXE pool tests that differed from DPC+ were not pool faults: every pool test run on the same kernel against the same blocks on plain Players 2 and 3 gives identical frames on PXE as well (Platform, Scroll, Bounce, Fire, Fire 2, Seek, Lock, Inertia, Collided, overlap starts; frames 300 and 450, after the PXE logo); the old DPC+ against PXE comparisons used pools that start at Player 1 (which draws in another place than the virtual sprites) and spawn every frame. Not done: a Set scene with a pool choice (what it should mean is not decided), Fade color on pool sprites (not possible: the colors of Players 2 to 9 are ROM tables), Pools starting at Player 1 looked into 2026-11-08: the pool at Player 1 gives frames identical to plain Players 1 and 2 on DPC+ (static positions, spawn in the update event, Rainbow colors). The 30 lines higher sprite seen earlier came from a bad test (C:/tmp/shots/pool4b.cjs has no Release all sprites at the start, so the sprites were still visible, nothing was spawned and the defaults were drawn). What the look did find: a pool whose blocks name a sprite of the pool (Set Player 1 Y inside a pool of Players 1 to 2) cannot share one copy, because the block means the same sprite whichever one the pool is working on but with the sprites exchanged it would hit the one swapped into its place (seen: Player 1 stayed where it was and the second sprite got the value); decided 2026-11-08 (least ROM, no copies, no extra cycles or RAM): such a block is left out of the build (disabled, like a pool sprite outside a pool) with a note in the compile log, and the pool block shows a warning in the editor saying which sprites are named and to use "pool sprite" or put the block outside the pool (namedPoolMembers in blocks/sprite-pool.js, the first loop of expandSpritePools in utils/sprite-pool.js; a pool of one sprite is not affected); checked: the compile log note comes up, the block is left out, and the ordinary pool tests (Platform, Fire, Seek, Rainbow at Player 1) still equal plain.
- DONE, committed b389f1d: Rainbow color range (asked 2026-11-08; a range is a span of hues): the Player and Playfield rainbow colors blocks have "hues from [any hue | 0 to F] to [...]" (HUEFROM, HUETO; any hue keeps the old code; a span can go round, 14 to 2; old projects load as any hue). Built as a repeating table made at build time (generators/bbasic/rainbow-range.js): a ramp of the hues of the span, one hue a row, luminance 10, as long as the tallest rainbow sprite (64 rows) plus the span; the offset of the block only picks the start row (the offset modulo the span, by a fixed number of subtract steps). Standard kernel: ROM data in the main bank, pointer = table address + start row (16 bits); the playfield one reads its table 4 bytes on per row, so each color is repeated 4 times in a table of 128 rows (the first try with one byte per row showed one hue for a span of 4). DPC+ and PXE players: the coprocessor reads colors from the cartridge data and not from RAM (a table copied to the display memory showed one flat color), so the table is declared like an animation color table ("playerNcolor:", which sets the pointer) every frame the rainbow runs and the start row is added to the pointer; that costs about 70 to 80 cycles and about 70 bytes of ROM for each rainbow player. DPC+ and PXE playfield: the rainbow rows it loads already, only the hue list changes (the 255 rows join up only when the span divides 255). A later block of the same player or the playfield with another span is told in the compile log (the first is used). - Bank packer (asked 2026-11-08, built, builds checked on the Bricks variants, committed): a build that overflows is now assembled a second time with every bank given a separate room in the output file (utils/bank-packer.js, measureBanks in hooks/bb-compiler.js: the file origins of the banks are spread out, the program addresses are not changed), which gives the room left in every bank (the old way only knew the banks before the first one that overflowed) and the address of every label, so the exact size of each relocatable unit (events, graphics, subroutines, families; graphics now have a _reloc_entry and a _reloc_end label in the main bank too). planLayoutFromMeasurement (hooks/rom.js) then plans the whole layout in one step with a seeded annealing search (planBankPacking): every bank keeps a margin, what runs often stays in the main bank, units that were measured are moved again only when they have to. Sizes found are remembered for the rest of the build; a unit never measured is guessed from its estimate times the ratio found for its kind. DPC+ only (the other kernels have the same addresses in every bank, so labels cannot be told apart: they keep the old way, as does any build where the measuring did not work, and a plan that does not converge falls back to it after 12 layouts). New: on DPC+ with the Text Minikernel the top bank (bank 6, 2,257 bytes free in Bricks) can take units too: they go in the minikernel's section after its code (textTopBank, TOP_BANK_UNITS_MARKER); this is what made the pooled Bricks fit. Same project laid out by the old and the new way gives the same frames (Bricks 0-0-20, 36 frames of 700, with fire taps). Not done: the standard kernel (needs per-bank symbols), a better first guess for the units of the main bank, the music bank with the Text Minikernel on DPC+ (still not the top bank), a check of music in bank 6.

Next candidates, measured on a 1,822 cycle pass of Bricks 0-0-22 (cycles a pass, expected saving): (a) the spill chooses by the number of places that use a name, not by how often they run: the ball's fire state and the NUSIZ lines read about 17 places in the display memory each pass at about 17 cycles a pointer (298 cycles in the pointer lines alone, measured): weigh the uses in the per-pass code (fire, animation tests, box checks) ten times: 150 to 250; (b) the animation test of a player whose used animations all have one picture (the paddle): the counter and its test cost 78 a pass (player1animationsStart), and once the picture is loaded (bit 7) nothing is left to do: jump to the end: about 70; (b2) _run_once_edge_reset (about 77 a pass with its return: it reads the run-once byte in the display memory and works out the fired bits every pass): skip it when no run-once block can have fired this pass: about 50; (c) the box tests of the sprite pairs (_boxpair_*, about 130 a pass) run for hidden sprites too: skip a pair when a sprite has height 0 (the blank): about 60; (d) the fire, platform and box checks are called one by one into other banks (about 45 cycles each way): one call to a family that runs them: about 90; (e) the ball's coprocessor call (function 101) takes about 235 cycles in the pass: start it first and read its result last, if the 6507 can run while it works (to be found out in the emulator); (f) the NUSIZ line of each player is written every pass from a size byte in the display memory (about 20 each): write it when the size changes; (g) the exchange of the sprites for a pool body (poolbody swaps) still exchanges everything through the fetchers: the same temp byte idea could be used for the bodies that only read a sprite; (h) ROM: the pool step routines read bytes in the display memory (about 200 bytes more than the exchange had): keep the loads that the code does not need (the color when there is one table). ROUND 2 (asked 2026-11-08, built, committed; items a, b, b2, c, d, f, h of the list above; Bricks 0-0-22 pass 1,822 to 1,645 cycles, ROM 288 bytes smaller): (b) processAnimations: a DPC+ player whose used animations all have one picture and that no 'animation has finished' block watches gets a quick test of the loaded flag only (singlePicture; the counter and the limit test are left out); (b2) dpc-ram-flags.js runOnceResetLines: a byte that is 0 is written back at once; (c) sprite-box-width.js: the hidden test of the sprite pairs comes first, before the tests of the heights; (f) the NUSIZ line of a player 2 and up that a visibility block can hide is left out while its frame is 255 (bbasic.js extraPlayerNusizLines), and the block that shows it sets it first (sprites.js, showLines in the visibility setter; Blockly.BBasic.extraPlayerNusizLine); (d) bbasic.js combineWrappedChecks: Fire, Seek, Inertia, Jump and Lock checks that are wrapped and follow one another in the main loop are called through one subroutine _pass_checks (a family with them: the main bank makes one call, about 70 cycles less), buildRom makes the build again with them called one by one when the family does not fit (combineChecksOff); (h) the routine of a pool sprite does not read the color when the code has one color table (colorLadder) and does not read the animation number when the group has one animation; (a) spill.js lineWeights: a use of a name counts 100 in the main loop and what it calls on every pass (labels main, commongamelogic, the checks, the pool step routines) and 1 in the events that run once (this changed nothing in Bricks: it needs 26 spills and has exactly 26 names that can be spilled, so all of them are, the hot ones too; it can only help a project with room). Checked after (frames of the new builds against the builds before, or against sprites without a pool): pool of 3 and 2, pool2, pool3b, pool4, pool4b, pool6 with the sprites at different heights, finished flags, wide pool, set width, hide and show on DPC+ and on PXE: 0 frames differing in 81 to 134 sampled frames each; a 3 powerup Bricks ran 1,500 frames with no crash; cut frames in the forced-spawn test 61 to 55 of 300. - Code size and cycles round, checks 2026-11-08 (committed): standard kernel tested, the combined fire/seek/inertia/platformer/lock check (_pass_checks) gives the same 76 frames as the separate checks on an 8K standard ROM. (e) the DPC+ coprocessor call: the 235 cycles are the 6507 side marshalling the parameters (spilled reads are most of it), the emulator runs the coprocessor program in no 6507 time at all, so nothing is lost waiting; only fewer spilled parameter reads, or a change in the coprocessor program (no build tools for it here), would cut it. Not planned. (g) pool body swaps: the profile of the code after drawscreen (the vblank part ends at drawscreen, the budget there is about 2,812 cycles; game code runs in the overscan after it) of the forced-spawn three powerup Bricks gives a median of 674 and a maximum of 2,904 cycles; 12 of 244 passes are heavy, with about 458 cycles of bank switching and about 210 of swaps (_poolswap_*_for_poolbody*), the average swap cost is 95 to 130 cycles a pass. A step routine that swaps, runs the body and swaps back, kept in the body's bank, would cut the cross bank calls of the heavy passes; DONE 2026-11-09 (committed 6698f99): a block "sprite_pool_step" for each sprite after the first of a pool, a routine _poolstep_<first>_<n>_for_<body> that swaps, calls the body and swaps back; the step, its swap and the body are one family (the body is the member the others are hosted by, so also with the function a pool is in), the main code makes one call for each sprite; a build that does not fit the banks is made again with the three calls one by one (poolStepsOff in buildRom). Checked: the 12 pool test ROMs (DPC+ and PXE) give the same frames as before (120 frames each); the three powerup Bricks run (400 frames, fire at 60, 100, 140) after drawscreen: mean 707 to 583 cycles, median 674 to 553, 90th percentile 1191 to 1098, maximum 2904 to 2715, passes over 2280 cycles 3 to 2. The screen frames of that run are the same as before up to frame 261, after that the passes and the screen frames are out of step (cut frames at other places); compared by the frame counter of the game instead (the position and height of every sprite at each pass, 248 passes, fire at 60, 100, 140, 180, 220, with random spawn values and with fixed ones) the two builds are identical. A comparison by pass number is off by one from the start (the first pass is counted differently), which showed as differences that are not. One more gosub level is used (main, step, body). Tried and left out: ending the step with a jump to the second exchange instead of a call (saves about 15 cycles by count): the identical game, but the same three powerup run took 651 cycles on average instead of 583, the banks were laid out differently. Reason found 2026-11-09: the bank packer sizes a subroutine by the length of its text, and "goto" is two characters shorter than "gosub" (4 characters in all for the two steps); that was enough to change which families the packer moved and where (460 symbols in other banks; the pool code in bank 4 instead of 2, 3 instead of 5). With the same text length ("goto" and one more space) the layout is the same as with gosub, and then the jump is better: mean 583 to 567 cycles, median 553 to 531, maximum 2715 to 2649. So the placement of the families is worth more than this jump (651 against 583 for 4 characters of text). DONE 2026-11-09 (committed a779359): the layout planner (utils/bank-packer.js, planLayoutFromMeasurement in hooks/rom.js) now knows which unit calls which (a call graph of the code text, made once for a build) and charges a call across banks by how often the less frequent of the two runs; a unit with a part the main loop can reach (the pool code, the per pass checks, what the events of the main loop call) counts as running every pass for the planner (the moves of single units and the order of candidates keep the old frequencies). Bricks three powerup run (jump ending): the pool code is in the bank of movePowerups with either ending (before, 651 cycles on average with the jump when the layout moved away, 583 with the call), mean 563 cycles with the jump ending that is now in use (median 533, 90th percentile 1057, maximum 2722; 707, 674, 1191 and 2904 without the step routine). Calls under a rare condition do not count as every pass (utils/call-scan.js reads the text of each event and subroutine: a call on a line "if ... then", between a jump on such a condition and its label, or after a "then return" on one is rare; a rare condition is an input (joy0/joy1, switches), collision, rand, or the label of a run once or every N frames block). A first try that took every condition as rare put the Bricks powerup code in another bank than movePowerups (mean 655 cycles): movePowerups sits inside the "gameState <> 6" block, which holds for the whole game, so a condition on a variable counts as holding. Three powerup run with this: mean 566, median 531, 90th percentile 1096, maximum 2723; movePowerups and the pool code in one bank. Limit: a condition on a variable that is seldom true (a boss health of 0) is still taken as holding; the text cannot tell it from a game state. Checked: tools/bank-packer-check.mjs (npm run check:packer: the cost the planner reports against the cost of the layout worked out again, a caller and a much used callee in one bank); the 12 pool test ROMs (DPC+ and PXE), Bricks DPC+, Feet DPC+ and the standard kernel fill test give the same frames as before (120 frames each); the Bricks standard kernel project does not build, with or without these changes (a message about title screen variables, not the layout). A failed build is made again without the pool and check families only when it failed for fit.
- Sprite pool: variables, hidden sprites and blocks (asked 2026-11-08, built, committed, the 2-sprite pooled Bricks plays the same as the original after it: 60 sampled frames of 1,500 pixel-identical). (1) Three powerups at once in Bricks (pool 2 to 4, dpcPlusSpriteMax 5): it first failed with 'Too many variables' (60 defined, 56 available). The display memory spill (generators/bbasic/spill.js) now also takes an asm block that starts with a label (the animation code of a sprite is 'playerNanimationsStart asm'), and the instructions bit, cmp, cpx, cpy, adc, sbc, and, ora, eor on a spilled name (the byte is read into temp5, the accumulator kept on the stack), and the branch range check counts the real size of each rewritten access. That frees the size and animnext bytes of the pool sprites; 3 sprites then fit the variables (58 available), but the ROM is about 340 bytes short (the spilled accesses make the exchange routines of the sprites, _poolswap and _poolanimswap, about three times longer: _poolswap_2_4 is 103 lines against 36 for 2_3). Still failing: the frame of a pool sprite is used in the createPowerup function (a bB function cannot have spilled variables), the flag bytes fail the scratch test next to lines that use temp6, and pfboxx fails the branch range. Levers for the last 340 bytes: a spill-aware exchange (lda A / ldx B / sta B / stx A with a spilled name through the data fetchers DF0 to DF3, as poolPointerLines does), the rainbow code in asm (queued above), the spawn tests of a pool outside a function. No packing of the sprite bytes was found: the flag bits of the pool sprites are already packed by flag-pool.js (4 bytes in Bricks), and the frame, size, animnext, animation and color of a sprite are 8 bits each in use. (2) Hidden sprite at x 0, y 0: a block checkbox, not a global option: 'also set x and y to 0 when hidden' on Player set (Visibility), and 'set x and y to 0' on Release the pool sprite / Release all sprites in pool (all off by default). Found by the 3-sprite test: under DPC+ a hidden sprite left on the rows of a shown one makes the shown one drop out on every other frame (the same with three hand-made sprites). (3) New block 'Sprite pool: players x to y has a free sprite' (a value for an If block; the Sprites category of the toolbox, DPC+ and PXE only). (4) 'All pool sprites' in the dropdowns only when the project has a pool block ('any pool sprite' is still always offered). (5) The 3-sprite pool in a small project equals three plain sprites over 400 frames (spawn into the first free one, move, release). UPDATE 2026-11-08 (later): three powerups at once in Bricks now builds (pool 2 to 4, dpcPlusSpriteMax 5, a test copy that spawns every 16 frames) and shows up to three at once; the 2-sprite pooled Bricks and the original still match the earlier frames (60 sampled frames of 1,500). What got it to fit (sizes from the first compile, all units in bank 2; the lower the better): (a) the rainbow restore routine in asm (utils/dpc-rainbow-restore.js, a pass over the compiled assembly, DPC+ only: one copy of the 14 instructions that load a background's color rows, with a table of the row addresses indexed by the number of the background; 322 bytes became about 90 in Bricks, 153 bytes in the first compile) and (b) an exchange of two sprites' bytes with a spilled name through the data fetchers DF0 to DF3 (spill.js exchangeLines: 192 bytes). Tried and taken out: the free-sprite tests of a pool in a custom function moved to a subroutine (so the frame bytes could spill): 359 bytes bigger, not smaller (the spill then chooses by the fewest uses and picks names that cost more per use), so the tests stay in the function. Not done in the rainbow: the per-frame rainbow code (the player 1 color, the background pfscroll and the offset, about 220 bytes in Bricks) in asm; the Stop routine's test of three bits of flagPool0 (spilled forms are not matched by the pass: it leaves the routine as it was). The per-frame rainbow code on DPC+ is assembly now (generators/bbasic/sprites.js: the flag byte is read once, the offset loaded once, the scroll is the coprocessor call the compiler makes for 'pfscroll temp2 4 4', the last offset is written only when it moved; the loading of the 255 rows stays statements because it needs the compiler's labels for the rows). Frames equal the old code (200 of 200, a test with Player 1 and 2 and the playfield rainbow); Bricks 0-0-20 and the pooled one still equal the earlier frames (60 of 1,500 sampled). The gain is small (about 10 bytes unspilled, more where the flag byte is in the display memory): the statements it replaces compiled to nearly the same assembly. The big rainbow saving was the restore routine. Rainbow in asm, all kernels (asked 2026-11-08, built, committed): the span-of-hues player rainbow on DPC+ and PXE (flag test and start row in asm: rainbowPhaseAsm in rainbow-range.js, the table stays a statement), the standard kernel's player and playfield rainbow (one asm block each, with the span when the stride is a power of two), and the DPC+ playfield scroll left as the statement 'pfscroll temp2 4 4' (the compiler of the PXE kernel makes another call than the DPC+ one). Superchip RAM needs nothing special: the variables of the app are plain zero-page names in the assembly there too (var12 and up are ordinary RAM; only the read/write pairs, reserveDevVarRW, are in the Superchip RAM, and they already come as r0NN/w0NN names). The first version of the standard kernel path wrongly kept statements for Superchip, and a second try mapped varNN to r0NN, which read the wrong RAM (the pointer never moved): both taken out. Checked: the pointers set on a Superchip 16k ROM equal the old code's phase by phase (read from the emulator's RAM). PXE legalization: the asm only uses instructions the PXE 6502 has. Checked old code against new: DPC+ with spans 120 of 120 frames equal; PXE 61 consecutive frames equal; standard 4k: the pointers set are equal (read from the emulator's RAM: the same (offset mod span) x stride from the table), the frames equal but for a 4-pixel sliver on one line on phase 0 frames, which comes from where the table sits (a read with an index that crosses a page takes one more cycle: a rainbow table that is not aligned to a page makes it; it is in the old code too, at other phases). Found and fixed (the standard 8k and 16k banked ROMs showed hues outside the span in the old code too): the data tables of a span of hues were put in bank 1, but the kernel reads them (through pfcolortable and the player color pointers) while the top bank is mapped, so it read whatever the top bank has at that address. They are now emitted in the top bank, like the playfield color scroll tables (rainbowTablesTopBank in generators/bbasic.js; in the relocated sections, or in the text minikernel's section when it is on). Checked on 8k (the playfield rows 0 to 4, Player 1 2 to 6, Player 0 3 to 7, as asked), on 16k Superchip, and on 8k with the text minikernel. Not rebuilt: 32k and 64k (same code), and a project that has both a span rainbow and relocated units in the top bank. Shrinking the rainbow tables (asked 2026-11-08, built): a player's table of a span of hues was at least 64 rows long (plus the span); it is now as long as the tallest sprite (rows of its pictures and the ROM noise heights), twice that under DPC+ and PXE where a row is two lines (the standard kernel indexes a sprite's color table with the row of the picture: std_kernel.asm, ldy player1y after dcp). In the 8k test it took the two player tables from 68 bytes to 12 each in the top bank (measured in the ROM), which has little room. DPC+ (120 frames) and PXE (61 frames) with spans equal the earlier frames; 8k standard shows the hues asked for. Not shrunk: the playfield table (125 bytes read from index 0 to 124 by the kernel's last-line read plus 4 per step of the start row: only about 5 bytes to gain, not worth the risk), the playfield color scroll tables (aligned to a page so that a read does not cross one: a page crossing adds a cycle). The documentation (randomterrain bB reference, bankswitching and data statements) says a bank cannot read data from another bank and the kernel is always in the last bank: a table the kernel reads cannot leave the top bank. Playfield color scroll tables (standard kernel, buildBackgroundColorScroll in bbasic.js): they were put after a fixed 'align 256', so the pointer (the table address less `before`, 80 to 84 bytes) was in the page before the table and every read of the kernel crossed a page (one cycle more: rows of the test showed pixels of the pattern merged or moved at their left edge), and up to 255 bytes could be lost in front of them. Now the assembler places each table by where it falls, the rule the compiler follows for its pfcolors tables: a table that would cross a page goes to the next page, and one that starts less than `before` bytes into its page is moved down to that place (zero bytes only for the difference). Checked on a standard 8k test (pointer high and low bytes read from RAM: no borrow, the pattern clean), 16k Superchip with pfres 24 and 8k without blank lines. In the 8k test the zero bytes in front of the table went from 50 or more to 3. Still lost: up to `before` bytes (or a page, when the table would cross) when the table does not fall in the right place; filling that gap with other top-bank data (the rainbow tables) is not done. Also seen while reading the kernel: the kernel starts reading the playfield rainbow table at index 132 - 4 x pfres (84 for 12 rows), so the first 84 bytes of that table are never read: pointing the pointer 84 bytes below a shorter table would save about 80 bytes of the top bank (not done). Packing the scroll tables without page crossings (asked 2026-11-08): the placement rule leaves only a table that starts in [before, 256 - length] of a page; with before 80 to 84 and a table of 108 to 204 bytes two tables never fit one page (80 + 2 x 108 > 256), so the packing that can be done is in the length: a table is now only as long as the tallest background needs (the row offset of the scroll goes up to the rows a background cycles through, a page being 16 of them at most): 124 bytes became 108 for a 12-row background (the window for its start is 80 to 148 instead of 80 to 132), the frames equal over 130 frames covering every row offset (8k, 8k without blank lines, 16k Superchip with 24 rows). A smaller page (4 or 6 rows) would put two tables in a page, but it needs more pages per background and the routine's shifts (lsr x4, and #15) change: not done. Only one table is needed for up to 4 pages (the pages of 4 backgrounds are woven into it), so most projects have one table and a gap in front of it of at most `before` bytes, or a page when the table would cross; filling that gap with other top-bank data is still open. Rainbow tables in the scroll tables' gaps (asked 2026-11-08, built): placeKernelTables (utils/bank-tables.js) emits the playfield color scroll tables and the tables of a span of hues as one block of assembly, placed by the assembler from where each falls (DASM if on (<*), a flag rbfill<N> SET each pass for each rainbow table): a scroll table that would cross a page goes to the next one and a rainbow table that fits the rest of the page is put there; the bytes up to `before` in front of a scroll table are filled with rainbow tables that fit; what is left is placed after, each in one page (the rainbow tables had no rule against crossing a page before, which is a cycle more on every read, the cause of the 4-pixel sliver seen earlier on 4k). Checked: 8k with a scroll and two player rainbows (the two 12-byte tables landed in the end of the page the scroll table skipped), 8k and 4k rainbow only (hues 0 to 4, 2 to 6, 3 to 7), 8k with the text minikernel, 16k Superchip with a 24-row scroll (130 frames equal to before), 16k Superchip rainbow. Left over: in the 8k scroll test 106 zero bytes remain (26 at the end of a page and 80 in front of the table) because there was nothing else to put there; the playfield rainbow table (147 bytes) does not fit a gap of 80 (it needs a page to itself), and the first 84 bytes of it are not read (see above). Playfield rainbow table trimmed (standard kernel, asked 2026-11-08, built): the kernel reads a row's color at index 132 - 4 x (rows of the playfield data) and up (a row less with blank lines), so the first 76 to 84 bytes of the 147-byte table were never read (shown by overwriting them in a ROM: 0 of 60 frames changed, against 35 and 36 for the next ranges). The table now holds only the bytes from `skip` on (range.skip in rainbow-range.js, rows 126 - skip) and the pointer is aimed `skip` bytes below it (rainbowTableAddress); like the scroll tables it has to start at least `skip` bytes into a page (placeKernelTables: minOffset). 147 bytes became about 65 on 4k with 12 rows. Frames equal the build before over 57 frames on 4k, 8k and 8k with the text minikernel; 16k Superchip with 12 and 24 rows show hues 0 to 4 only. Not built: Superchip with 32 rows (skip 0, the table is as long as before), 8k Superchip. Easy code-size round (asked 2026-11-08, built, DPC+): the score color table (8 bytes at scoredata) was written with eight unrolled 'sta DF0WRITE' (24 bytes of code at each of the Score color and Flash Score color sites); it is now a loop of 8 bytes (ldx #8, sta, dex, bne; score.js SCORE_COLOR_FILL), 16 bytes less at each site (4 sites in the test ROM). Checked: a DPC+ project with Score color and Flash Score color (digits, 3 flashes of 20 frames, with the Score tab line colors different and equal) shows the same frames as the build before (20 sampled frames equal, the digits alternating flash color and the set color). The keypad blocks were already one shared subroutine (nothing to gain). The fade color ladder (colorSectionFor) is not easy: each color is a table of identical bytes in the ROM that the color pointer is aimed at, so the saving needs the color rows written into display memory at run time (space for it per sprite has to be found), not just a smaller test list; left as a medium item. Medium code-size round (asked 2026-11-08, built): (1) Fade engines: with two or more fade registers (score, text, background, playfield, players) the fade step is one subroutine (_fadestep in background.js: in A color, X target, Y timer; out A new color, X 0 waiting / 1 step / 2 landed) and each register keeps its active test, timer and stores: 254 bytes less with score + background + playfield fades on standard and DPC+, 208 on PXE; a project with one fade is unchanged. Frames equal to the build before on standard (51), DPC+ (51) and PXE (71, with the fade finished watcher). Found on PXE: it rewrites CPX so that only the carry of the compare survives (a BEQ on the first CPX read the wrong flag, the background fade stopped after one step); the code now compares twice. Not tested: the DPC+ player fade (missile color step) and the text minikernel fade with the shared step. The title screen graphics fades still use a separate copy. (2) Fire and Accelerate tables: the y table of each pair (main, minor, sign, inertia) is the x table turned a quarter (y(i) = x(i - 4), checked for all four), so one 20-byte table serves both: 30 bytes less on standard, 24 on DPC+ (frames equal over 51 samples on both). The stretch tables (DPC+) are not symmetric and are as before; the bounce heading table (16 bytes for each Bounce) is not shared yet. (3) Standard kernel pfscroll: the include pf_scrolling.asm holds all four directions (about 400 bytes); each is now left out when the program has no pfscroll of that direction (flags pfscroll_noleft/_noright/_noup/_nodown set from the generated code by standardPfscrollFlags in bbasic.js; include and manifest updated, see UPSTREAM_CHANGES.md): 179 bytes less with Down only, 176 with Up and Left, 87 with Down 2x, Right and Up 2x (8k standard); frames equal over 31 samples of each, with the picture scrolling. DPC+ and PXE scroll with the coprocessor and are not affected. Bounce heading table (looked at 2026-11-08, not changed): the 16-byte (8 for 8 directions) table of a Bounce with Fire is made once for each object, inside that object's _bouncepix_<name> subroutine (buildPixelReflect in sprites.js), not for each Bounce block. A shared copy would save 16 bytes for each extra bounced object, but the subroutines are relocated to banks one by one and a table can only be read from the bank it is in, so one copy would be wrong whenever two of them land in different banks (the bank is only known after the build allocates the units). Left as it is. Checks (2026-11-08): (1) Rainbow tables, standard kernel, 8 test ROMs run in the emulator and the hue of every pixel mapped through the 6502.ts palette: Superchip 16k with 32 rows (skip 0), Superchip 8k, 32k, 64k, 8k without blank lines, two spans (Player 0 hues 1 to 3, Player 1 9 to 13, playfield 5 to 11: all seven playfield hues show over 47 frames), and the playfield color scroll with the rainbow on (hues 0 to 4, 2 to 6, 3 to 7 only, nothing outside the span); the tall background without a playfield rainbow only shows the sprites inside their spans (the rows are the colors of the background). One pixel group of hue 15 on the top rows of the 32 row test is the palette giving the same color to $1E and $FE. (2) Per-player copies, cost of a second player (8k standard): Platform 494 bytes (each player's jump speed, gravity and fall limit are constants in the code, so sharing needs them in bytes and a state exchange), Accelerate 171 bytes; not shared. The playfield box test is already one routine for all players. (3) 3 powerups in Bricks (pool 2 to 4): the forced-spawn test (a powerup every 16 frames) has frames that are cut in two by the sync (a short frame of about 3,800 cycles then a frame of 18,800): all of them are frames where a pool sprite steps its picture through the exchange (the two exchanges of _poolanimswap cost about 700 cycles, with the shared animation code and its bank changes a step is about 1,100 to 2,300 cycles), and the 6507 logic of Bricks is already about 3,500 cycles a frame, so one step is too much (4,000 cycles of logic is still fine). Small pool tests (plain 3 sprites, pool of 3, pool of 2) have none. Tried and taken out: one step a frame by frame parity (blank frames 72 to 67 of 300, so more than one step at a time is not the whole cause), a frame-counter byte for it (one more variable: 24 of 25 are used, the spill makes the ROM too big and the build fails), the exchange and the call in one relocatable subroutine (the layout no longer fits: the ROM has 620 bytes free in four banks). To make a pool of 3 play in Bricks the cost of a step has to drop by about 1,000 cycles (an exchange that moves the whole 13 byte block of a sprite through one pair of data fetchers, or a body that works on any sprite without the exchange) or the logic of the rest of the frame has to. Pool pointer retarget (asked 2026-11-08, built, DPC+ not PXE): the exchange of the sprites that share animation code (_poolanimswap) no longer swaps the picture and color pointers (playerpointers, 144 of its 353 cycles): the units of the first sprite (player<N>animation<i>, player<N>colortable<i>) write the slots of the sprite that runs them, moved on by the number of bytes in temp6 (utils/pool-pointer-retarget.js, a pass over the compiled assembly after the rainbow restore pass: clc / adc temp6 on the low byte and adc #0 on the high one at each pointer write, 7 cycles). The exchange of another sprite sets temp6 (2 for each place after the first), the shared code enters at _poolanim_player<N> (sets temp6 to 0, for the first sprite) or at _poolanim_player<N>_other (keeps it). temp6 is not used by the bank jumps (they use temp7) or the units between the exchange and the shared code. The pass throws when the entry label is there and no pointer write was found. Checked: pool of 3 and pool of 2 test projects equal the build before (251 sampled frames of 500 each); Bricks forced-spawn test ROM 158 bytes smaller (about 120 bytes of it the pointer code of two exchanges), a pool of 3 Bricks 0-0-22 build 26,096 bytes in use. The forced-spawn test still has cut frames (72 to 62 of 300): a step is still about 1,000 cycles (the state exchange about 209 a call, bank changes about 370, the shared code). Tried and left out: one pool sprite stepping a picture a frame by turns of the frame counter (62 to 55 cut frames, but the pictures change a frame later: 47 of 251 frames of the pool test differ in a 5 x 10 pixel box). Next steps if needed: exchange the two adjacent spilled bytes (animation and color) with one fetcher setup (about 40 cycles a call), the shared code and the exchange in one bank (saves the bank changes). Adjacent exchanges (asked 2026-11-08, built): spill.js exchangeRunLines: exchanges that follow one another in an exchange routine (lda A, ldx B, sta B, stx A) are put in the order of the display memory addresses, and where the next byte of both sides follows the one before the fetchers are not set again (they move on by one after each read and write): 108 bytes less in the forced-spawn Bricks test (26,142 to 26,034), pool of 3 and pool of 2 tests equal (251 sampled frames of 500 each). Measured what decides a cut frame (iter-dpc.js: cycles from commongamelogic to drawscreen for each pass of the Bricks test, against the frames cut): a pass up to about 2,500 cycles is fine, from about 2,800 the next frame is cut (2,750 to 3,000: 22 of 33 cut; 3,000 to 3,250: 21 of 24; above 3,250 all), a normal pass is 1,700 to 2,500 (median 2,107) and a pass with a picture step of a pool sprite is about 900 more, so every step goes over; the step has to cost 300 to 500 cycles or the rest of the pass has to drop by the same. Cut frames 72 (before the retarget), 62 (retarget), 61 (adjacent exchanges). Not done, with the reasons: (a) the shared code and the exchange in one bank: the animation units, the shared code and the exchange are about 900 bytes together and no bank has 100 bytes free (620 bytes are free in all, in four banks), so they cannot be put together in Bricks; (b) no exchange at all (the code working on any sprite through an index): the fields it uses (size, frame, animnext, animation, color, a flag bit) are in zero page for some sprites and in the display memory for others (and not at regular distances in zero page: frame is at $DA, $E5, $E6), a spilled byte read or written costs 25 to 30 cycles (about the same as an exchange of it), and the bytes of 3 sprites do not fit zero page (24 of 25 variables are used): the step would be about 50 to 100 cycles cheaper, not the 400 needed. What would help more: fewer cycles in the rest of the pass (the cross-bank calls of a normal pass are about 350 cycles, the ball's coprocessor call about 235), or one call for the color and the animation unit of a step (2 bank changes, about 90 cycles). Profile of the pass of Bricks 0-0-22 (3 pool powerups, gameplay frames 300 to 1500, 540 passes of commongamelogic to drawscreen; the profiler is /c/tmp/pxe/prof2-dpc.js with symbols and assembly from the build): 2,195 cycles a pass. Biggest costs: (1) the hidden sprites load their blank picture again every pass (a sprite with frame 255 runs "size{7} = 0" and a cross-bank goto to its default unit, which writes the pointers and jumps back): measured by building without it, 2,195 to 1,782 cycles, 413 a pass (19%), for 4 sprites (Player 0 and the three powerups) about 100 each: the goto 15, the unit 24, the way back 15 and two bank changes 45 each. (2) Spilled variables: each access sets a data fetcher pointer (about 19 cycles); at least 336 cycles a pass in the pointer lines alone, the bytes at BKCOLS + 242, 251, 237, 244 (the ball's fire state, run every pass) the most; the spill chooses by how many places use a name, not how often they run. (3) The coprocessor call that moves the ball (function 101): about 235 cycles a pass, mostly waiting for the coprocessor, plus about 45 around it. (4) Bank changes: about 346 a pass in all (about 210 of them the hidden sprites above; the rest the calls of the fire, box test and platform checks in other banks). (5) The tests of each sprite's animation (animationsStart / HeldCheck and the NUSIZ lines that read the size bytes): about 250. (6) Run once bookkeeping 77, box pairs about 130. Plans for what was asked: (A) skip the blank picture of a hidden sprite once it is loaded: a bit of the size byte (4; 5 is the finished bit, 7 the loaded bit, 6 paused) set when the blank is loaded and cleared by whatever shows the sprite again (the visibility setters and the pool Release/Claim, which already clear bit 7): about 400 cycles a pass, 4 bytes in the main bank for each sprite. (B) Shared code and exchange in one bank (item 2): they are the units 408 bytes (animation 0 150, animation 2 208, two color tables 25 each), the shared body 115 and the two exchange routines (about 150 each, smaller after the retarget and the adjacent exchanges): about 820 bytes, and 886 are free in all after those changes, so a bank has to be cleared for it: make the group a family (as a custom function and what it calls: computeFunctionFamilies in rom.js moves them together), take the shared body off the pinned list, give wrapRelocatableGraphics the bank of the family instead of the primary one for the units of the group, and one routine in the family that does exchange, body, exchange, called from the main bank (2 bank changes in place of 8): about 276 cycles a step. (C) No exchange (item 3), the way that works: not arrays (the fields are in zero page for some sprites and in the display memory for the others, a spilled access costs about the same as an exchange) but the units made without state names: they read the frame and the flags from temp bytes and leave the new animnext, height, loaded flag and frame in others, and a short routine for each sprite (about 50 bytes, in the family) loads the state of its sprite into them, calls the shared code with the pointer offset in temp6, and stores the results: about 100 to 150 cycles a step less than the exchange and about 300 bytes of ROM. The three together bring a step from about 900 to about 500 cycles. Cheapest order: A (413 a pass), then B, then C; and a spill chooser that counts the places that run every pass about ten times (the fire and animation code) would move about 150 to 300 cycles out of the pointer lines. Do 1, 2, 3 (asked 2026-11-08): (A) hidden sprites load the blank picture once (generateAnimations, hiddenBlankLines): the blank has no height and every picture of an animation has at least one row, so a hidden player whose height is already 0 has it loaded and its handler jumps to the end of its animation code; otherwise it clears bit 7 (so the animation's picture loads when the player is shown) and loads the blank. DPC+ and PXE, for the players and for the partners of wide players (same height test: the partner takes the height of its player while it is shown). The standard kernel hides a player with a flag, never with frame 255, so its handler is never entered and has nothing to save. A first version marked 'blank loaded' with bit 4 of the size byte: that is the 'loop disabled' bit of the Set animation block, so it would have cleared the loop setting of an animation at each load (found before it left the tree; the pool tests did not use loop disabled); the height needs no bit. Bricks 0-0-22 pass 2,195 to 1,801 cycles (540 gameplay passes); pool of 3 and 2 tests equal (251 of 500 frames each); hide/show test (a player hidden and shown every 32 frames) equal on DPC+ (134 frames) and PXE (101 frames); three wide-sprite DPC+ tests equal (51 frames each). (B) The shared animation code, its pictures and colors, and the exchange and step of each other sprite are one family now: the units are made inline in the body (Blockly.BBasic.inlineGraphicsUnits makes wrapRelocatableGraphics return the unit in place and not record it), the body is a wrapper node of the families of rom.js (functionCallWrapperNames) with the exchanges and steps hosted by it, and computeFunctionFamilies joins a hosted member that is a wrapper too (it skipped those before, so the members were moved one by one); a wrapper is no longer a standalone relocation candidate. The main bank makes one call for a step (_poolanimstep_<first>_<name>: exchange, body, exchange, plain gosubs inside) and one for the first sprite (bank tag from getSubroutineBank). Built in Bricks all of it landed in one bank; pool tests equal; ROM 216 bytes smaller than before it (26,158 to 25,942 in the forced-spawn test). A first version put the members in different banks (a plain gosub across banks crashed the coprocessor emulation: thumbulator invalid instruction) because of the union bug above. (C) No exchange: not done. What is left of a step of about 950 cycles (forced-spawn Bricks, a pass up to the point of the cut frame against the passes without a step: 1,846 to 2,799): the exchange about 340 (the spilled bytes cost most), the animation code and units about 330 (Sel 54, Load 75, Start 29, Found 24, height, the tests), the sprite's tests in the main bank about 150, bank change about 70. The way to drop the exchange is to leave the fields the code only reads (animation, color, a flag bit) where they are and read them at the address of the sprite that runs it, and to index the others (animnext and height are consecutive zero page bytes already); it needs the allocator to give the pool sprites the same layout for each field (frame is at $DA, $E5, $E6 and size is in zero page for one sprite and in the display memory for the others now) and the spill to place a field at a fixed distance for each sprite (13 now by chance); about 200 of the 340 cycles. Also tried again, now the steps are cheaper: one pool sprite stepping a picture a frame (turns of the frame counter): cut frames 62 to 58 of 300, so a single step still goes over in the forced-spawn test; the cut frames are not caused by steps that coincide. The pass without a step is 1,846 cycles at the median now (2,107 before A and the retarget), the limit about 2,750. (C) No exchange (asked 2026-11-08, built): the shared animation code of a pool (generateAnimations, processAnimations, POOL_REFS) gets the state of the sprite in temp bytes instead of by exchange: temp4 the size byte, temp5 the frame, temp3 the frame where it runs next (in and out), temp2 the color, temp1 the color override flag (masked), temp6 the pointer and height offset. The code is the units of the animations (each called by a routine of the sprite, one for each picture) and the color tables (one subroutine called first); each sprite of the group, the first too, has a routine (_poolanimstep_<first>_<name>) that loads its size, frame, next frame, color and flag, calls the color tables, picks the unit by its animation number (one read, compares in a chain), and stores the size, frame and next frame back; the height is written to the array of the heights at half of temp6 (asm in the units written as tables, the retarget pass for the store the compiler makes after a picture written as statements). A name the code still uses that is not one of those (the flags that say an animation is finished, the byte that sets the width) is exchanged with the first sprite around the call as before (_poolanimswap, only when there is one; the names of the heights are left out of what it looks for). Bricks 0-0-22: no exchange routine left, family in one bank, pass with a step 2,790 to 2,509 cycles at the median in the forced-spawn test, ROM about 200 bytes bigger than with the exchange (the routines of the sprites read and write bytes in the display memory), cut frames still 61 of 300. Checked equal to the version with the exchange: pool of 3 and 2 (251 of 500 frames each), pool2, pool3b, pool4, pool4b (126 frames each, mostly still pictures), pool6 with the sprites at different heights; with two spawned sprites at the same y (pool6) one of the two is not drawn in both versions, and which one differs (the DPC+ sprite sort): not looked at further. Tested after (2026-11-08): a pool of 2 with non-looping animations (Bonus, Strike of Bricks) and a 'When animation has finished' block for each sprite (the flags that say it is finished are exchanged with the first sprite around the call, the fallback): 81 sampled frames of 400 are identical to the same two sprites without a pool, and the score changes when each one finishes; the version before the temp bytes (the exchange with the pointers retargeted) was wrong there (79 of 81 frames differed from the plain sprites: the pictures were not drawn), so the temp bytes also fixed that; a pool of 2 with a wide animation (the pool sprites have partners, so they do not share the code): identical to the same sprites without a pool (81 frames) and to the version before. A set width in a pool (Set width/quantity: double-size for the first sprite, 2 close-spaced copies for the second, both with looping animations): 81 sampled frames of 400 identical to the same sprites without a pool; the build makes the exchange routine _poolanimswap_player2_player3 for the two sizeset bytes (the only names it exchanges), so the fallback was run. Nothing known is left untested for the pool animation code. The cut frames are not decided by the pass alone: with three sprites on the screen passes of 2,000 to 2,500 cycles are already cut about half of the time (the display setup takes more of the frame), so the limit seen before (2,750) holds for fewer sprites only.
- Rainbow code size and cycles (asked 2026-11-08, DONE 2026-11-08, committed: the per-frame code is asm on all kernels, the tables are shorter, placed together and tested; see the sprite pool entry, which holds the details; the ideas below were the plan): make the Rainbow hue span code smaller and faster, in asm where that helps (now: on DPC+ and PXE a color table of about the tallest sprite plus the span is declared again every frame the rainbow runs, about 70 to 80 cycles and 70 or more bytes for each rainbow player; the phase of the start row is a chain of compare and subtract lines; the standard kernel playfield table is 128 + 4 x span bytes). Ideas: declare the table once (a data statement the pointer is set from, not the declaration statement again), one shared table for the players with the same span, the phase from a small table or a running counter, the 16-bit pointer add in one short asm routine.

(9) DONE 2026-11-08, committed af84737: the Scene tab trigger list offers every sprite the project has, each of the players 10 to 16 of PXE with a checkbox. The limit was the record format that was chosen for the scenes: a trigger is a row of 8 values and its mask had room for 13 bits (the 8 of the seventh value and bits 1 to 5 of the eighth: players 0 to 9, the missiles, the ball). Now bits 13 to 19 (players 10 to 16) are kept in a second row for each trigger after the offset areas, [mask >> 13, 0 x 7], and header value 6 is 1 when those rows are there (so scenes made before read as they did; SCENE_TRIGGER_OBJECT_LIMIT, SCENE_TRIGGER_EXTRA_BIT, triggerObjectsOf, triggerSpritesOf, sceneHasTriggerExtra in utils/scene.js; the notes and formats of the Data tab cells follow). - Score bars, the top line of the left bar (found and fixed 2026-11-09, committed bd6a92c, tested in the emulator): (1) DPC+ with the bars in the colors of the score lines (config dpcPlusScoreBarColors): the top line started at pixel 24 instead of 16 (the first two pixels missing); in that mode the color write of the loop takes 3 cycles in front of the write of the left bar, which on the first line has no write at the end of a line before it (the loop writes the bar for the next line at the end of each line); traced with the writes of the TIA (C:/tmp/bbx/pftrace.cjs, the writes with the color clock). (2) The standard kernel: the top line of the left bar was missing altogether at health 8 (the right bar was there), also with the default settings, with the Text Minikernel it was fine. Both are fixed the same way: the value of the left bar is put in PF1 at the end of the line before the first one (not before the right half of that line is drawn, so nothing shows in it), in the time made by doing setup before the WSYNC (the time before it is free: 22 cycles in the standard kernel, about 26 in DPC+) and, in the standard kernel, the NUSIZ, VDELP1 and HMP1 setup before the RESP strobes. The RESP strobes and the HMOVE are at the same cycle as before (checked with the trace: 126, 135 and 219 in DPC+, 129, 138 and 219 in the standard kernel); only with pfscore (builds without score bars have the same code: a standard ROM without bars is byte for byte the same). Checked: DPC+ bar pixels 16 to 47 on all 8 lines with the line colors and with the Text Minikernel, 5 digits, health 7 gives 20 to 47, flat bars and another font the same frames as before (120 frames), standard health 8 and 7, 5 digits and the Text Minikernel, Bricks DPC+ frames the same (150). Files: DPCplus_kernel.asm, std_kernel.asm, std_kernel_vertical_reflect.asm and the manifest. Other things tested: DPC+ line colors on the digits (a gradient), another score font, PXE bars (complete).
- Cycle-exact loops of the DPC+ kernel (looked at 2026-11-09, fix committed bd6a92c): all the branches of the kernel code in a built ROM were listed with the number of the 256 shifts of the code that would make each cross a page (a taken branch that crosses a page takes one cycle more). In the main kernel loop (loop, kernel1 to kernel11) the only branches are "beq repo", which is already guarded (STA or STA.w by the pages of repo and norepo), and the exit; the score loop was fixed 2026-11-06. The one that was not guarded: the loop that sets the horizontal positions of the sprites (HorPosLoop and DivideLoop in kernel_setup: bcs DivideLoop and bpl HorPosLoop count cycles, a page crossing put every sprite 3 color clocks further right for each time round, and the sprites in different places). Shown: a build with 14 bytes left out in front of it (its loop across the page boundary) differs from the same build with the padding that keeps the loop in one page in 27 of 200 frames (a sprite at another X), and with the new guard it is frame for frame the same as the padded one. The guard (DPCplus_kernel.asm before kernel_setup, label HorPosLoopEnd after the loop, and includes-manifest.json): padding bytes, never run, move the loop to the start of the next page when it would cross one (up to 16 bytes of bank 1 where needed, none in the layouts checked: a Bricks DPC+ ROM is byte for byte the same with it). All 76 DPC+ symbol maps from earlier builds had the loop in one page (4 different layouts), so no build was affected so far. The text minikernels (text12a/b/DPCplus) have "align 256" in front of their loops and the title screen position routine (position48) has no branches, so those were left alone; Audit of the rest, 2026-11-09: (1) the standard kernel (std_kernel.asm; std_kernel_vertical_reflect.asm is the same code and was not built apart): it already has its guards (the position loop in std_overscan.asm is padded off a page end by "if ((<*)>$e9)&&((<*)<$fa)", the score loop and the last line of the picture have "align 256"), and its kernel starts at the first byte of a page whatever the project is (f100 with the Superchip RAM, f000 without: the RAM takes the first 256 bytes of the bank), and the options hardly change its size (the end of the kernel is at f26c or f26d in 11 builds: player colors, playfield colors, row heights, screen shake, pfres, without the Superchip), so the loops of the main kernel keep their place within a page. Branches that cross a page right now in the kernel of those 11 builds: none (the list of branches with the shifts that make them cross is made by C:/tmp/bbx/shiftset.cjs and branchscan3.cjs). A shift test (a jump over N bytes of padding in front of the kernel, 120 frames against the unshifted build) shows what it would take: the shifts 43, 213 and 249 give the same frames and the shifts 90, 128 and 171 garble the playfield and add lines (the branches continueP1+35, continueP0+3 and altkernel+13 of the draw loop cross at f200), so an option or a change that added more than about 60 bytes in front of the draw loop would break it, which nothing does now. Nothing was changed for this. (2) The collision minikernel (collision_undo.asm) runs in the vblank and has no timed writes: nothing to guard. (3) The PXE kernel: the 6502 side is "drawscreen: nop, rts" and the tables, so there is nothing to check there. The ARM side is checked with gopher2600, which times the ARM (6502.ts does not): the ELF cartridge of the vendored gopher2600 was given counters for the ARM cycles of each run of the ARM (a local patch, saved as C:/tmp/pxe-audit/elf-audit-counters.patch with the harness C:/tmp/pxe-audit/pxe-timing-main.go, built natively with "go build -mod=vendor"; reverted afterwards, nothing of it is in the repo), and each run is set against the 6507 time between it and the run before (58.8 ARM cycles for each 6507 cycle at 70 MHz). Results on PXE ROMs of 600 frames (past the splash): in the visible lines (40 to 221) the ARM runs about once for each 16 lines and uses 12% of the time it has (8,600 ARM cycles in 1,247 cycles of the 6507), none over half, with the stress project of 12 players on screen the same (the kernel cost does not depend on the number of sprites in these runs); in the vblank and overblank the ARM runs the game and the 6507 waits for it, and the ARM uses 8% to 11% of a frame on average and at most 11% to 22% (the 12 player project) of the 1.17 million ARM cycles of a frame. So on the ARM side the PXE builds have room by a wide margin. Not measured: the Bricks project on PXE (it did not build with the PXE option on), and ROMs with much more game code than these; the figures come from the cycle model of gopher2600, not from a board.
