- Sprite pool ids (asked 2026-11-09, DONE 2026-11-09, committed 553af63; the editor looked at in the browser pane for the blocks, a pool block with a Number block plugged in, the "all/any pool sprites" id input, the Bricks project): a pool is made by one "Sprite pool ID N: players A to B" block (sprite_pool_define, put anywhere, in System start in Bricks; the id is a number field, 1 to 4: at most 4 pools so the state kept for the pools can be bit packed, nothing is packed yet because the pools keep no state at all yet, a sprite is free while it is hidden), and spawn, for each, release all, is full and has a free sprite name only the pool id in an input that takes a Number block (a constant, the pools are made when the ROM is built: another block is left out with a warning on the block and in the compile log), as do the "all pool sprites" and "any pool sprite" choices of the blocks that take them (POOLID, with a shadow number). The id input of every pool block starts with a Number block (1) in it wherever the block comes from, not only from the toolbox (the extension sprite_pool_id_shadow in blocks/sprite-pool.js; checked in the browser pane on the 6 blocks, and a block loaded with id 3 keeps 3, one with a real Number block 2 keeps that). The build (expandSpritePools in utils/sprite-pool.js) puts the range of the id in the fields it worked with before, so the rest is unchanged; an id nothing defines leaves the blocks that name it out (a is full or has a free sprite block says false). A saved project with ranges is moved over when it is loaded (hooks/migrate-sprite-pool-ids.js, from main.js and Project.vue: each range used becomes an id and a define block put loose at the left). Pools work on the DPC+ and PXE kernels only: on the standard kernel the pool blocks are left out of the build with a note (the toolbox already did not show them) and a warning on each block. Checked: the pool test projects (pool4, pool6, pt_bounce_pool, pt_pool3, pt_seekarr_pool; pool5 does not build before or after) built from the old format give the same ROM before and after the change, byte for byte; new projects with two pools and the release player block build on DPC+, PXE and standard, the pool sprites fall on DPC+ and there are none on the standard ROM; a project with an id that is not a Number block, an id nothing defines and a release of a player that is not in the pool builds and says so.
- Title screen: importing a graphic resizes the canvas to the size of the imported graphic (asked 2026-11-09; chosen: the height may go above 64, the width stays the card's; DONE 2026-11-09, committed b904639, looked at in the browser pane (see the checks at the end): the image import of the toolbar, "Import card frames" and the Aseprite import set the height up to 255 (TITLE_GRAPHIC_MAX_HEIGHT in TitleScreenEditor.vue; PixelEditor.vue has a maxHeight prop, 64 by default), and the Set height slider of the title tab goes to 255 too so a taller graphic is not cut to 64 by it; 255 is the limit of the format: frame offsets and the window are bytes, a row color list is one page; checked 2026-11-09 in the browser pane: an image of 96x150 through Import card frames gives 150 rows of 48 and 150 row colors, a 48x200 image through the toolbar import gives a 200 row editor and the saved data; and in the emulator a DPC+ project that draws the AtariAge card of Bricks padded to 100 rows and to 255 rows with a window of 44 rows draws the same picture as the card of 44 rows, to the pixel; the Bricks project itself with its AtariAge card padded to 80 or 150 rows does not build, its title bank is full (4,597 of 3,956 bytes for 150 rows), which is the bank limit and not the row limit). Before the change: an imported image already sets the height of the frame (the image height, 1 to 64: handleImportImage in PixelEditor.vue, imageToCardFramePixels in TitleScreenEditor.vue) but the width is the card kernel's (48, or 96 on a 96x2 card) and the image is squeezed to it, and a taller image than 64 rows is squeezed to 64.
- Title screen graphic: skip rows so a graphic can scale up on the y axis (asked 2026-11-09, not started; a question of whether it is possible): a block would set how many rows the kernel skips (draw every row, every second row, ...), and the number goes down over time so the graphic grows. The 48x1 loop (pf48x1_X_loop in 48x1_X_kernel.asm) takes exactly 76 cycles a line, but it has 2 spare cycles (the "sleep 2") and a 6 cycle color reload (lda aux4, sta COLUPF) that does nothing when the background color is the same on every line, so "dey" can be 2 to 4 "dey" (the next row is saved in aux2) in loop copies chosen before the loop for each step size, with the start row a multiple of the step so the loop still ends on 0. The number of lines drawn would be the rows divided by the step (the window height block already sets the lines at run time), and what sits below the graphic would move up unless it is padded. All the kernels looked at 2026-11-09 (public/bb19/titlescreen; the cycles counted by hand from the source, nothing built): (1) 48x1 (one color for the graphic, one line a row): as above, a variable step ("tya, sec, sbc skip, sta aux2", 10 cycles, for the 5 of "dey, sty aux2") needs the 2 spare cycles and the color reload moved into the setup. (2) 48x2 (row colors, two lines a row, loop pf48x2_X_loop): both lines are exactly 76 cycles; the row is kept in aux2, Y is overwritten by the picture reads so "ldy aux2" is needed, and the color of the row is read with that Y (so the color list skips the same rows with no change); line A has 8 cycles for the step ("sleep 3" and "dec aux2"), line B has 4 spare ("sleep 4"). A fixed step of 2 fits ("ldy aux2" then "dey, dey, sty aux2" is 7 of the 8 cycles, one more from "sta.w missile0y"); a step of 3 or more and a step in a byte do not fit (10 or 11 cycles for 8). The loop ends on "bpl", so a step past row 0 ends it without trouble. (3) 48x2 with one row color (pf48x2_U_loop, "title_uniform_48x2"): 11 spare cycles in line A, so a step in a byte fits ("tya, sec, sbc skip, sta aux2", 10 cycles). (4) 48x2 shared setup (48x2_S) only changes the setup, nothing in the loop. (5) 96x2 (row color list, one line a row, the two flicker loops of every card, absolute addressing, the end is "cpy temp1, bne"): about 26 cycles of "sleep" fillers a line (the TIA writes around them are fixed, the fillers can be other code that does not touch the TIA), so a step in a byte fits, but the color of the next line is read at "colors-1,y" (it has to be "colors minus the step"), and the end test has to be "bcs" (not equal) or a step can jump over it; every card has a copy of the loops (8 cards, 2 loops), so a skip adds code to each. (6) the pointer setup of every card and the "win" byte (window height at run time) already exist, so the number of lines a step leaves is the window height block. PXE has no title kernel. A way with no kernel change: the build makes the squashed copies of the graphic as frames (ROM cost: a picture for each).
- Bounce with a random spread did not build when nothing else in the program used division (found 2026-11-09, fixed, committed 553af63): the spread turns the heading by "rand / 52" (and 86, 37, 29 for the other steps), which is a real division, and the routine for it (div8) was only asked for when the steps were worked out while the game runs, so a project with an edge bounce and a spread with a number of steps failed with "Unknown Mnemonic 'lda #>(div8-1)'". spreadTurnLines in generators/bbasic/sprites.js now asks for it.

What it was: the Player card of the title screen offers a Width of 1x, 2x or 4x only (TitleScreenEditor.vue TITLE_PLAYER_WIDTH_OPTIONS, saved as player0Width/player1Width = 2 or 4, written as NUSIZ 5 or 7 by titlePlayerStartLines in generators/bbasic/titlescreen.js). Bit 3 of the byte is the flip bit and bits 4 to 6 are flags of the loop, so the start line keeps those bits as it does now.

- Title scrolling graphic: window height at runtime (asked 2026-11-09, asked to be built the same day; DONE 2026-11-09, committed bd6a92c, tested in the emulator on DPC+ and the standard kernel): a block "Set title screen graphic [card] window height to [N] rows" (titlescreen_window_set, Title blocks; the dropdown lists the graphics with a window smaller than the picture). N is 1 to the height of one frame; the window keeps its top row (the scroll position moves up if the window would run past the bottom). A graphic that has the block (the block is in an event or routine and is enabled) gets: a byte for the number of lines (titleCardWindowVarName, started with the window of the Title tab by titleScreenStartLines: lines less one for a 48 wide graphic, the lines for a 96x2 one, windowVarValue), the card has "bmp_KEY_win" (the 24 kernel copies 48x1_N, 48x2_N and 96x2_N read the number of lines from it with ifconst, else as before), its pointer tables are made for a window of one line (bmp_KEY_window = 1 for 48 wide) and its index is worked out every pass as the scroll position plus the window byte (a graphic that plays: frame offset plus both; a still one: generateTitleScreenAnimationChecks writes it), so a graphic with the block also has a separate scroll byte (the scroll blocks write to it, Scroll by with stop at edge works the end out from the window byte). It does not use the shared setup of the 48x2 graphics. Checked: the same title page (48x2 with 40 rows and a window of 20, 48x1 with 30 and 10, 96x2 with 24 and 12) with and without blocks that never run is frame for frame the same on DPC+ (120 frames) and standard (frame 60), an animated 48x2 with the block the same as without it (150 frames); with the blocks run the windows are 7, 4 and 6 rows and the graphics below move up; 30 rows (more than the window of the Title tab) with the start scroll 6; the start scroll 6, 5 and 4 with the block present; Scroll by down with stop at edge after a window of 12 stops at the bottom of the picture. PXE: nothing to test, the PXE kernel has no Title Screen kernel and every title screen block makes no code there (titlescreen.js wraps them), checked 2026-11-09: the same project with PXE on builds with no title graphics code and no window variables. Also checked 2026-11-09 on DPC+: a second page (the same frames with the blocks that never run, 100 frames) and a graphic whose frames keep the picture under different row colors (colorSplit; the same, 150 frames). A graphic whose colors are faded with the multicolor fade (row fade plans) cannot have it: the kernel reads its row colors by the offset of the fade alone, so the block does nothing there and the build logs a warning (the build with the block and without it is the same, 150 frames). The extra cost is a byte of memory and about 6 bytes of code for each graphic with the block, and 1 cycle more in the setup of the graphic (not seen on the screen). What it was: not possible now, the window is a number of the build. In the kernel files (48x1, 48x2, 96x2, and the shared setup 48x2_S) it is used as ldy #(window-1) for the number of lines drawn and in the six row pointers, which are ROM addresses of the form bmp+height-window that the scroll position (bmp_KEY_index) is taken off, and for the color rows the same. A block that sets it would need: a RAM byte for each scrolling graphic (the line count read from it), the pointer base made for the full height and the index made to include height minus window (the 16 bit subtraction the setup does would then add), about 10 to 20 bytes of setup code for each copy of a kernel and a few cycles in a setup that already makes graphics start a line low; the lines the page takes change as the window does (the picture is cut at about 200 lines, the frame length comes from the timer). Possible, a few days of work and a check of every kernel copy, with a limit of the full height of the picture.

- Bricks on PXE (asked 2026-11-09; builds, uncommitted, runs in the emulator): the Bricks DPC+ project with the PXE option on failed with "extraneous end statement found" (line of "function _dataElementDispatch"): the PXE compiler (batari Basic v1.9-PXE-beta-46) has no "function ... end", and the Data blocks use one for a table id given at run time. In generators/bbasic/data.js the three dispatches (element, bit, size) are plain subroutines on PXE (usesPxe): the same chain of tests, the table read into the result (pxeElementLines; the tables are read from the primary bank, the one their copy is emitted in, which is not bank 1 on this kernel: the first try read "_dt_1_BG1" while only "_dt_1_BG1_b2" was there, "Unknown Mnemonic"). Checked: Bricks for PXE builds (105,406 bytes) and runs 1,100 frames in 6502.ts (the playfield, the score, the score bars after fire; PXE has no Title Screen kernel or Text Minikernel, so no title page or text) and why Bricks stays at the stage selection (looked into 2026-11-09; it is not PXE, the DPC+ ROM does the same): in the saved project file the events gameplay_start and gameplay_update are disabled blocks, and so is the "Change state to gameplay" block of the fire tap check in Title screen update; with only that block enabled the jump into gameplay_start_begin is made (traced in the emulator) but the event is a 14-byte stub, so the screen does not change. With the two events enabled as well (copies, saved as C:/tmp/vp/bricks_dpc_go2.json and bricks_pxe_go2.json) the DPC+ and the PXE ROMs build (PXE 105,406 bytes) and show the game after the second fire tap: the paddle, the ball and the lives bars (the PXE frame has a taller red bar under the playfield and the right score bar, a difference not looked into). The ball then stays on the paddle: gameState is 0 after the jump, and the launch code is under "if gameState = 5", which nothing sets in this project, so the game logic of the project, not a kernel, needs a block that sets 5. The standard kernel: the Bricks project does not build there at all (Too many variables without Superchip RAM; with it at 8k bank 1 over by 8,236 bytes; at 32k the title screen bank estimate (3,855 of 3,956 bytes) fails), so Bricks was not run on standard); the DPC+ Bricks is the same as before (200 frames); the ARM time of the Bricks PXE ROM in gopher2600 (see the audit above): 8.7% of a frame on average, at most 12.3%, 12% of the time between runs in the visible lines. Still true: other Function blocks do not work on PXE either (the same error), not worked on; the bit and size dispatches were written the same way but not built.
- Harmony cart fix option:
 needs a test on real hardware or a Harmony emulator with the option off.
- Restart ROM / fewer variables in Bricks: the restart works (checked in the headless harness); the rest of the variable savings are listed in the details below ("Not done").
- Grouping display memory reads (asked 2026-11-06, done 2026-11-07, committed 1b42693): a pass over the finished code (mergeAdjacentReads in spill.js, called from workspaceToCodeAllPasses) leaves out the four lines that set the data fetcher pointer when a read of the display memory comes right after a read of the byte before it (12 cycles and 10 bytes each; reading moves the pointer one byte on). It merges 0 reads in Bricks (the coprocessor moves the ball) and 2 with Enable coprocessor off. Not done: giving variables that are read together neighbouring bytes (only the dev variables made together are). It first looked as if the pass crashed a build; the cause was an old bug, found 2026-11-07: the checks of the main loop that are subroutines (_platformer_checks, _fire_checks, _seek_checks, _lock_checks, _inertia_checks, _sprite_box_checks) were made with the call tags of bank 1, so when a build over capacity moved one of them to another bank, its call to _pfbox_player1 (bank 1) was a plain gosub across banks and the 6502 ran data (invalid instruction at the first paddle move, Bricks with Enable coprocessor off). Fixed in generators/bbasic.js (inSubroutineContext: the code of those subroutines is made as in the subroutine, so the tags are for its bank); checked on the ROMs that crashed (700 frames in the 6502.ts harness and with the paddle held in the app, both emulators). The calls in other code that moves, from a block made outside its subroutine, were not looked at one by one.
- Ideas, not planned: skip the work of off-screen sprites (only pays when more than about 60% of the sprites are off screen); use the DPC+ stack (fetcher 7) for variables (no room gained, see the details).

- Title screen: single color 48x2 and 96x2 graphic types to save ROM (asked 2026-11-10; DONE 2026-11-10, uncommitted, tested in the emulator on standard and DPC+, the editor not looked at by the user): a graphic of two-line pixels with one color, like 48x1, stored with one color byte instead of one for each row. Found: the 48x2 kernel already has a loop that does not read row colors (pf48x2_U_loop in 48x2_X_kernel.asm, used today only when a title screen is too big for its bank); the 96x2 kernel reads the row color with "lda bmp_96x2_N_colors-1,y", which a single color copy can read as one byte at the same cycles. With the color in a RAM byte, "Set title screen graphic color" and the fade could work on these types too. Built: a card setting singleColor on a 48x2 or 96x2 card (titleCardTypeInfo and isSingleColorTitleCard in blocks/titlescreen.js), added from the Add graphic menu (the 48 sizes together, then the 96 sizes, asked 2026-11-10); the graphic has one color like 48x1 and stores one color byte (bmp_<key>_color, bmp_<key>_onecolor in the kernel copies: 48x2 takes the loop without row colors, 96x2 reads the byte with lda.w at the same cycles). "Set title screen graphic color" and the fade work on it (a RAM byte). The single color graphics share the eight kernel copies of their size with the ones that have row colors. Found and fixed on the way: the 48x2 flag that picks the loop without row colors was kept in temp6, which on the standard kernel is scorepointers+11 (the picture pointer of the sixth column), so with that loop in the ROM the right sixth of every 48x2 graphic was garbage (this was so already for a compact title screen, one too big for its bank); the flag is in stack2 now. Also fixed (reported 2026-11-10, "single color canvas preview is not showing correct single color"): the Title tab canvas kept the color it was made with when the color of a single color graphic was changed (48x1 too): PixelEditor.vue makes its drawing tools again and redraws when fgColor changes. Checked: Title Screen Demo (standard) with a single color 48x2 (animated, 6 frames) and 96x2 next to a 48x2 with row colors that matches the build without them; Bricks (DPC+) with its logo single color.
- Title screen: Player sprites card not limited to one title screen, but one of each per title screen (asked 2026-11-10, "do player sprites per title screen"; DONE 2026-11-10, uncommitted, tested in the emulator on DPC+ and standard, the editor not looked at by the user): each page can have one Player sprites card (the Add graphic menu says "(one on each page)" once a page has one). The kernel (player_kernel.asm) has the positioning and one drawing loop for each number of lines a row that a card uses (draw_player_go_k1, draw_player_go_k2, a macro for the start); each page's card has its data (bmp_player0_<page>...) and a routine made by the generator (draw_player_display_<page>, buildPlayerDataAsm) that sets the line count, heights and pointers and jumps (not jsr: one more return address on the stack was overwritten by TSpositionp0p1's "sta stack1,X", the ROM reset) to the loop. Its numbers are set before the sprites are placed, so one more WSYNC keeps the first line where it was: a Bricks build with its one card is the same as before in every one of 700 frames. The frame counter and the frame offset of a player stay one byte each: a page's animations play while it is the one drawn, and "Set title screen player sprite frame" works on the card of the page that is drawn. With cards on several pages, a page drawn after another one puts its card's sprites at its Start X and Y (centered without them) and its Size (one copy when another page's card has a Size), and Title screen start sets the page number to one no page has, so the first page drawn does that too.
- Title page shift (found 2026-11-10 testing the Player card on each page; "fix title page shift" asked 2026-11-10; the options below were put to the user, who chose (3) leave it, 2026-11-10): a title screen too big for its bank (compact) has its 48x2 graphics use the shared setup (48x2_S_kernel.asm), 365 cycles from the card to position48 against 242 for the setup in the card's copy (measured on Bricks, C:/tmp/bbx/setupcycles.cjs), so every 48x2 graphic and everything under it on its page starts one or two lines lower; adding anything that makes the title screen compact (a Player card or a 48x1 graphic on page 1 of Bricks) moves page 2 down a line. Options: (1) every 48x2 setup takes a fixed number of lines whatever its kind (a WSYNC before it and its work split in lines): no shift ever again, but every 48x2 graphic of every project moves down once by 1 or 2 lines and a page uses those lines more; (2) a faster shared setup (the cards' numbers in one table read with absolute,x, unrolled) padded to the cycles of the card's setup of the same kind: compact pages stay where the others are, more work, and a page crossing can still change a count; (3) leave it.
- Title screen: a 48x2 graphic with more than one color on a line (asked 2026-11-10: "a multicolor card that can change color on the same scanline", look into whether it is possible; the AtariAge screen mentioned was not found in the transcripts). Looked at 2026-11-10: the 48 pixels are Player 0 (columns 1, 3, 5) and Player 1 (columns 2, 4, 6), 8 pixels each, written back to back in 12 cycles while they are drawn, so a color store in the middle of the picture would take the place of a column. What fits: a second color for each row, Player 1's columns in one color and Player 0's in another (two colors on a line, alternating every 8 pixels): the loop of 48x2_X_kernel.asm reads the row color in its first line and stores it in its second; reading both colors in the second line after "ldy aux2" takes the same 19 cycles (the sleep 4 and the missile0y byte go), with a second pointer (two bytes of zero page). Pixels that are off can show the color of the box behind the picture (already there, per frame). Not built. Asked 2026-11-10: a color for each of the 6 columns (changing the kernel is allowed). Not possible on a line: a column is 8 pixels, 2.67 cycles; the 48 pixels last 16 cycles, in which the four GRP stores of columns 3 to 6 already go back to back (3 cycles each, A, Y and X all holding picture bytes); 6 colors need 4 more stores (COLUP0 between columns 1 and 3 and 3 and 5, COLUP1 between 2 and 4 and 4 and 6), 8 stores in 16 cycles where 5 fit, and the registers are full when the first one would go. The same holds on DPC+ (the coprocessor cannot write the TIA; fast fetch makes loads 2 cycles, not stores). What is possible: 2 colors on a line (Player 0 columns 1, 3, 5 and Player 1 columns 2, 4, 6, a color each per row), and the two lines of a row can have different pairs. Asked 2026-11-10: 3 colors on a line. For pixels that are on, no: with VDEL each of the four picture writes has its 8 pixel window (the first between columns 1 and 3, the second between 2 and 4, the third between 3 and 5, the fourth between 4 and 6), and those are exactly the windows a color change of Player 0 or Player 1 needs; two stores 3 cycles (9 pixels) apart do not fit in one window. A third color for pixels that are off, yes: the box behind the picture (PF1/PF2 in COLUPF) with its color set for each row; reading the row's box color and storing it to COLUPF (8 cycles) fits in the cycles the first line of the loop frees when both player colors are read in the second line (11), after the picture is drawn. Asked 2026-11-10: could the four spots be color changes (4 changes a line)? Yes, but each spot is one write: a column from 3 to 6 gets either its picture or its color, not both. A column whose spot changes the color shows the picture of the column before it of the same player (with VDEL the picture write is also what brings the next picture in). Six colors on a line means columns 3 to 6 repeat columns 1 and 2 (16 pixels three times); any mix is fixed by the kernel code (for example new pictures in 3 and 4 and new colors in 5 and 6, which repeat 3 and 4). A column with a new picture and a new color needs two writes in its 8 pixels (9 pixels at the least between two): not possible. The user (2026-11-10): each change would need to change both picture and color. So a color for each column of a 48 pixel picture with no gaps is not possible (settled 2026-11-10). It would only fit with gaps: copies of a player 32 pixels apart (NUSIZ medium) leave 24 pixels between two copies, room for a picture and a color write each, but the picture then has a 16 pixel gap after every pair of columns, and the four writes need four values ready where the 6502 has three registers. Asked 2026-11-10: could any title kernel be rewritten for a color for each column? Not for a picture drawn on every line: a picture wider than 16 pixels on one line is copies of the two players, and the copies of a player take its one color register (COLUP0, COLUP1); a new color for a copy is a write between two copies, and a copy also needs its picture write there (see above). 96x2 is the same (copies, writes back to back, and players moved every line). The playfield and the background add colors (COLUPF, COLUBK) but 4 pixel wide blocks. What would give each column its picture and color: one copy of each player on a line (two columns, no write in the picture), the players moved to the next pair of columns on the next line (RESP0/RESP1 and HMOVE every line), so a row of 3 lines shows columns 1 and 2, then 3 and 4, then 5 and 6: every column is drawn on one line of three (stripes, a third as bright), not built. Otherwise flicker (frames that show different columns), which shows each column 1 frame of 3. Asked 2026-11-10: can the missiles or the ball add colors on a line? No new color: a missile is drawn in its player's color (COLUP0, COLUP1) and the ball in the playfield's (COLUPF); a line has those three and the background (COLUBK), so 4 colors at most without writes during the picture. What the ball could do: a block 1, 2, 4 or 8 pixels wide anywhere behind the picture (players in front) in the box color, finer than the playfield's 4 pixel blocks; in front of the players (CTRLPF priority) it covers their pixels with a solid block, which loses the picture there. A missile behind the picture fills the off pixels of its strip in its player's color. Asked 2026-11-10: can the kernel or the graphics be made faster to make time for a color write during the picture? No: it is not a question of cycles. A copy of a player that needs its picture and its color needs two writes between the end of the copy before it and its start, 8 pixels; the 6502 writes at most once in 3 cycles, 9 pixels, so even a kernel with nothing else to do cannot fit them (switching VDELP0 to show a picture loaded before is also a write). A write before the first copy's end or after the last copy's start changes no copy in between. More colors per line only by flicker: two frames with other colors for Player 0 and Player 1 and different pixels (the Titlescreen Kernel's color by flicker example), so a pixel on in one frame, the other, or both shows one of three colors for each player, at 30 Hz flicker; doable now with two frames of duration 1 and different row colors. Asked 2026-11-10: with no playfield pixels on, can the ball add a color, drawn on top of the picture? Yes, as a solid strip: with CTRLPF priority (playfield and ball in front of the players) the ball covers everything in its strip, the pixels that are on and the ones that are off, in COLUPF; it has no picture, only a width. Its width (1, 2, 4 or 8 pixels) and place are set for the graphic (RESBL/HMBL in the setup), and it can be turned on and off for each row (ENABL, 8 cycles: fits in the 11 the first line of the loop frees when both player colors are read in the second), or have its color set for each row instead (not both in those cycles). So a 4th color on a line, for a run of up to 8 pixels in one place: right for a solid accent (the picture solid under it on those rows), not for pixels of the picture. Not with the box behind the picture at the same time (its playfield would be in front too). A missile can do the same over Player 1's columns in Player 0's color (Missile 0 is in front of Player 1), no new color. Asked 2026-11-10: the ball behind the picture to fill an area, and missiles for the player colors. Ball behind (the usual priority): it shows in COLUPF where the pixels of its strip are off, like the box but 1 pixel exact and with on and off for each row (8 cycles, fits). Missiles (the order is Player 0 and Missile 0, then Player 1 and Missile 1, then playfield and ball, then background): Missile 1 is behind Player 0, so in Player 0's columns it fills the off pixels with Player 1's color, and in Player 1's columns it is the picture's color, so solid; Missile 0 is in front of Player 1 and has Player 0's color, so it only makes solid strips. The 48 pixel picture sets NUSIZ to three copies, and the missiles follow it: a missile is three copies 16 pixels apart, at the same place in each column of its player (Missile 1, 8 wide, over Player 0's columns fills all their off pixels in Player 1's color). No color past the 4 of a line. In the 11 free cycles of a row only one object can be turned on and off for each row; the others are on or off for the whole graphic (set in its setup), so bands down the picture. Asked 2026-11-10: can missiles be drawn behind the player sprites? The order is fixed by the TIA: only CTRLPF bit 2 changes it, and only for the playfield and ball (in front of the players). Missile 1 is always behind Player 0; Missile 0 is never behind Player 1; a missile and the player of the same number share one color register, so where they overlap it is the same color either way.

- Title tab Ball mode (asked 2026-11-11: "drawing ball is very, very slow", "ball is also drawing on top of pixels sometimes in editor", "make sure length of ball on row is limited to valid sizes"). DONE 2026-11-11, uncommitted, checked in headless Chrome (C:/tmp/shots/balldraw.cjs on the Bricks logo and C:/tmp/std/ball2.vcsgm): (1) the colors the canvas puts over cells (cellColors) are made again only when what they come from changes (ballCellColors keeps them for each frame) and PixelEditor.vue watches the list itself, so a stroke paints only its cells; (2) out of Ball mode the ball is drawn only on cells that are off (a pixel just drawn shows as drawn), in Ball mode the picture is drawn over the painted cells (cellColorsCover), as the TV shows it (checked: no picture pixel drawn in the ball color in either mode); (3) the ball cells of a row are one run of 1, 2, 4 or 8 cells (PixelEditor rowRunWidths, applied when a stroke ends, in its undo step): 3 cells painted become 4, 5 become 8, a cell taken off the end of 8 leaves 4, one Undo takes the stroke and the change back. The two rows of a pair still share the wider width (the kernel sets the width for two rows). (4) Found on the way: Undo just after Ball mode was turned on put the picture into the ball cells (one canvas for both, its history had the switch); Ball mode now has a separate canvas (key), so the picture's undo steps are not mixed with the ball's. - Title ball y offset (asked 2026-11-11: "see if there is any way to fix ball y offset. see if timing can be improved so there's more time to draw it in the right y position", then "build full fix for single color graphics, and keep researching full fix for row-color graphics, try to get the cycles needed"). DONE 2026-11-11 for both, uncommitted, tested in the emulator (6502.ts, C:/tmp/bbx/frameball.cjs and exactball.cjs: every pixel of the graphic's lines against titleFrameBallLines) on standard (C:/tmp/std/ball2.vcsgm: the Title Screen demo logo with row colors and the small single color graphic; ball3.vcsgm: the logo as single color) and DPC+ (C:/tmp/bricks/p6_ball2.vcsgm, the Bricks logo, and p6_ball3.vcsgm, single color): 0 wrong pixels with the ball on its rows' lines; the frames are the same as before but for the first line of each run of the ball (the picture, its colors and the line it starts on unchanged). PXE has no title screens (the title blocks build nothing there). How (found looking for the cycles): a row's two lines show the same picture, so the second line does not read the sixth column again (still in X) nor the fourth (kept in a byte), and the first line reads only five, its fourth column read on the line before into a byte of scorepointers (two bytes, one for each row of a pass; the loop does not use the pointers). The line before a row then has 32 cycles beside the picture: the row index, its color, ENABL, the width and the HMOVE (30). The test for the last row went to the first line of a row (36 cycles there): the last row's second line is drawn by a copy of it after a WSYNC that ends the loop (pf48x2_N_ball_lastA, _lastB). One loop for single color and row colors (48x2_N_kernel.asm, the card's setup reads the top row's ENABL into stack2 and its fourth column before the timed lines; draw_bmp_48x2_XB sets ENABL on the line before the graphic and reaches the loop at cycle 9). On the way: the first try read the top row's ENABL on the line after position48 and the graphic started a line lower (moved before the ball's place). The canvas out of Ball mode shows the ball on its rows (no half rows), the caption no longer says a line lower; TITLE_BALL_LOOP_BYTES 300 (the loop is 288 bytes). Not looked at by the user.
- Reset playfield scroll does not put the playfield back exactly where it started before the scroll (reported 2026-11-11: "it should reset to exact starting point before scroll"; "check all, don't ask about specific kernels"; then "don't reload background, fix scroll reset", "use exact count and do horizontal too", "make sure it works on PXE, which supports horizontal scroll as well"). DONE 2026-11-11, tested in the emulator on all kernels (tests/projects/mkscroll.cjs makes 15 projects, tests/emulator/scrollreset.cjs drives the joystick and compares each frame after Fire with the one before any scroll; sh tests/emulator/scrollreset_all.sh builds and runs them all). Before: the reset did nothing on the standard kernel when no background is taller than the screen (pfscroll turns the rows round in place and nothing followed them) and on DPC+ and PXE when the tall scroll cannot be used (pfscroll turns the playfield memory round; rows of 9 lines with pfres 22, or too many rows); the sideways scroll was not reset anywhere. A first fix loaded the background again (committed 97e0c21); the user did not want that (pixels drawn since were lost), so it was replaced the same day. Now: standard kernel with no background taller than the screen, the scroll blocks count exactly what pfscroll has turned (backgroundScrollTurns: the rows, round the rows of the playfield, a row counted when pfscroll's playfieldpos wraps; backgroundScrollShift: the pixels a row is turned left, round 32) and the reset turns the rows back (_pfturn: each row moved once, in cycles; about 650 cycles for 12 rows) and the pixels back (_pfshift: a byte swap for 16 pixels and at most 8 single steps a row; worst case about 3700 cycles for 12 rows, so a reset after a large sideways scroll can make that frame long), playfieldpos back to the row height it starts with, the row variables and row colors back to the top. DPC+ and PXE without the tall scroll: the scroll blocks add up what they gave pfscroll (_dpcPlainScroll, and _dpcPlainColorScroll for the blocks that move only the colors) and the reset gives pfscroll the rest of the way round. PXE sideways: PF_HOR_SCROLL_LO/HI set to 0. Unchanged (already exact): the standard kernel with a background taller than the screen (the rows come from the background in ROM as they scroll in, so the reset copies the top rows from it: the picture is exact, pixels drawn in rows that scrolled away were already gone, and the copy also undoes a sideways turn), DPC+ and PXE with the tall scroll (its position in a byte, turned back). Results 2026-11-11: all 15 projects back to the start exactly after every Fire (60 resets), on standard, DPC+ and PXE, the sideways ones too (PXE moves its playfield a column, 4 pixels, at a time, so 3 pixels Left shows no change there).

- Tiles of different heights (asked 2026-11-12: "do all tiles need to be the same size, or can they vary?", then "keep one height for now, but add variable height tiling to the plan"). Not started; one height for every tile stays for now. Today a background is a grid of tile numbers 4 across, every map row the same playfield rows, and the coprocessor finds a tile at its number times the height, so all tiles have one height (the width is always one column, 8 pixels). What it would take: each column a separate list of tiles with their heights (the bands of the Pitfall II ROM), a small table of each tile's start and height in the tile data; armprog.c functions 106 and 108 to 111 read a column's list and fill rows until the column is done; the build chooses the heights (an optimization, not the try of heights 1 to 8; a first version could take the tiles the user drew as they are); the Tiles section and the Stamp work with tiles of different heights (the fixed grid of cells goes); the Set playfield tile block finds the tile of a column that covers a row (a few more cycles a call). Worth most for backgrounds with large repeated areas beside fine detail.

- Playfield color rows not lined up with the pixel rows (reported 2026-11-12: "color rows seem off. for example if a 96 row playfield has pfres 96 and pf color rows 96, color rows should line up with pixel rows"). Found (DPC+, in the emulator): at 96 rows of 2 scanlines every color came a row late (row 1 had row 0's color and so on); 48 and 24 rows lined up. The kernel reads the color lists once every 2 scanlines, and one color a read is a step of 256, which DF4FRACINC/DF6FRACINC (a byte) hold as 255: the second read still took the first color. Fixed (uncommitted): DPCplus_kernel.asm (and its copy in includes-manifest.json), with dpccolorprime defined, reads the color lists once before the picture (bit 0 playfield, bit 1 background), which starts their fraction at 255 so each read takes the next color (up to 255 reads); the generator reserves dpccolorprime when a background's color rows are 2 scanlines or less (dpcPlusColorPrimeOf; with separate color rows, the picture's lines over the color rows; with the tall scroll, its thin rows) and sets it as each background loads. - Tiles on the standard kernel (asked 2026-11-12: "can standard kernel use tiles?", "how does standard kernel use 6502?", then "implement for standard kernel"). Built (uncommitted): pf-tiles.js planStandardTilemap (a tile is the byte it is in the playfield memory, the second and fourth columns with their bits the other way round, so a tile is kept for the column it is in and a mirrored half keeps one tile; no flips; up to 255 tiles; the height with the fewest bytes), standardTileRoutineLines (the 6502 routine _pftile_std: each row of the playfield memory takes, for its four columns, the byte of its tile from the plane of its row in the tile, the tiles kept as planes so the tile number is the index; _pftile_stdset for the Set playfield tile block, stopping at the last row of the memory; with Superchip it writes through the write port), the data in the same subroutine (one bank), each background's load setting the map's address and its rows and calling it (gosub with the bank tag). The build keeps the tiles only when they with the routine (about 110 bytes) and the calls take fewer bytes than the rows and their copies, and says which in the build log. The Options tab has the checkbox on the standard kernel too, the toolbox the block, the Playfield tab its tile tools (the Tiles button numbers the standard way). Tested in the emulator (6502.ts): five backgrounds of 12 rows that share parts, 13 tiles of 6 rows, 298 bytes (the rows 300): the same frames as with the option off over 288 frames of switching backgrounds, without and with Superchip (320); the Set playfield tile block put tile 0 into column 1 rows 6 to 11 and changed nothing else. - Color swatches keep their width on a narrow window (asked 2026-11-12: "color swatches should not change width on narrow window, like text bg color or score color swatches"). Built (uncommitted): the swatch of ColorSwatchPicker.vue (every tab's color swatch: the Text background color, the Score colors, the background cards' colors and the others) and the Quick colors swatches no longer shrink in a row with too little room (flex: 0 0 auto). Not looked at in the browser yet (the user checks it).: a tile is one playfield column (8 pixels) wide and a set number of rows tall; a background is a map of tile numbers with one shared tile set. On DPC+ the driver's copy (CALLFUNCTION 1: N bytes from the ROM down one column queue) is one tile, so the tiles and maps stay in the graphics bank (the copy reads the ROM, which avoids the display memory clash of the row dictionary) and a background loads with one call a tile (about 30 cycles); a tile can be changed while the game runs with one call. PF2 columns draw their bits the other way round from PF1 (both orders stored, or laid out by the build). Standard kernel: the 6502 expands tiles into the playfield RAM at load (few rows, little saved). PXE: its playfield setup per column, wider maps. The editors (a tile set and a map, or backgrounds cut into tiles by the build) are the bigger part. Would replace the row dictionary below.
- Background data compression, learned from a commercial DPC cartridge ROM (asked 2026-11-12: "can you learn anything from the rom in the file about how to compress background data? see if it's compatible with any of the existing kernels. the most likely is dpc+ so start there."; the ROM: C:/Users/nickr/Downloads/Pitfall II - Lost Caverns (USA).zip). Looked into 2026-11-12 (6502.ts runs it as an 8K DPC cartridge; scripts in C:/tmp/pf2an: trace.cjs, kernel.cjs, ramwrites.cjs, disasm.cjs, findstores.cjs, rowstats.cjs). What the ROM does: (1) the screen is cut into horizontal bands; between bands the kernel loads new pointers and windows into the data fetchers from RAM tables (one entry per band), so a band's graphics are read straight from the 2K display ROM, nothing copied; (2) the room map is one byte per band per screen (8 screens across): the low nibble picks one of 16 band templates (four tables: the PF1/PF2 data, the background color, the wall shapes), the high nibble the creature or object; (3) walls are one byte per map row with a bit per screen column, which sets a mask; the wall shape bytes hold the left wall in the high nibble and the right wall in the low, and PF0 is written twice a line through the mask (walls on one side only although the playfield is mirrored); in the caverns only PF0 changes line by line, PF1/PF2 are set once a band; (4) the surface reads three bytes a line from one fetcher: the color (its bit 0, which the TIA ignores, is a flag; one value marks the end), one byte for PF0 and PF1 both (PF0 shows only its top nibble), PF2; (5) the DPC fetchers can give a byte bit-reversed or nibble-swapped, so one table serves registers drawn in opposite bit orders; (6) vertical scrolling shifts the band tables by one and fills the new band only. For the app: the fetcher repointing per band does not fit the bB DPC+ kernel (its fetchers read the display RAM, which the coprocessor fills, and the kernel has no time to repoint between bands), but storing backgrounds compressed and expanding them when they load does, on every kernel: DPC+ (pfsetup now copies 4 bytes a row, column by column, with 4 coprocessor copies; an ARM routine, best as a program kept in the ROM as data like the offset block so only projects using it pay, could build the rows from a dictionary), the standard kernel (the 6502 copies a few rows; worth it mostly for backgrounds taller than the screen) and PXE (wide and tall playfields, the most rows). Measured on the projects in Downloads (rowstats.cjs; rows of 32 pixels, 4 bytes): Bricks of Olympus 110 rows, 20 different, every row a mirror image: 440 bytes now, 190 with one dictionary for all backgrounds (4 bytes a different row + 1 a row), 150 with mirrored rows kept as 2 bytes; RPG 2048 -> 1572; MOLES 384 -> 236; the 8-background projects 768 -> 392 to 552. Row colors (one byte a row) could be kept the same way. Build on DPC+ asked 2026-11-12 ("yes build on dpc+"). Built (uncommitted): generators/bbasic/pf-dict.js; the rows of the backgrounds that do not tile for the tall scroll (not PXE) are a dictionary (each different row 4 bytes) and a list of row numbers for each background, moved after the compile into the display data (the graphics bank, where the "playfield:" tables were: moveDictionaryToDisplayData in rom.js's shareDpcPlusPlayerData), and a routine of about 94 bytes in the background load subroutine builds a background with the data fetchers (DF4 reads the list, DF5 the row, DF0 to DF3 write the four column queues; about 20 cycles a row); used only when it is smaller in all and the code banks do not grow. A first version kept the tables in the code banks: more room taken where a big project runs out. Bricks 0-0-25: 20 different rows, 266 bytes less (250 in the graphics bank); the game frames the same as before. But its build then takes the compact title screen (the 48x2 graphics a line lower): the bank planner measures a unit from its label to the next label, so the title screen's data counts to whatever unit is before it; with the dictionary the title routine measured 5175 bytes (more than a bank) and no plan fitted; without the dictionary the load subroutine's text (the "playfield:" lines) made its estimate 5848 instead of about 1400, which happened to give a plan that fitted. Changed (uncommitted): the build goes compact only on a plan that is mostly measured (rom.js); estimateSubroutineSize leaves out the dictionary's rows. Not solved: the planner's measurement of the title screen unit. Asked 2026-11-12: fix the planner first, then keep the dictionary on. Found then: the dictionary as built does not work: the display data shares its addresses with the playfield queues (PF_data1 at $D65B is inside PF1L, $D608 to $D707; the coprocessor copies the "playfield:" tables from the ROM image, which does not change), so the rows read back with the fetchers were the queues' bytes and no background was drawn; it is switched off (DPC_ROW_DICTIONARY_ON in pf-dict.js) and is to be replaced by the tilemap (above), which copies from the ROM. Planner: tried giving subroutines an end label (exact sizes: the title routine then measured 2686, not 5175) and going compact only on a mostly measured plan or after 3 plans: Bricks 0-0-25 still went compact (the second plan 303 bytes short with about 900 bytes of room in all: two units of about 2600 bytes, the coprocessor program and the title routine, each need most of a bank), while without those changes it fits; put back as it was. Left: the packing of big units (planBankPacking in utils/bank-packer.js) and the margins it keeps.

The fork's vcs.h drops SWCHA2 and the four JOYx_DIR bytes without keeping their places, which moves TIM1T to $28F and TIM64T to $291 (should be $294 and $296): it can not be taken as it is (the standard and DPC+ kernels use it); the app's vcs.h with SWCHA2 left unnamed keeps the addresses. - Scroll position (found 2026-11-11 reading the scroll code; asked 2026-11-11 "fix scroll position count. that is the entire point of these bug fixes"). Checked on every kernel with a test that reads the background row at the top of the playfield from the picture and compares it with the Playfield scroll position block's value (tests/emulator/scrollpos.cjs, the projects of tests/projects/mkscroll.cjs put the position into missile0x every frame and have Player 1 follow the scroll; sh tests/emulator/scrollpos_all.sh). Found before the fix: (1) standard kernel, background as tall as the screen: the position went the wrong way (Up took it down, below 0 to 255) and did not go round the rows, and its count of lines toward a row went up whichever way pfscroll went; (2) DPC+ and PXE with the tall scroll: the position was the rows of the thin scroll (two to a background row here), counted the wrong way (Up took it down from 120); (3) DPC+ and PXE without the tall scroll: no position at all (a variable that was not there); (4) the sprites that follow the scroll: on the standard kernel they moved a y step for each row the picture moved instead of each line (2 scanlines for 16), and on DPC+ and PXE the wrong way (the picture down, the sprite up); without the tall scroll by 1 or 2 lines for a row of 9; (5) Stop at edge going up on the standard kernel with a taller background let the picture go up to a row past the bottom of the background (the old row showing) before it stopped; on DPC+ with the tall scroll it stopped only before going round, past the bottom of the background. The standard kernel with a taller background had the right position. Fix: the row at the top is counted when pfscroll goes round a row (playfieldpos wraps: to the row height going up, the row going up by one, round the rows of a background as tall as the screen; to 1 going down), the same way for the two kinds of background; the DPC+ tall scroll keeps the thin row at the top of the view (0 to the background's thin rows less one, the view at the top of the memory after a load instead of one background in) and the position is it divided by the thin rows a background row is cut into (_dpcScrollRepeats when the backgrounds differ); without the tall scroll the count of what pfscroll was given (_dpcPlainScroll) is the position; the sprites move with the picture every call (the lines it moved, the same way); Stop at edge: Up stops at the furthest row with its top at the top of the screen (the standard kernel: only a call from the top of the furthest row is stopped; DPC+ wrapping: the rows less the rows on screen, _dpcScrollLimit when the backgrounds differ), Down at row 0 the same way. The background load of a background as tall as the screen puts playfieldpos back too (the sub-row count is gone). Reset playfield scroll uses the row (the turns count is gone) and puts the DPC+ view back to 0. PXE: the start showed background row 16 at the top (rows 16 to 23 in the top 32 thin rows): the tile load (utils/dpc-tile.js) writes the last repeat past the end of the 256 row buffer onto the top rows and never wrote the first repeat back; the old code hid it by starting the view one background in (one Up then jumped to row 16). Fixed: with a remainder the loop writes the first repeat too. DONE 2026-11-12 (uncommitted), tested in the emulator (6502.ts): the position, the picture and the sprite agree at every step on 18 projects (standard 9, DPC+ 5, PXE 4; tests/emulator/scrollpos.cjs reads the PXE variables from the program's memory, pRAM); Reset playfield scroll back to the start on 19 projects, 4 resets each, the sprites left out (they follow the scroll and Reset does not move them back). Found on the way: on DPC+ 3 lines of Player 1's graphics stayed at the bottom of the screen (lines 192 to 194) at its x (seen in scroll_dpc_plain after Up 23 and Reset). Not the scroll: a virtual sprite (Player 1 to 9) drawn over the last lines of the screen (y 178 to 190 in the test projects) left them there after it moved away, on every DPC+ project. The coprocessor (public/bb19/includes/custom/main.c) cleared 192 bytes of the sprite buffer P1GFX (and filled 192 of P1COLOR and 193 of P0COLOR) each frame, the kernel draws 195 lines. Fixed (DONE 2026-11-12, uncommitted): the whole 256 bytes of each (257 from P0COLOR-1); the 16 programs rebuilt (C:/tmp/armbuild/build16n.sh, the repo's main.c built the same bytes as the committed ones first), 4 to 8 bytes bigger, custom2.bin also in includes-manifest.json. Tested in the emulator: Player 1 stepped through y 150 to 255 and back leaves nothing on 5 DPC+ projects (before: 3 lines), Reset back to the start on them (Player 1 left out), Down 120 then Up 120 (and the other way) gives the same frame as the start, sprite included, on 8 projects (standard, DPC+, PXE); the score 123456 reads right on DPC+ (tests/projects/score_dpc.vcsgm, tests/emulator/scoredigits.cjs: the score loop did not cross a page).
- Title ball loop, more cycles, round 2 (asked 2026-11-11 "continue", stopped when the session was moved toward the cloud; not built): on paper the line before the second row of a pass can get to 6 or 7 free cycles with row colors and the line before a pass about 10: one table for ENABL and HMBL (ENABL reads bit 1, HMBL bits 4 to 7; written together on the line before a pass), the second line of a row taking its third column from the byte the first line read, the end test only on the line of the last row of a pass (the rows shown are known when the ROM is built), the next row's color, ENABL, width and sixth column read a line early with what that frees (tables at -1 need a byte before them in their page). To check: whether the score mode (CTRLPF bit 1) changes the ball's color (if not, the width and ENABL can share a table for graphics with no box).
- Tests folder (asked 2026-11-11: to put the tests of C:\tmp in a tests folder of the repo, so a cloud session could reuse them, then the reusable harness only, the newest test files and ROMs, Bricks included; "when pushing to other repos than absply, do not include test files"): DONE 2026-11-11, committed 6118a01, pushed to absply: tests/ (browser, emulator, tia, hooks, lib/paths.cjs, projects, roms, README.md), tests/out ignored; the emulator and TIA scripts run from the repo, the browser runner needs the dev server. Leave tests/ out of pushes to other remotes. Asked too: remove the older test files and ROMs in C:\tmp (the scope put to the user first).
- Title ball loop: more free cycles (asked 2026-11-11 "before building anything, try to optimize the loop's spare cycles to get more cycles"). DONE 2026-11-11, uncommitted, tested in the emulator: the frames of the four test ROMs (C:/tmp/std/ball2, ball3, C:/tmp/bricks/p6_ball2, p6_ball3) are the same pixel for pixel as with the loop before it, the ball exact; a two frame card (ball4, ball5, rc2) exact on both frames; row colors seen right on every line (C:/tmp/std/rc1.vcsgm, rc2.vcsgm: four colors in turn, two lines each); 262 lines a frame. Changes (pf48x2_N_ball_loop): the loop turned so its jmp is on a line inside a row (the line before a pass no longer takes it); the second line of a row reads the picture with the next row's index (the tables at +1: each table is in one page with the byte after it, so no page is crossed), so the line inside the row has no iny; the third column of a row is read a line early too (scorepointers+2 and +3), the first line of a row then takes 31 cycles before the picture's stores (draw_bmp_48x2_XB reaches the loop at cycle 10); a single color graphic has no color stores in the loop (sleep 10 in their place). Fixed on the way: the copy that draws the last row read its tables at +1 with the next row's index, which is 255 under the bottom row (one past the table): it uses the row's index. Free cycles a line now (row colors / single color): the line before the second row of a pass (where the pair's width and move are set) 3 / 13 (was 2), the line before a pass 13 / 23 (was 9), the lines inside the rows 2 and 6 (were 7 and 14): the reads a line early move cycles from the lines inside the rows (7 each) to the lines before the rows (1 each), which is where a feature for each row needs them. TITLE_BALL_LOOP_BYTES 330 (the loop is 290 bytes). Seen on the way (not from this change): a card with row colors and more than one frame starts one line lower than with one frame (C:/tmp/std/rc1 at line 69, rc2 at 70; the frame stays 262 lines).
- Title ball in more than one place on a line (asked 2026-11-11: "see if there is any way to place the ball in more than 1 x location per line, even if there needs to be empty columns in-between usage", then "look more deeply into multiple balls position per line. it shouldn't shift ball placement below it"). Looked into with a test ROM (C:/tmp/dasmtest/mkresbl.cjs, balllines.cjs, in 6502.ts): a RESBL draws the ball on the line it is written on, 4 pixels after the beam (RESBL at clock c: pixel c-64), a ball the counter had put further right on that line is not drawn (the counter starts again), and the lines after it have the ball where the RESBL put it; two RESBLs on every line (clocks 90 and 180) give two balls on every line (pixels 26 and 116) with nothing moved below. So the lines below need not move: each line strikes RESBL again for each place. What stops it inside the picture: a RESBL places the ball where the beam is when it is written, and while the beam crosses the picture's 48 columns (cycles 41 to 57) the CPU is writing the picture (GRP stores ending at cycles 44, 47, 50, 53, the fewest the 48 pixel picture can be drawn with: four changes of what the players show, each store makes one); the first free write is at cycle 56 (column 48, just right of the picture) and the last before is at 41 (column 3), which starts the counter again and so takes away the ball further right on that line. So two places inside the picture on one line is not possible with this kernel; outside it (left of column 0 or right of column 47) a second place on every line is possible, on a 3 pixel grid, with a RESBL a line (3 cycles) and more for a place that changes by line. Asked 2026-11-11 "keep looking for other ways inside the picture": the ball has one position counter, and inside the picture its place can only come from the HMOVEs (a RESBL written while the beam is over the picture would need a free write cycle there, and there is none), so one ball a line inside the picture is a limit of the TIA with this kernel. Other ways to add color behind the picture: (a) two or more ball layouts on frames that alternate (each shown 30 times a second, flicker): works now with a second animation frame of the same picture, duration 1, Play once off (tested: C:/tmp/std/ball4.vcsgm, frames 100 to 103 alternate between the two balls); costs a copy of the picture for each layout; an editor option could make the frames. (b) Playfield blocks behind the picture in the ball's color: PF2, 4 pixels wide, mirrored (columns 0-23 mirrored into 24-47, the reflected playfield; the other playfield registers would also show left of the picture); a new PF2 for every two rows fits the loop (9 free cycles on the line before every other row), for every row only for single color graphics (with their color stores taken out of the loop). (c) The background color for each row (a band across the whole line), the same cycles as (b). (d) The missiles: only in the picture's color (COLUP0/COLUP1), three copies 16 pixels apart. Put to the user, waiting.
- Questions answered 2026-11-11 (no change made): (1) a missile behind the 48x2 picture: its color is the player's (COLUP0 or COLUP1), which the kernel sets to the row's color on every line for both players, so it would only add pixels in the picture's color, and NUSIZ is 3 copies close for the 48 pixels, so each missile shows three times 16 pixels apart; it is drawn over the ball and the box, at the players' level. (2) The box behind a title graphic (PF1 and PF2, reflected) is set once for the whole graphic (the setup writes PF1 and PF2), so it is the full height; a box shape for each row needs two table reads and stores a row (14 cycles, each in its window: PF2 after cycle 60 and before 33 of the next line, PF1 after 71 and before 28): the shared loop (pf48x2_X_loop) has 7 spare cycles a row, the per graphic ball loops a little more; not possible as the loops are; a single color graphic (no color stores) would have the room in a separate loop.

PART 1B: DETAILS OF THE OPEN ITEMS (the full entries)
------------------------------------------------
- Fire / Bounce vertical speed on DPC+ (asked 2026-11-06; built, tried in the emulator, committed 115a766; played in Bricks and confirmed working by the user): a pixel is about 1.6 lines wide on a TV, so equal numbers of lines and pixels made up and down headings (and the up and down leaning ones of 16 directions) look slower than sideways ones (measured at speed 2 over 30 frames: up 60 lines, up-leaning 78 lines and 33 pixels, right 60 pixels). Chosen: always correct it on DPC+ (not PXE): every heading moves its vertical part 1.6 times as far (up and down 1.625 x speed, diagonals 1.625 vertically and 1 sideways, halfway headings 2.06 / 0.56 and 1.31 / 0.87), in amounts a frame with the leftover sixteenths kept in the object byte (tables _fire_tab_smm, _fire_tab_snm, _fire_tab_sbig and the step tables _fire_tab_smain/smin in generators/bbasic/sprites.js: stretchRow). Objects that check the playfield step a pixel at a time with the same main and minor amounts, so the ball is stretched too; the coprocessor version of that movement (function 101 of armprog.c) is not used on DPC+ any more (it still has the old amounts). Measured now: up 97 lines, diagonal 97 and 60, sideways 60, right-leaning 52 lines and 79 pixels; with check playfield the same numbers; Bricks 0-0-20 builds (620 bytes free) and runs. Not checked: how the Bricks ball feels (it goes up and down 1.6 times faster than before on DPC+), speeds above 14 (the amounts are bytes), Bounce frames at the walls beyond a run, PXE (unchanged).
- Sprite pool (asked 2026-11-06, built, build checked on DPC+, not played): blocks in the Sprites category that share a range of virtual sprites (the user picks "players x to y") out among spawned objects, e.g. Bricks powerups. Blocks: "Sprite pool: players x to y spawn in a free sprite" (takes the first hidden sprite, shows it, runs its blocks; an "if no sprite is free" input), "For each active sprite in pool", "Release the pool sprite", "Release all sprites in pool" (use it at the start of the game: a sprite is free while hidden), "Sprite pool is full". "pool sprite" is a choice in the Player blocks' player list, the Collided blocks' lists and the Fire object list. The build (src/utils/sprite-pool.js, called from withHeadlessWorkspace in hooks/rom.js like the scene blocks) makes ONE copy of the blocks inside a spawn / for each block, as a subroutine for the first sprite of the pool; for each other sprite it swaps what that sprite has with the first sprite's (kernel x, y, height, the frame, animation, color and size, every dev var named player<N>..., and the sprite's bits in the shared flag bytes: found through devVarRegistry and spriteFlagBits in bbasic.js), calls the subroutine and swaps back (an asm subroutine _poolswap_A_N per sprite, no RTS: it may sit in another bank, where the return the compiler adds is the one that works). The blocks are also given as "sprite_pool_shadow" blocks for the other sprites: not generated (their subroutines are dropped) but seen by every pre-scan and by the generators, so the other sprites get the same variables, bits, colors and animations. Inside a custom function the subroutine is kept in the function's bank (changed 2026-11-08, see the entry at the top: no copy for each sprite). The exchange uses only registers (no temp bytes). A pool with a Return, Go to label or Change state block in it uses the exit through temp1 (2026-11-08). The blocks outside a pool that name the pool sprites must name all of them ("all pool sprites"): a block that names only one (for example Stop gravity on Player 3 alone) makes that sprite use variables the first one has no need of (Gravity: Mask and Max), which are not carried over; the build says so in the compile log. Played in the emulator, pool (two sprites, the second one spawned so the exchange runs) against the same blocks on the player itself: Seek, Lock, Inertia (accelerate then decelerate), Fire, Fire with Gravity, Gravity, Set animation, ROM noise, Rainbow colors, all the same. Played later (2026-11-07): Platform, Bounce, Scroll with playfield. Not possible: Fade. ROM: a 7-block Seek/Lock/Inertia/Gravity/Bounce pool of 2 saved about 220 bytes over copies; a body smaller than the swap code costs about the same. RAM does not grow (the hidden state is the frame 255 flag). Checked on DPC+ in the emulator: exchanging two sprites twice gives the original picture, spawning into the second sprite of a pool puts the values on that sprite, noise works. Not checked: all the blocks with state (Seek and so on) played The editor warns when two pools of different ranges share a sprite. Seek, Lock/Unlock, Inertia, Gravity, Bounce and Stop fired also have "pool sprite" (checked on DPC+: they build, each pool sprite gets its state bytes, so a pool of 2 with all of them filled the 29 DPC+ bonus bytes); Stop fired now lists players 2 and up too. Also done: "Find playfield pixel collided with" (both blocks, which now list players 2 to 9 under DPC+ too, with the sprite finding its pixel from that player's x, y and height), "Undo last move", the Platform blocks (jump, state, jumps made) and the Distance blocks have the pool sprite (the Distance blocks list players 2 and up too). Pool sprite is only offered in a block inside a Sprite pool "spawn" or "for each" (blocks/sprite-pool.js patches the dropdown lists); "All pool sprites" (any block that does something) and "Any pool sprite" / "All pool sprites" (the Collided block) work anywhere: the block shows "pool players x to y" and the build makes a copy for each sprite (utils/sprite-pool.js), joined with or / and for Collided; checked on DPC+ (Set X on all, Collided any with Playfield). Rainbow colors work on players 2 to 9 under DPC+ (pointer in playerpointers like Player 1, one flag bit each in rainbowExtraFlags): seen in the emulator on Player 2 and Player 1 (the same bands of colors); ROM noise on a pool sprite, on "all pool sprites" and with Stop also checked there. The sprite overlap event has no pool choice. Fade color for players 2 to 9 is not possible (their colors are ROM tables for fixed colors). Not done: PXE sprites in those lists, the sprite overlap event, Fade color (Player 0 and 1 only); a pool sprite outside a pool block is left out of the build with a note in the compile log.
- Score bar colors (asked 2026-11-06, option 3 chosen: bars follow the digit line colors, or one flat color from a block). Done: on DPC+ the kernel's score loop writes COLUPF at the end of each line from the line's color (cycle neutral), so the bars show the same gradient one line later (the top bar line keeps the plain score color). New block "Score bars: set color": the bars keep that one flat color (the template stops copying scorecolor to pfscorecolor, and on DPC+ const scorebarflat turns the gradient code off); does nothing on PXE. Seen in the emulator. Kernel changes must also go into public/bb19/includes-manifest.json (the build reads that, not the .asm). Not tried with the Text Minikernel or a Squish font. A title screen that hides the score does not hide flat bars.
- Fire speed differs by angle (reported 2026-11-06; opt-in checkbox "same speed at every angle" written, builds, played and confirmed working by the user 2026-11-06): a diagonal moves speed pixels on both axes, about 1.41 times a straight shot. Ticked, a straight shot gets 7/16 of its speed in extra pixels, taken off the speed again after the move (a byte of memory a fired object, its speed kept in a variable). Changed 2026-11-06 from slowing diagonals.
- Bounce block random angle spread (requested 2026-11-06, written, built; played and confirmed working by the user 2026-11-06): a checkbox and a 1 to 4 steps number after Set angle manually; ticked, a fired missile or ball leaves at the reflected (or manual) angle turned by 0 to N direction steps either way, unticked nothing is worked out. Without a manual angle it runs on the first frame of a collision bounce, and at each flip of an off screen edge bounce.
- Skip the work of off-screen sprites (asked 2026-11-05, after the #23 estimate: the guard costs about 7 to 8 cycles a sprite on screen, a skipped sprite saves about 60 to 70). Chosen: skip the box tests and the animation step of a sprite that is off screen (its animation freezes: no frame counting, no "animation finished" for it until it is back) and count a sprite hidden by a visibility block as off screen. Where: generators/bbasic/sprite-box-width.js (box pairs) and the animation quickPast in generators/bbasic.js. Looked at in the code before building (2026-11-05): not built, the numbers are against it. The animation step of a sprite that is on screen and not changing is already the quick path in the main bank (quickPast: bit, bpl, bvs, ldx, inx, cpx, bcs, stx, jmp: 23 cycles, plus 5 for the hidden check); a skip test in front of it (y at or past 192, x at or past 177, about 7 cycles each) costs 14 on every on-screen sprite and saves 23 for an off-screen one, so it pays only when more than about 60% of the sprites are off screen. The box pairs end at their first compare, 12 to 24 cycles a pair, and a test for each pair costs about as much; skipping them for a sprite needs the pairs grouped by sprite (a pair has two sprites). A hidden sprite (frame 255) already skips its animation and its box. Worth building only for a project with many sprites that are mostly off screen; say so and it can be an option.
- Title Screen Kernel bytes during gameplay (requested 2026-11-06, built, committed in 91e7620, not released yet, not shown to work yet): on DPC+ the freed sprite bytes the Title Screen Kernel works in only while the title screen runs can hold user variables that only gameplay uses (titleScratchHosted in init of generators/bbasic.js; set back to 0 when gameplay starts). Bricks has 10 such variables but they already host its 13 Title Screen variables, so its totals did not change (16 of 16 bonus slots, 8 in display memory). Open: a project with more gameplay-only variables than Title Screen variables to see it work; the 3 Title Screen variables of Bricks that still take a slot each could share a slot with gameplay-only system bytes.
- Stack for variables (looked into 2026-11-06): the DPC+ stack is 256 bytes through data fetcher 7 (USERSTACK), a pointer that moves with push and pull; the display memory spill already has 768 slots through fetcher 6, so it adds no room, random access costs the same pointer setup, and fetcher 7 is used by the kernel for the second player's motion. A use would be a second pointer kept while the first is in use. Not planned.
- End title screen should allow the title screen variables and flag bits to be reused (asked 2026-11-05). Today a project with an End title screen block gets no title/game flag bit sharing (flagOverlay in generators/bbasic.js needs !titleEndUsed), because "Title screen update" keeps running after the block, so title blocks there can still touch the title flag bits while game blocks use the same bits. In Bricks 0-0-16 the sharing was tried with that condition removed and the build turned it off again (a game-phase flag is used by a block in Title screen update). The variable sharing already handles code after End (titleEndResets, keepsAfterEnd). Done 2026-11-05, uncommitted: after End has run the title graphic blocks in Title screen update do nothing (titlescreen.js, a goto past them while the kernel-ended bit is set), and the flag sharing is no longer switched off by End (flagOverlay). Bricks 0-0-17: same variable count (25 of 25; its missileFireFlags are also used by a Subroutine, so the sharing still turns itself off), the title screen frames match the old build over 341 frames; not tested: pressing fire to End the title screen, and a project where the sharing stays on with End. Also added 2026-11-05 (asked): a "Start title screen" block next to End title screen (titlescreen_start: goto title_start_begin, so the kernel comes back on and the Title screen start blocks run again; nothing inside Title screen start); builds in Bricks 0-0-17 with the block before End, the branch with fire pressed was not run; wiki entry not written (only when told).
- Bricks of Olympus: the score does not show properly under the DPC+ kernel (reported 2026-11-01, project L:/Dropbox/Game Development/Atari 2600/VCS Game Maker/Bricks of Olympus/Bricks_of_Olympus_0-0-16.vcsgm; the user says the score font does not show a 2: the 6th digit of the score row looks wrong on the title screen, the first five zeros are the edited zero of the .21stcentury font); also check the standard kernel. Being looked at.
- Bricks of Olympus score digits (reported 2026-11-01, not fixed): the last digit on the right of the score is corrupted under the DPC+ kernel with the 21st Century font, edited or not, and fine with the default font (the user). The ROM holds the font in order (the table at 0x1434 of the Bricks build, digits 0 to 9, 8 bytes each), so the suspects are the other coprocessor program (DPC_SCOREFONT in public/bb19/includes/custom/main.c: it reads the table address from the 89th and 90th byte of the score digits in the display memory, in the scorefont build of the program) or the 6502 side that puts the address there (dpcPlusScoreFontPointerLines in utils/score-font.js), for the sixth digit; the default font does not use that path. A node check of the display memory needs the emulator to reach the stage select (the ARM of the node harness stops on a fire press: invalid 16 bit ARM read from $AAF2).
- Open (asked 2026-11-01): Restart ROM button checked 2026-11-05 in the headless harness (ballpf_dpcplus: the frames after a click match the frames after a fresh load, and the game 4 seconds in is different): it restarts the ROM. Use fewer variables in the Bricks of Olympus 0-0-16 project. Done so far (uncommitted): a Subroutine or Function that nothing calls is left out of the build (src/utils/unused-definitions.js, used in withHeadlessWorkspace in hooks/rom.js): 24 to 22 of 25 letters, bank 4 free 31 to 64 bytes (powerupSpawn was never called; it was the only user of the collision pixel result bytes). Text scroll (done, uncommitted): when the scroll speed and pause are fixed numbers from 1 to 31 the frames-left count lives in the top 5 bits of textScrollState (text-scroll.js, text-minikernel.js), one byte less: Bricks 22 to 21 letters, scroll checked on the Bricks title page (forward, hold, back, hold). Looked at 2026-11-05 (Bricks 0-0-16 is 25 of 25 letters and 15 of 15 bonus RAM): the flag pool holds 22 bits in 3 bytes, and only 1 bit (missileFireFlags) is game-phase, so letting the title and game flags share bits (it is off because the project has End title screen) would not save a byte; of the 15 title variables 14 already sit in gameplay-only slots, the one left over is titleCardBoxColor_3_1; the scroll's end byte (textScrollEnd) could be a compiled-in number when the project has only one scrolling message with a fixed place (about one byte), not built. - Harmony cart fix option (requested 2026-11-06, done, committed and released in 0.53.17): a checkbox on the Options tab (DPC+), on by default, for the temp1=temp1 after each bank declaration (enableHarmonyCartFix in the configuration; a project that never saved it counts as on).
