PART 1C: DONE, KEPT FOR REFERENCE
---------------------------------
- DPC+ score digits drawn wrong (found 2026-11-06 in the sprite pool tests; fixed, not committed): a score of 123456 came out as about 122254 on DPC+ (standard kernel fine), in 0.53.17 and since 47f4923 (0.53.16). Cause: the coprocessor program custom2*.bin is included at the start of bank 1 and the kernel code follows it, so its size moves the score loop; the rebuilt programs of 47f4923 are 16 bytes bigger and put the loop across a page boundary, which makes its taken branch (bpl scoreloop) take 1 cycle more, every line of the score is drawn one cycle late and the digits are scrambled (the same programs with the loop moved 96 bytes: fine; the source change of that commit does not matter: the old checkswap with 16 padding bytes was wrong too, the new one with the old size fine). Fix in DPCplus_kernel.asm (and the manifest): before scorekernel, padding bytes (never run) move the loop to the start of the next page when it would cross one, worked out from the labels scoreloop and scoreloopbpl, so it holds for any size of the program and any options. Checked in the emulator: 6 and 5 digits, Squish font, score bars with line colors, shifts of 16, 40, 70 and 130 bytes in front. Other cycle-exact loops of the kernel may have the same weakness (not checked). The padding costs up to about 50 bytes of bank 1 in the layouts that need it (b17nocircle still builds, 620 bytes free).
- Distance X/Y blocks failed to build on DPC+ (found 2026-11-06 while testing the pool; fixed, builds with a plain pair and with a pool sprite): "Unknown Mnemonic 'jsr ._distance_abs_diff'" at assembly. The shared routine was registered by generateDistanceChecks, after generateSubroutines had already written the subroutines out; bbasic.js now registers it before that.
- Paddle drawn and stopped a few pixels off in Bricks 0-0-17 and 0-0-20 (DPC+, reported 2026-11-05; fixed, committed in 0.53.16): since 02f1a2a (0.53.15 and 0.53.14 not affected: 0.53.14 was built before? no, 0.53.12 and 0.53.13 are good) the sprite sort of the coprocessor driver (checkswap in custom/main.c) gave each idle sprite with no height a display line of its own (order 0,1,2,3,4, 4 lines) instead of one line for all of them (order 0,4,4,4,4, 1 line), and that drew the paddle 3 px to the right (stops: left 28, right 137 instead of 25 and 134, the walls being at 23 and 136). The special case that stops a text row's no-height sprite and the paddle taking turns being left out now applies only to a sprite with no height next to one with a height, within 5 lines. The 16 custom2*.bin are rebuilt (12 to 16 bytes more each; Bricks still fits bank 1 with 0 free). Checked on b17nocircle and Bricks 0-0-20: the paddle stops at 25 to 40 and 119 to 134, the sprite order is the old one. Not checked: the text row flicker case after the change, and the ball stopping at a pixel report (the ball bounces between the walls in the 0-0-20 run, bricks not tried).
- Bricks of Olympus 0-0-20 did not build: Too many variables, 46 defined and 45 available (reported 2026-11-05; fixed, committed in 0.53.17). A variable the blocks only set to a plain number and never read, change or pass on is no longer kept, and its setters make no code (writeOnlyVariableIds in generators/bbasic.js init, variables_set in generators/bbasic/variables.js): bounceCount and bounceCountMax in Bricks (one of them was enough). Checked: it builds with all 5 sprites on, 25 of 25 letters, 16 of 16 bonus RAM, 7 in Display RAM; the paddle stops at 25 to 40 and 119 to 134 and the ball bounces as before. The Too many variables error now lists the variables that wait for a letter. What the 22 that waited for a letter were: 11 user variables that each host a title screen variable (stageCount, brickMin, activePowerup, stageSelect, brickMax, playerHealth, playerLives, flashTimer, bounceCount, bounceCountMax, colorOffset) and 11 internal bytes used in assembly (flagPool1, flagPool2, pfboxhit, pfboxx, pfboxy, ballFireDir, missileFireFrac0, ballBounceStage, player0animnext, player1animnext, _ctrlpf). More that could be done: a variable set only once to a number and never changed could be a constant (stageCount 5, brickMin 0, brickMax, colorOffset 0 in Bricks); pfboxx and pfboxy are copies of the one player's x and y when only one player asks for the box test; the joystick tap flag takes 2 bits that could be 1; the 3 flag bytes hold 22 bits (6 fewer would make it 2 bytes).
- Bricks of Olympus 0-0-20 did not build at 28 or 32 rows (reported 2026-11-05; fixed, committed and released in 0.53.17). The cause was not the ROM running out: for a row height that is not a whole number of lines the ball's fire code (generateMissileFireChecks in generators/bbasic/sprites.js) put the row conversion routine of rowOfLineCode in the program as it is, with its asm block closed by a literal @end and its instructions in the first column, so the assembler read the lines after it as assembly (Unknown Mnemonic 'temp2') and the relocator took the errors for an overflow and moved units about for 77 attempts. rawAsmLines in sprites.js now writes that block the way raw code wants it. Measured on the real file: 24 rows built with 620 bytes free before; 26, 28 and 32 rows (project and every background) now build, and the ball bounces in the emulator (a uniform 32 has a shorter playfield: the backgrounds have 22 pixel rows). Note for test files: the row count of a DPC+ project is dpcPlusPfres in the configuration (pfres is the standard kernel's), and a background that sets a pfres of its rows overrides it. Still open: check the other raw code that calls rowOfLineCode (the stage code of the bounce, the pixel collision blocks) with a row height that is not a whole number of lines, for the same mistake.
- Coprocessor program (requested 2026-11-05, committed and released in 0.53.17, more could come): the program for the Bricks set went from 2,088 to 1,932 bytes (the 8-direction tables are gone, the fired-object movement checks a lit cell in one place, the bounce reads the two cells beside the hit once; custom/armprog.c and the 125 files of custom/prog), with the same answers on 6,000 random calls and about 1% fewer memory accesses (tool: C:/tmp/arm/fuzz.cjs runs two builds in the project's ARM emulator on the same calls). More could come from the bounce (about 700 bytes) and the fired-object movement (about 830). Open: the bank panel reports 27,716 of 28,336 bytes used before and after, so check what the panel counts. Update 2026-11-06: 1,920 bytes for the Bricks set (from 2,088), the bounce reads its two cells beside the hit once and tries its two wall cells with one piece of code, and the fired-object movement checks a lit cell in one place; the ball bounce and the pixel finder now also run on the coprocessor for rows that are not a whole number of lines (a row increment instead of 256 / lines). Checked with C:/tmp/arm/fuzz.cjs and allsets.py (all 96 programs with these functions, 500 calls each, no difference). Tried and dropped: inlining the lit-cell test with its numbers in locals (+58 bytes, -3% accesses), packing them in one number, the step tables as constants, merging the first two bounce stages.
- Score Bar units block (requested 2026-11-06, done, committed in 91e7620, not released yet): returns the number of units a score bar shows (the bits set in its byte, 0 to 8: a health bar of N units gives N, N lives give N) as a plain number; the count is made by asm lines ahead of the statement that uses it (temp1, or the byte scoreBarUnits inside a custom function). Checked on the standard kernel in the emulator (health 3 reads 3 and sets 3 lives). Also checked on DPC+ and inside a custom function (health 5 reads 5 and sets 5 lives). Open: the wiki Score page entry (when told).
- DPC+ stack (requested 2026-11-06, done, committed in 91e7620, not released yet): a function that reads its arguments pushes the old values of the shared fnParam snapshot bytes when it starts and pulls them back before each Return, when some function body calls a function (generators/bbasic/function.js; DPC+ only), so a function that calls another and then reads the argument it was given gets that value (checked: outer(9) calling inner(5) returns 9; the standard kernel, with no stack, still returns 5). New block 'keep [variable] safe on the stack while' (push, the blocks, pull; nest it for several variables). Open: the standard kernel could keep the same values in a variable kept for that; a Return or jump inside a keep block leaves the stack out of step (said in its tooltip); wiki entries (when told).
- DPC+ score line colors (requested 2026-11-06, done, uncommitted since 91e7620): the Score tab shows 8 swatches for DPC+ in the pinned area under the graphic editor toolbar (label first, a reset icon button at the end; dpcPlusScoreColors in the configuration, 8 color bytes; none = white on every line), written to the scorecolors: table, and the glyph previews show the line colors when they differ. When the lines have different colors the Score color blocks no longer change the DPC+ score lines (checked in the emulator: 8 different colors with a Score color block in the project); a new block 'Score line colors: set hue to' recolors them and keeps the brightness of each line (checked). The Score tab's graphic editor toolbar has no color swatch or eyedropper (showColorSwatch of GraphicEditorToolbar.vue, also off for the Text Minikernel font editor), 'Use extra glyphs' is in the row of 5 score digits, and the Restart ROM button under the emulator uses mdi-restore. Open: the wiki Score page (when told).
- Text line colors (requested 2026-11-06, done on DPC+ and on the standard kernel, uncommitted): the Text tab has 5 swatches, one for each of the 5 glyph rows of a line of text (textLineColors in the configuration; none or all the same = the one color of TextColor). The text kernel reads the color of each row from a 5 byte table in the ROM (ldy.w TextLineColors+row instead of ldy TextColor: 1 cycle more on lines that have room for it): withDpcPlusTextLineColors and withTextLineColors12b in generators/bbasic/text-minikernel-files.js. Checked in the emulator on both kernels (5 rows in 5 colors). While the colors differ the Text color blocks do not change the rows, and row 2 uses the same colors. Open: a hue block for the text lines like the score one (not asked), the Title screen text cards, a look at the Text tab in the app.
- Variables freed by dpcspritemax (requested 2026-11-06, checked): 4 variables for each virtual sprite that is off, on top of the 9 var0 to var8 and the letters: a project with 1 virtual sprite and no title or text kernel has 41 bonus slots (9 + 32), measured. The Title Screen Kernel works in 21 of those 32 bytes while the title screen runs (titleKernel in dpcPlusFreedSpriteRam) and the Text Minikernel keeps 4.
- Relocator (changed 2026-11-06, committed and released in 0.53.17, in hooks/rom.js): units are placed by size. It keeps what each bank has left (from the assembler's report of the banks before the one that overflowed, moved by the units put in and taken out), learns a scale for its estimates of the sizes of units from the banks that took units and the exact size of a unit that moved alone between two reports, only sends a unit to a bank it fits in, takes units out of every bank the assembler says is over, takes more out of a bank that overflows again (300, 600, 1,200 bytes) when it does not say how much, and may use the Title Screen Kernel's bank after 8 attempts. The give-up error starts with the plain reason and the build log says which bank overflowed and what each bank has left. Open: the 28-row failure turned out to be the asm block above, so these were only checked on a build that really overflowed bank 5 (banks 2 to 4 packed to within 40 bytes): test with a project that really is over, and the 24-row project builds as before.

PART 3: PXE ROM SUPPORT IN 6502.ts
----------------------------------


Goal: the preview emulator's main backend (6502.ts, `tools/stellerator`) runs PXE ROMs, so they no longer fall back to gopher2600.

### What a PXE ROM is

PXE stands for Phantom logic's eXtended Elf kernel (ZackAttack's thread on AtariAge,
https://forums.atariage.com/topic/381582-pxe-pronounced-pixie-new-elf-based-bb-kernel/ : it is part of batari Basic v1.9, has demo
programs such as `full-playfield.bas`, `paddle-scrolls-pf.bas` and `quadtari-demo.bas`, and runs in gopher2600 and Stella). From that
thread: it is a new kernel written in C and compiled to an ELF file for the cartridge's ARM chip (the Chameleon Cart's is a 64 MHz
Cortex-M3), mostly compatible with DPC+ programs, with up to 60 KB of ROM and no bank switching commands in the program: the ARM
driver does the bank switching itself by watching the 6507's jumps and returns. It adds per-line playfield and background colors,
horizontal scrolling, a 40 pixel playfield and more than 50 extra variables.

- A PXE ROM is an ELF file, run by an ARM program that drives the 2600's bus itself (the "ELF" cartridge type, in the same family as
  the Harmony/Melody and CDF boards, but with no fixed 6502 program: the ARM code executes the 6507 cycles through a bus library).
- In this repo the pieces are `public/bb19/includes/PXE-pre.arm` (the start of the ELF), a 6502 ROM image that begins with the text
  `PXE-ROM`, and `PXE-post.arm` (the rest of the ELF). The kernel (`PXE_kernel.asm`) hands the ARM code the addresses it needs
  (`drawscreen`, `BKCOLS`, `PFCOLS`, `C_function`, the player variables, ...). `PXEheader.asm` and `PXEfooter.asm` build the ROM.
- The app does not build PXE ROMs itself yet; gopher2600 loads one from memory (changelog 0.52.7). The reference implementation is
  gopher2600's ELF cartridge: `tools/gopher2600-wasm/vendor/github.com/jetsetilly/gopher2600/hardware/memory/cartridge/elf/` (about
  3,900 lines of Go: `elf.go`, `memory.go`, `strongarm*.go`, `gpio.go`, `stream.go`, `pxe.go`).

### What 6502.ts has and lacks

- Has: cartridge classes for DPC+ and CDF (`CartridgeDPCPlus.js`, `CartridgeCDF.js`, about 300 lines each), and a `harmony/Soc.js` (196 lines)
  that wraps `thumbulator.ts` (David Welch's Thumb emulator compiled to JavaScript) with a RAM and flash bus.
- Lacks: an ELF loader, any ELF cartridge, the bus-library ("strongarm") functions, and a way for ARM code to run the 6507's cycles.
- The ARM code in this repo's PXE ELF (`PXE-pre.arm` + a 64 KB ROM blob + `PXE-post.arm` makes a valid ELF; `readelf -A` on it) is
  **ARMv6S-M, Thumb-1 only** (`Tag_CPU_arch: v6S-M`, `Tag_THUMB_ISA_use: Thumb-1`), even though the first Chameleon boards have a Cortex-M3.
  So `thumbulator.ts` is a good base: no Thumb-2 core is needed, only the few ARMv6-M instructions it may lack (`BL`, `MSR`/`MRS`,
  `DMB`/`DSB`/`ISB`, to be checked against the ELF's `.text` and `.RamFunc`). The ELF has `.text` (7 KB), `.RamFunc` (8.7 KB of code
  that runs from RAM, the timing-critical part), `.rodata` (67 KB of the kernel's tables), `.bss` (11 KB) and a `.bbpxe` section.

### Steps

0. **Recon (1 day; the ISA question is already answered, see above).** Get real PXE ROMs (the examples under `C:\tmp\pxe-examples` and any `.elf` the user has). Read the ELF with
   `arm-none-eabi-readelf -h -S -s -A` (toolchain in `C:\tmp\armtc`): the CPU architecture tag, sections, symbols (`pRAM`, the bus
   functions it imports), and the relocations used. Decide Thumb-1 or Thumb-2. Run each ROM in gopher2600 and record screenshots
   and a few frames of TIA writes as the reference to compare against.
1. **ELF container (2 days).** Parse ELF32 little-endian in TypeScript: sections, symbols, relocations (the ARM REL types gopher2600
   handles in `memory.go`), the `PXE-ROM` 6502 blob, and the PXE section. Build the memory map (flash/ROM, RAM, the PXE palette region
   `0x700-0x7ff`, pRAM). Unit-test against the same files with golden section dumps.
2. **ARM core (2 to 4 days).** Wire `thumbulator.ts` to the ELF memory map (flash, RAM, `.RamFunc` in RAM) and run the kernel's functions
   one at a time against gopher2600 (same inputs, compare registers and memory). Add whichever ARMv6-M instructions it lacks. The cycle
   counts need to be good enough for the bus timing in step 3.
3. **Bus library ("strongarm") (4 days).** Port the functions in `strongarm_definitions.go`, `strongarm_vcslib.go`,
   `strongarm_support*.go` (`vcsLibInit`, `vcsInitBusStuffing`, `vcsJmp3`, `vcsWrite3`, `vcsLda2`, ...) as functions the emulator
   catches when the ARM code branches to them, and that run the 6507 bus cycles (bus stuffing: the ARM puts opcodes and data on the
   data bus while the 6507 runs). This is the part that has to match the 6502.ts CPU's cycle-exact mode, so expect the most debugging here.
4. **Cartridge class and detection (1 day).** `CartridgeELF` in the style of `CartridgeCDF`, `CartridgeFactory`/`CartridgeDetector` picking
   it from the ELF magic number, 6502.ts's `Soc` hooks for GPIO and streams (`gpio.go`, `stream.go`).
5. **PXE specifics (2 days).** `pxe.go`: pRAM, palette origin and memtop, the per-scanline palette writes, and how they reach the
   TIA color output. 6502.ts needs a way to take a palette color from cartridge memory per line.
6. **App integration (1 day).** `tools/stellerator/worker-entry.js`: accept ELF ROM bytes, remove the automatic fallback for PXE once
   it is solid, keep the gopher2600 fallback while it is not. Update the emulator settings text and the wiki.
7. **Verification (2 days).** Compare frame by frame with gopher2600 on every sample ROM (pixel diff of the first few hundred frames),
   check input, audio and the debug overlay. Add the samples and a small Node test that loads each ROM headless.

Total: about 2 to 3 weeks of work, most of it the bus library and its timing.

### Where the code goes

6502.ts is an npm package (`6502.ts` 1.1.4, MIT) that this repo bundles with esbuild. Options: (a) a fork or a patched copy under
`tools/` bundled by `tools/stellerator/build.js` (recommended: no waiting on the upstream project); (b) a pull request to the 6502.ts
repository afterwards.

### Licensing

gopher2600 is GPL-3; 6502.ts and this app are MIT. A line-by-line port of the Go code would be a derivative work under the GPL. Either
write the new code from the documented behavior (the ELF/ARM specifications and the observed bus traffic), using gopher2600 only
to check results, or accept that the ported files are GPL-3 and keep them clearly separate. Decide this before step 1.

### Done when

Every sample PXE ROM draws the same frames on 6502.ts as on gopher2600 (apart from known sub-pixel timing), audio and input work, and
loading a PXE ROM no longer needs the gopher2600 worker.

### Recon results (2026-11-01, step 0 done on a real ROM)

A PXE ROM can be built here and now: `C:/tmp/pxe/build.mjs` (Node, uses the WASI .wasm files of public/bb19 through node:wasi: preprocess, 2600basic,
postprocess, dasm, the same chain as src/hooks/bb-compiler.js) compiles a program that starts with `set kernel PXE`. A 4 line program and an 8 row
playfield program (C:/tmp/pxe/t1.bas, t2.bas) both give a 91,670 byte main.bin with 61,000 bytes of ROM left. (The AtariAge example attachment
`C:/tmp/pxe-examples/full-playfield.bas` is a Cloudflare error page, not a program.) So the toolchain side already works; the app only lacks a way to
pick the PXE kernel (nothing in src mentions it) and the emulator side below.

What the ROM is, from `arm-none-eabi-readelf`/`objdump` on that main.bin (the ELF is `C:/tmp/pxe/out1/main.bin`):
- ELF32 little endian, type REL (a relocatable object: nothing is linked, the cartridge loader places the sections and applies the relocations),
  ARMv6S-M, Thumb-1 only (every instruction is plain Thumb-1: ldr, movs, adds, lsls, blx, bl, push/pop, uxtb/uxth/sxtb/sxth, muls, ...; no
  msr/mrs/dmb, no Thumb-2).
- Sections: .text 7.4 KB, .RamFunc 8.8 KB (code that runs from RAM), .text.startup 96 B, .rodata 67 KB (holds `DpcpBin`, a 65,536 byte 6502 ROM image,
  and tables such as `PaddleOverblankBin`, 115 bytes), .init_array (one entry, the C++ constructor of `pRAM`), .bbpxe 4 B (`pRAM`, the pointer gopher2600
  reads to find the PXE memory), .bss 11 KB.
- Relocations are few and simple: 415 R_ARM_ABS32, 30 R_ARM_THM_CALL, 1 R_ARM_TARGET1. No MOVW/MOVT, no PC22, no veneers.
- The program is C++: a `CPU` class (execute, read, write, ADC, SBC, ...: 8.9 KB object `cpu`) is a software 6502 that runs the game's 6502 code (`DpcpBin`)
  inside the ARM, a `MultiSprite` class (the kernel, object `kernel`, 2.3 KB) draws the screen, `elf_main` is the entry point.
- It imports only 18 symbols: the bus library functions vcsLda2, vcsLdx2, vcsLdy2, vcsSta3, vcsStx3, vcsSty3, vcsStx4, vcsJmp3, vcsNop2, vcsNop2n, vcsWrite5,
  vcsRead4, vcsStartOverblank, vcsEndOverblank, plus memcpy, memset, randint, ReverseByte. They are called through pointers (`blx` through a literal pool
  word with an R_ARM_ABS32 relocation), never through BL.

What that means for 6502.ts (this changes the plan above):
1. The 6507 does not run a program that is in the ROM. The ARM program runs the game in its software 6502 and tells the real 6507, one instruction at a time,
   what to do: vcsLda2(x) means "the 6507 executes LDA #x" (bytes $A9, x), vcsSta3(zp) "STA zp" (bytes $85, zp), vcsJmp3 a JMP back to $1000, vcsNop2 a NOP,
   vcsRead4(addr) an LDA absolute that returns the byte read, vcsWrite5(zp, data) LDA # then STA. (gopher2600 injects these bytes on the data bus when the 6507
   fetches; the ARM sits in the cartridge.) vcsWrite3 is the bus stuffing one: the cartridge drives the data bus during the write cycle of an STA/STX/STY/SAX so
   the TIA sees `data` whatever the registers hold; vcsStartOverblank/vcsEndOverblank copy a small overblank routine into the console's RAM and hand the 6507 to it.
2. Because every ARM to 6507 interaction is "execute this instruction", 6502.ts does not need the strongarm timing machinery. The cartridge object can be a
   coroutine: when the 6507 fetches a byte from cartridge space and the queue of bytes is empty, run the ARM (thumbulator.ts) until it calls the next vcs function;
   the function (an import trapped by `trapOnBx32`, like CDF's calls, if the imports get even (ARM state) stub addresses) queues its bytes and stops the run
   (return a nonzero trap code), and the next byte request resumes it (write r15 and call `run` again, without `reset`). The 6507 is the real 6502.ts CPU, so the
   cycle counts come out right by themselves. The speed of the ARM is not modelled.
3. Needed: (a) an ELF REL loader and linker (the three relocation types above, section placement: flash for .text/.rodata, RAM for .RamFunc/.bss/.data, the
   .init_array run before elf_main), small; (b) the 18 imports in JS (memcpy, memset, randint, ReverseByte are trivial), vcsRead4 returning the byte the 6507
   actually read, and the bus stuffing write (a hook on the CPU's write for the next write, or a cartridge-side bus override); (c) `Soc`-like memory map for the
   thumbulator (it exists in harmony/Soc.js; its RAM and flash sizes are 8 KB and 32 KB, so a version with 64 KB+ flash and about 24 KB of RAM is needed; thumbulator.ts
   takes any bus object); (d) a cartridge class that the worker picks by the ELF magic. thumbulator.ts (0.3.1, MIT) has the hooks needed: `run(cycles)`,
   `trapOnBx32`, `trapOnInstructionFetch`, `readRegister`/`writeRegister`, `stopAddress`.
4. "PXE specifics" (step 5) shrinks: gopher2600's pxe.go only records, for the debugger, which palette address a color came from (`LastPXEPalette`); the colors reach the
   screen through ordinary TIA writes, so no palette hook is needed in 6502.ts. (To confirm by reading the frames once the first ROM runs.)
5. The estimate drops: no strongarm bus-timing port. Rough: ELF loader 1 to 2 days, Soc and imports 2 days, coroutine and bus stuffing 2 to 3 days, debugging against
   gopher2600 frames 2 to 3 days.

Not settled yet:
- (Settled 2026-11-01, see "gopher2600 and PXE" below: the black picture in gopher2600 was the missing patch 0003; both emulators run the PXE ROMs with the same pictures.) Both emulators showed a black picture for the two test ROMs when loaded through `window.gopher2600.loadRom` in the headless harness (C:/tmp/shots/pxeref.cjs).
  Unknown whether the harness did not select gopher2600 (the setting is read by public/index.html), whether the ROM needs more setup (this app's DPC+ programs set up
  the kernel with a driver; `drawscreen` alone may draw nothing in PXE), or whether gopher2600 needs a longer start. Check with the Emulator Settings dialog and a
  program from the AtariAge thread (the user can supply `full-playfield.bas` and the compiled ROM) before writing code, to have frames to compare with.
- The app itself: a "PXE" choice in the kernel list (Options tab), a generator path (how much of the DPC+ generator the PXE kernel shares: the thread says it is mostly
  DPC+ compatible, with per-line playfield and background colors, a 40 pixel playfield and horizontal scrolling), and the ROM size message (a PXE ROM is an ELF of about
  90 KB, not a 4 KB bank).

Forum files (2026-11-01; thread https://forums.atariage.com/topic/385318-pxe-kernel-templates-tutorials-and-documentation/ by Mike_at_AEI, page 1 read; the
attachments download only through a click in the Browser pane, not by fetch or navigate): saved in `C:/tmp/pxe-forum/`: `17_Sprites_PXE.bas` (8,260 B) and its
`17_Sprites_PXE.bas.bin` (91,702 B), `Playfield_Indexing.bas` (9,186 B) and its `.bas.bin` (91,702 B). Both .bin files are ELF (`7f 45 4c 46`). Still on the page,
not downloaded: 8_Way Scrolling Rev B and C, paddle-scrolls-pf.bas (ZackAttack), Starfield_Glitch_POWERLINE (102,940 B bin), Hi_Score_PXE_Template (a high score table saved
on the cartridge: the emulator will need whatever save storage PXE uses), Screen_Scroll_Lesson2.bas.
- The author's .bin is 32 bytes bigger than what this app's bundled toolchain builds from the same .bas (91,670 B; the bytes differ from offset 33): the author uses a newer
  PXE than bB 1.9's includes (ZackAttack in the thread: PXE sets SWCHA to $00 at start, a bug to be fixed in a later PXE; the thread says bB 1.9 itself was not tested with PXE).
  So both versions of the ELF should be run by the emulator work, and the includes in public/bb19 may need updating from the PXE repository.
- What the templates show of the language: `set kernel PXE` (NTSC/PAL detected by the kernel, no `set tv`), `PF_MODE = $F1` (40 column playfield and the color/row resolution
  by DF0FRACINC, set once instead of before every drawscreen as in DPC+), `DF0FRACINC = 2`, PF_WRITE_OFFSET / PF index and offset registers for drawing into part of the
  playfield, PF_SCROLL variables and `pfscroll up/down/left/right n` (columns are 8 pixels, rows are scan lines in fine mode), 17 sprites (player0 plus 16 virtual) with NUSIZ bit 6 =
  PXE masking, a `simple48` function that turns a 48 column playfield into a 48 pixel sprite, background colors with a separate scroll index in the top 2 bits of PFMODE.
- The harness test: both the author's .bin and ours load (`loaded 91702` / `loaded 91670`) but show a black picture in the in-app gopher2600 (its wasm does contain the
  ELF code: `vcsWrite5` is in public/js/gopher2600.wasm) after 12 s, so the harness is missing something (the load error goes to the console of the worker, which the harness does not
  read). Next step: read that console (CDP `Runtime.consoleAPICalled` for the worker target) or run the same .bin in the installed Stella (C:/Program Files/Stella) to get reference frames.

Progress 2026-11-01 (the steps of Part 3 are numbered as above):
- Test ROMs, all ELF, in `C:/tmp/pxe-forum/` (thread 385318: 17 Sprites, Playfield Indexing, 8 Way Scrolling and Rev B and C, Starfield Glitch, Hi Score; each with its .bas) and
  `C:/tmp/pxe-forum/orig/` (thread 381582: full-playfield, paddle-scrolls-pf, quadtari-demo with .bas). Three PXE versions by size: 91,670 (the first one, the thread 381582 demos),
  91,702 (most of the templates), 92,338 (Rev C, Hi Score), 91,810 (Starfield): the app's bundled toolchain builds the 91,670 one, and its build of full-playfield.bas is
  byte for byte the author's full-playfield.bas.bin.
- Step 1 (ELF container and linker) done, in `tools/stellerator/pxe/elf.js` (parseElf, linkElf: places the allocated sections, applies ABS32/TARGET1/THM_CALL, gives the
  undefined imports even (ARM state) addresses, returns the symbols and the init_array). Tested on the 91,670 ROM (C:/tmp/pxe/run-arm.js).
- Step 2 mostly done: thumbulator.ts runs the linked ELF in a flat memory (any base address works). Facts found:
  - `elf_main(args)`: r0 points to 5 words: the TV (0 NTSC, 1 PAL, 2 PAL60), the ARM clock in Hz, flags (bit 0 multicart, bit 1 Chameleon, bit 2 reduced HMOVE, bit 3 NV storage), elapsed
    and threshold. Without it the first load faults (read of address 8). The .init_array constructor (`_GLOBAL__sub_I_pRAM`) is called first with the return address set to the stop address.
  - A `blx` to an even address does not call `trapOnBx32`: `run()` returns 2 (blxLeaveThumb) with r15 = the address of the blx + 4 (the instruction is at r15 - 4, its register in bits 3 to 6);
    continue by writing r14 and r15 = (r15 - 2) | 1 and calling `run()` again without `reset()` (verified: the program ran on through hundreds of calls).
  - The program starts in `ShowLogo` (the PXE logo) and calls vcsJmp3 once per scanline, as expected.
  - thumbulator prints warnings ("cannot branch to arm", "push {lr} with an ARM address") through its `printer` option: give it a no-op printer.
- Not done: the cartridge object (queue of bytes for the 6507, coroutine, bus stuffing, vcsRead4 result from the CPU's A register, overblank routine of 6502 code copied to RAM $80 with the
  bytes of `overblank` in strongarm_tables.go: it waits for WSYNC lines, VSYNC at line $1d to $20, then `LDX $FFFF; BNE; JMP $1000` where the cartridge supplies the byte at $FFFF), the
  reset vector ($1000), registration with Board, a hook for the worker, PXE memory and save storage.
- In-app gopher2600 shows a black picture for every one of these ROMs (a DPC+ ROM loaded the same way shows its picture), so there are no reference frames from it yet; Stella 7.0 is
  installed (C:/Program Files/Stella) and can run them.

Result 2026-11-01 (later): PXE ROMs RUN on 6502.ts. `tools/stellerator/pxe/CartridgePXE.js` is a cartridge for 6502.ts (picked in worker-entry.js by the ELF magic;
`public/js/stellerator-worker.js` is rebuilt with it). All ten test ROMs (C:/tmp/pxe-forum) run 300 frames headless with no trap and draw sensible pictures: the PXE logo, then
full-playfield (PXE letters, rainbow borders), 17 sprites in every size, Playfield Indexing, 8 Way Scrolling title, Starfield, Hi Score template (castle), paddle-scrolls, quadtari.
They also run in the app (6502.ts backend). Speed: about 5 to 7 ms a frame in Node. Test script: C:/tmp/pxe/run-board.js (Board headless, PNGs of frames), build script C:/tmp/pxe/build.mjs.
How it works (the design of the research above held; two details were different):
- The cartridge keeps no ROM: the 6507 reads the address space at offsets from $1000. A bus library call puts the bytes of its instruction at `next` (offset) and moves `next`: vcsNop2n(n)
  puts one NOP and skips n-1 addresses (the 6507 reads what the data bus still holds, the NOP); vcsJmp3 puts 4C 00 10 and sets `next` back to 0 (the bytes are dropped once the 6507 has read the
  jump); vcsStartOverblank puts JMP $0080 and the 6507 runs the routine the program wrote in RAM; vcsEndOverblank sets the byte at the last address of the cartridge to 0, which that routine
  is waiting for (LDX $FFFF, BNE). The ARM program is resumed only when the 6507 reads the offset where the next instruction goes (the dummy read of an implied instruction counts: it reads the
  next opcode), or reads the last address while in the overblank routine. vcsRead4 returns the A register of the CPU when the program is resumed.
- `ReverseByte` is a data table (256 bytes), not a function: elf.js gives imported tables an address after the sections.
- thumbulator.ts: `run()` returns 2 at a BLX to an even address; r15 reads as the address of the instruction + 4 and the instruction run on resume is the one 2 below r15, so a resume writes
  r14 = return address | 1 and r15 = return address | 1 + 2. thumbulator aborts (throws) on an instruction it does not know; none came up in these ROMs.
Open: joystick, paddles, keypad and audio checks (they use the real TIA and RIOT of 6502.ts, so they should work; not tried); PAL and PAL60 (the TV standard goes to elf_main in the argument
block); the cartridge save storage of the Hi Score template (flag bit 3 of the arguments and its library calls: not imported by that ROM as built, check how it saves); bus stuffing
(vcsWrite3: no ROM here imports it); the other library functions (vcsRead6, vcsSta4/Sty4, vcsJsr6, vcsPha3 ... are in the table only for the ones a ROM imported or that are plain); the
debug overlay and the variables list for PXE (the symbols are in the ELF, not the .sym of the DASM build); the app itself (a PXE choice for the kernel, the generator, the ROM size
messages: a PXE ROM is not a bank file); the Test in Stella button works with the file as it is; gopher2600 in the app showed nothing for them then (fixed the same day, see the next paragraph: it runs them).

gopher2600 and PXE (2026-11-01): the in-app gopher2600 showed a black picture for every PXE ROM because the vendored sources lacked three of the five local patches
(tools/gopher2600-wasm/patches): 0001, 0002 and 0003 were not applied in vendor/ (0004 and 0005 were). 0003 (the ELF cartridge reads the loader's data) is the one PXE needs: without it
the cartridge tries to open a file called "rom" ("ELF: open rom: The system cannot find the file specified", found with a native Go test that loads the ROM the way main.go does:
C:/tmp/pxe/gotest/main.go, run as a package inside tools/gopher2600-wasm). The patches apply from the repository root with `git apply tools/gopher2600-wasm/patches/0001*.patch` and the
other two (a single `git apply` of all five from the sub-directory said "applied" and did nothing). With 0001 to 0003 applied and the wasm rebuilt (README command, 16,685,635 bytes
against 16,683,719; the old file is kept at C:/tmp/gopher2600.wasm.before-pxe) gopher2600 runs the PXE ROMs in the app, with the same pictures as 6502.ts. Uncommitted: six vendor files,
public/js/gopher2600.wasm. Open: whether the committed wasm had patches 0001 and 0002 (a speed gain) before: if the tree was meant to carry them, they were lost in a re-vendor.
Joystick and audio on 6502.ts (headless, C:/tmp/pxe/run-input.js): the joystick moves the sprites of 17 Sprites in every direction (the picture changes with each direction; the logo
plays for the first 180 frames), Fire starts Starfield with sound (non zero samples, peak 0.46). PAL, paddles and keypad not tried.

Frame comparison 6502.ts against gopher2600 (2026-11-01, C:/tmp/pxe/compare.js and the Go dumper C:/tmp/pxe/gotest/main_dump.go, built into pxetest.exe inside tools/gopher2600-wasm): the
gopher2600 frames are the TIA color signals of the frame (palette independent), the 6502.ts frames are mapped back to TIA color indexes, and the two pictures are matched with a vertical shift (the
crop windows differ by 21 lines). Every ROM at frames 100 and 400 (20 frames): 18 are pixel identical (0 of 30,400 pixels), including all the demos at frame 100 and the 17 Sprites, 8 Way
Scrolling (three versions), Hi Score, full-playfield, paddle-scrolls and quadtari at frame 400. The two others: Starfield at frame 400 (1,389 pixels, 4.6%): the ROM uses randint, which gopher2600
answers with an unseeded random number (two gopher2600 runs differ from each other by 348 values), mine is a fixed sequence, so a difference is expected; Playfield Indexing at frame 400 (9 pixels
in the score row, lines 218 to 224 at x 65 to 70, color 41 to 45 against 0), a deterministic ROM in gopher2600 (two runs identical) and a different score picture by a pixel or two: not looked into yet (a
TIA timing detail of the two emulators or something the kernel does that I do not do; the pictures of the frames next to it differ by 23 to 27 pixels because the demo is animated).

Condition from the user (2026-11-01): before PXE is offered as a kernel in the app, every existing block must work with PXE (a pass over the toolbox category by category on PXE, the
way the DPC+ kernel was done: generators, previews, the Test buttons, the debug overlay). PXE is not offered until that is done.

Cause of the 9 pixel difference (Playfield Indexing, frame 400; checked 2026-11-01 with a log of every TIA write on both emulators, C:/tmp/pxe/writes.js and a temporary print in gopher2600's
tia.Update): all 2,525 TIA writes of the frame are the same in register, value, scanline and color clock (a constant 1 clock offset in how the two logs sample the clock), so the PXE cartridge
feeds both emulators the same instructions at the same times. The difference is in how the two TIAs draw them: the score row (scanlines 218 to 225) is a 48 pixel sprite kernel (GRP0 and GRP1
written at clocks 66, 75, 84 and 93 with VDELP set, three close copies placed by RESP1 and HMP1), and the pixels at x 65, 66 and 70 are where a GRP write lands next to the start of a copy.
That is a TIA accuracy detail (the delay of a GRP write or of a delayed GRP latch against the position counter) in 6502.ts or in gopher2600, not in PXE; which one matches real hardware
needs Stella 7.0 (installed) or a real console as the judge. Not worth chasing before the blocks work.

Stella as judge of the 9 pixel difference (2026-11-01): Stella 7.0 runs the ROMs and can save exact TIA pixels without a window to click: a debugger script in %APPDATA%/Stella/autoexec.script
(frame N, savesnap, frame 1, ..., quit; deleted after each run) with `Stella.exe -debug -ss1x 1 -sssingle 0 -snapsavedir <dir> <rom>` writes a PNG per frame (320x228, 2 pixels per color clock).
Of 40 frames of Playfield Indexing (frames 381 to 420), the one with the same score text as gopher2600's frame 400 (the demo is animated, so frames only match for the right one) agrees with
gopher2600 in the whole score region (scanlines 216 to 226, x 40 to 120): 0 pixels differ. 6502.ts differs from both in the 9 pixels (x 65, 66, 70; scanlines 218 to 224), so 6502.ts draws
the 48 pixel score kernel (GRP0 and GRP1 written at clocks 66 to 93 with VDELP0 and VDELP1, three close copies) a pixel differently from Stella and gopher2600. It is a 6502.ts TIA issue (the
package is upstream; the player code is in node_modules/6502.ts/lib/machine/stella/tia/Player.js and Tia.js), found only through PXE here but it would affect any ROM using that kernel.
Open: find which write (a GRP write landing at the start of a copy, or the VDEL latch) 6502.ts treats differently, and patch it in the bundle (a patched copy under tools/stellerator) if worthwhile.

CORRECTION (2026-11-01): there is no TIA difference. The 9 pixels (and the Stella 'confirmation' reading above) came from comparing frames one apart: the frame counters of the two
emulators differ by one (gopher2600's NewFrame count N is the picture 6502.ts calls N-1), and Playfield Indexing is animated. Gopher2600 frame N+1 against 6502.ts frame N differs by 0 pixels
for the four pairs checked (frames 398 to 402), and at frame 100 and 400 the other nine ROMs are static pictures, so they matched either way. So 6502.ts's TIA draws the 48 pixel score kernel
exactly as gopher2600 and Stella do, and the only open comparison item is the random number of Starfield. Several experiments (delays of GRP, shuffle, VDELP, a lag of the players) changed
nothing for the same reason. The debug code put in the vendored gopher2600 for this (tia.go, player.go, video.go) was removed again; the vendor changes left are the three patches.

PXE block pass, first findings (2026-11-01; the user's condition: every block must work on PXE before PXE is offered):
- A hidden switch exists: config.pxe = true with kernel 'dpcplus' makes the generator write `set kernel PXE` (generateRomSize in generators/bbasic.js); not in the Options tab. Test project:
  C:/tmp/vp/pxe_ss.json (the score project with pxe on). It builds through the app and runs (the PXE logo first, 180 frames, then the game).
- The PXE core runs the game's 6502 code in a software 6502 written for bB's output, and it does NOT implement every official opcode. Opcodes found missing (read from the jump table of the CPU::executeInstruction
  of the ELF, then confirmed by running them in a tiny program on all three PXE versions: the program hangs in an endless loop, which CartridgePXE now reports as "the program ran without a bus library call"):
  CPX, CPY (all modes), INY, DEY, TYA, PHP, PLP, CLI, CLV, RTI, JMP (ind), LDA (zp),Y and LDA abs,Y, LDX abs / zp,Y / abs,Y, LDY abs / zp,X / abs,X, STA (zp,X) / (zp),Y / abs,Y, STX zp,Y,
  ADC / SBC / AND / ORA / EOR / CMP with abs,Y and (zp),Y (and abs for ADC, SBC, AND, EOR), most read modify write forms (ASL, LSR, ROL, ROR on memory; ROR A; DEC zp,X and abs,X), AND zp,X and abs,X,
  EOR in every mode but immediate. Implemented: LDA and STA in the usual modes (imm, zp, zp,X, abs, abs,X), LDX/LDY imm and zp, STX/STY zp and abs, INC, INX, DEX, TAX, TAY, TXA, TXS, TSX, PHA, PLA,
  JSR, RTS, JMP abs, branches, BIT, SEC, CLC, SED, CLD, SEI, NOP, plus ADC/AND/ORA/CMP/SBC in imm, zp and a few more. The exact table: C:/tmp/pxe/audit_ops.py (UNSUP) and C:/tmp/pxe/jt.js.
- So every piece of inline assembly the generators emit (the `asm ... end` blocks: animations, scrolling, the text and title kernels, the Jump block, fades, music ...) has to be checked against that table
  when PXE is on. C:/tmp/pxe/audit_ops.py main.asm lists the lines that use a missing opcode (the build of the score project: 29 lines, all from the animation code: cpx, cpy, iny, dey, lda abs,y).
- Also not available on PXE by design: the title screen kernel and the text minikernel (they need the 6507 to draw), bank switching (the PXE build ignores `bank N`, the code is flat in one 64 KB image), the DPC+
  custom ARM program (offset-arm etc.), the DPC+ audio chip functions.
- A rough scan of the assembly strings in src/generators and src/utils for opcodes the PXE core lacks (by mnemonic and addressing form; zero page against absolute cannot be told from the text, so this
  under-counts) finds 94 lines: background.js 42, pf-box.js 17, bbasic.js 16 (animations, loop code, scrolling), soundfx.js 7, speech.js 4, avox-memory.js 3, titlescreen.js 3, sprites.js 1, text-font.js 1.
  The opcodes with no PXE form cannot be swapped one for one (CPX has no replacement that keeps A and the flags; INY and DEY go through a zero page byte: sty, inc or dec, ldy), so each site is rewritten by hand.
- Next: rewrite the animation asm for PXE, then go category by category with the audit tool and a test project per category, on 6502.ts and gopher2600.

### CDF, CDFJ and CDFJ+ as a starting point

6502.ts already runs CDF, CDFJ and CDFJ+ (`CartridgeCDF.js`, versions 0, 1 and 2). They are a poor place to start for PXE: in those boards the
6507 runs the cartridge's own 6502 code and the ARM only fills data streams on request, so none of the bus-driving machinery PXE needs is
there. What they do give: the `Soc` class around `thumbulator.ts`, the pattern for a cartridge with ARM RAM and flash, and test ROMs
that already pass, which is a way to check any change to the ARM wiring does not break them. Start from the ELF cartridge (step 1) and reuse
that part of CDF.


#2. [ ] (In progress 2026-10-20: the user asked to see if the 14 lines of the one text row can be given back to the picture, starting with DPC+.) Text minikernel reserves too many lines when a project uses only one line of text (reported 2026-10-20): at least one more playfield row could be drawn then. Look at what the standard kernel and DPC+ take from the picture for one row of text. Measured on the loaded project (now DPC+, 32 rows): the picture is 164 lines (178 minus the 14 DPC+ takes for one text row, text_minikernel files), then the score (8 lines), a gap, and the text (9 lines, 181 to 189). On the standard kernel at 28 rows: playfield 170 lines, score 172-179, gap 180-184, text 185-193. The 14 lines were measured with no sprites on screen; the game's frames are 265 lines on DPC+, so nothing can be taken from them without the cause of the 3 extra lines (#3). Measured again 2026-10-26 on a DPC+ project with one line of text (frame 100 in 6502.ts): the picture is lines 0 to 163, 3 blank lines, the score on 167 to 174, 6 blank lines, the text on 181 to 189. In those 6 blank lines the hook runs (fast fetch off, the bank call) and textkernel copies the 12 message bytes (about 175 cycles) before its first sta WSYNC (showtextrow); one more sta WSYNC at firstbreak waits again, so that first sta WSYNC (ifnconst extendedtxt in text12DPCplus.asm) wastes about a line: without it the text would start a line sooner. But the picture height is set by a timer with only some working values (195 gives 164 lines, the next, 197, gives 166), so one line saved gives the picture nothing; two would. A whole playfield row (8 lines at 24 rows) is far away. The pointer copy could be made earlier (in the frame's idle time) only with 12 bytes of memory that survive until the text; the temps and SpriteGfxIndex it uses are overwritten by then.

Done 2026-11-05, uncommitted (standard kernel only): the ball's playfield-checked movement looks at the cell it is in after every pixel step, and each look is a pfread, a call into another bank in a ROM of more than 4k; a step that stays in the cell the last look was at (a byte, fireLast, the same cell number as fireCell) now skips the read (generators/bbasic/sprites.js sameCell, buildPlayfieldCheckedMovement). On the 8k test ROM with text (ballpf32t_standard) long frames went from 185 of 1499 to 25 (16 directions) and 183 to 63 (8 directions); the picture is pixel for pixel the same over 1441 frames of Fire presses in both, in the 4k ROM with no text and in the DPC+ project; Bricks is byte for byte the same (DPC+ is left out: its variables are tight and the coprocessor option does its own movement). Costs one byte of RAM for projects with a Fire block that checks the playfield on the standard kernel. DPC+ tried the same way (2026-11-05) and left out: the same ball test on DPC+ without the coprocessor option took 4,906 against 4,908 CPU cycles a frame in the game logic (executed cycles only, 1500 frames, same input), the same pixels, so the playfield read is already cheap there and the byte is not worth it. Not done: the long frames that remain (25 to 63 of 1499, on the frames Fire relaunches the ball); the Options warning for 32 rows with text.
#7. [ ] Title screens: let sprites overlap the graphics (asked 2026-10-20). Not possible with the Titlescreen Kernel as built: the 48x1, 48x2 and 96x2 graphics draw their picture with both player sprites, and the Player card is a separate stacked window; sprites can only go in the Player card. Options to look at: a Player card taller than its sprites as a stand-in picture, or the ball and missiles. (Looked at 2026-11-06, nothing built: the 48x1, 48x2 and 96x2 loops use both players with three copies each for the 6 or 12 blocks of 8 pixels and have 3 to 13 spare cycles a line (48x2 line loop: sleep 3 and sleep 4; the one-color loop sleep 11 and 13), so a picture of a sprite shape cannot be fetched per line. The ball and missiles are free: as vertical bars the height of the card they cost nothing a line (set once in the card's setup, moved sideways with HMOVE; color of a missile = the row color, the ball = the card color), but they are bars of 1 to 8 pixels, not shapes. For a real sprite shape over a graphic the way in is to bake it: the editor draws a Player animation into the frames of a bitmap card (the kernel already has card frames, Index for vertical scrolling and the scroll blocks), costing 6 bytes a row a frame for 48 wide; moving 1 pixel at a time across 48 pixels is 48 frames, 2 pixels 24.)

#17. [ ] Standard kernel: a height for each playfield row with pfheights, per background (asked 2026-10-26; not started). Facts from the AtariAge thread "pfcolors and pfheights together in RAM" (topic 112423): pfheights alone reads the heights through a 2-byte pointer (pfheighttable), so each pfheights statement can point at another ROM table and each background could have its own; the first row must always have height 8, any other value rolls the screen (the probable cause of the rolling when pfheights was tried before, see needsPlayfieldColorTable in bbasic.js); pfheights must be given before pfcolors; pfcolors and pfheights together read one combined table from a fixed ROM address, so it cannot change after compiling (batari: 3 more cycles needed) unless the table is copied into Superchip RAM with custom kernel files (std_kernel_SC.asm, romsize 8kSC or more). Plan: pfheights alone per background with an 8 in the first row, switched when the background changes; a background with row colors needs the Superchip RAM copy. Needs a row-height editor on the background card, and a check against the other kernel options (player colors). DPC+ has no per-row heights: one row step for each of the four columns only.

#23. [ ] Cycles of the Platformer Example (DPC+, 2026-10-27, asked "figure out how to optimize for cycles"). Measured with a profiler over the 6502.ts emulator (scripts in C:/tmp/proj: profile2.cjs by routine, mapasm.cjs, symbuild via a temporary window.__vcsSymbols hook in hooks/rom.js, removed). An idle frame: the DPC+ kernel/driver runs about 3,900 cycles of the vblank (fixed), the Text kernel about 1,150 in the picture, and the game code about 1,600; busy frames about 2,100. Of the 1,600: sprite animations and colors about 430 (5 sprites, each about 20 when idle, a full picture load about 150), the box tests of sprite pairs about 170-200, bank switching about 150-250 (every gosub/goto bank costs about 35-50; the hot addresses are the trampoline at the end of each bank), the text clear timer about 60, the rest many statements of under 25. Done: the box test of two fixed width sprites is assembly (generators/bbasic/sprite-box-width.js), the same compares and 8 bit sums as the statements (checked against them over thousands of random positions); saves about 10 cycles on an idle frame and about 20 per pair that overlaps. Ideas not done: keep what runs every frame (pf box, box checks, the animation code of the sprites that are on) in the main bank and relocate what runs seldom (the build chose banks 3-5 for them), a sprite whose animation is only ever one picture needs no per-frame code, share the sums of the first sprite of each pair, the text clear timer in assembly. Tried: relocating the every-frame box subroutines last; the build then moved _sprite_box_checks out anyway and the game code measured 2159 cycles against 2061 (same random input), so it was dropped. Done after: a finished one-shot animation on DPC+ left the animation code at once (it had been searching its pictures on every frame, about 60 cycles); the build no longer moves the every-frame subroutines (box checks, edge reset) out of the main bank, neither on an overflow nor to free room (hooks/rom.js runFrequency, EVERY_FRAME); the clear timer check is assembly (no measurable change). Game code on an idle frame of the Platformer Example: 1588 -> 1425 cycles; player x over 1500 frames of random input is identical to the old ROM. Then: a one-shot animation held on its last frame (DPC+) is no longer entered at all: the code puts a 1 in its limit (animnext) and the main bank skips it (bbasic.js quickPast/heldCheck); a write of the frame or animation puts 0 there again. 1425 -> 1280; frame counters, animations and positions identical over 1500 frames. Then: every animated DPC+ player has its limit byte (the constant-limit shortcut for one-picture animations of one length is gone, since a held one-picture animation needs the byte): 1280 -> 1145, same checks; costs a byte of RAM for each such player. Tried: all fixed width box pairs in one assembly block with the ends of each sprite's box worked out once and the flag bit set in assembly: 1147 against 1145, no gain (the pairs mostly end at the first compare), dropped. Then: the fast path of the animation step branches on a paused player instead of the others (1145 -> 1143). A sprite a visibility block can hide (DPC+: frame 255) now has no box while hidden (generators/bbasic/sprite-box-width.js, hideablePlayers): the pairs it is in look at its frame after the height compares. Costs 7 cycles on the idle frame of the Platformer Example (players 2 and 3 have visibility blocks and nothing is hidden), 1143 -> 1150. Off screen sprites are not skipped.

#33. [ ] AtariVox features (asked 2026-10-28; built in 8276b94 and after, checked 2026-10-29). Left: stop does not clear the chip's own buffer (up to 64 bytes already sent are still spoken; asked 2026-10-29 to clear it: the chip has no serial command for that, code 31 only puts volume, speed, pitch and bend back to their defaults and the Reset line is not wired on an AtariVox, so it can not be done; gopher2600's chip model agrees); the limit is 254 phrases (a phrase is called by a one byte number); the say blocks' tooltips now say 254 phrases, not bytes (2026-10-29). Not tried: real speech on hardware, the blocks in a finished game.

#37. [ ] Title screen colors inside a scanline (asked 2026-10-29, after reading the AtariAge copyright kernel in Downloads/aa.asm: it turns COLUBK and PF2 on and off at fixed cycles of every line, and uses the ball and both missiles as extra colored bars, black missiles to trim the edges): for graphic cards as well as text cards. Looked at, nothing built; the options for the ball and missile bars were put to the user and none was chosen (2026-10-29: told not to build any of them). Facts: the 48x1, 48x2 and 96x2 kernels in public/bb19/titlescreen use every one of their 76 cycles on every line (48x2: both lines of the loop add up to 76; 48x1 already writes COLUPF from aux4 at cycle 34 of each line). The ball and the missiles are not used by those kernels (turned off in titlescreen_kernel.asm), but the kernels set NUSIZ0 and NUSIZ1 to 3, three copies 16 pixels apart (the comments in the kernels say twice 32 apart, which is wrong), and the missiles follow that: a missile bar is drawn at x, x+16 and x+32, so only the ball makes a single bar. A ball bar costs no cycles per line, takes the color of COLUPF (the card's background color, as the playfield bars do), and could only be placed in the margins left and right of the graphic without an extra positioning line (position48 strobes RESP0 at cycle 40 and RESP1 at 43 of one line, with spare cycles before and after them for more strobes). A mid-line COLUBK or PF2 change needs cycles: the 5 cycle (scorepointers),y loads of a card that does not animate or scroll could become 4 cycle absolute loads (as the AA kernel does), 6 cycles a line. Text cards (text12a.asm) and space cards were not looked at in detail. Any of it needs a pixel check in 6502.ts.
#54. [ ] AtariVox 5 channel synthesizer (asked 2026-10-29; on hold, nothing built): the SpeakJet's synthesizer can be set directly through its Serial Control Protocol (SCP: a null byte enters it, X or A leaves it; a command is a number and a letter: 'nJ' picks the register, 'vN' stores the value, 0J envelope frequency, 1J to 5J oscillator 1 to 5 frequency, 6J distortion, 7J master volume 0 to 127, 8J envelope control, 11J to 15J oscillator 1 to 5 volume; 'R' clears the input buffer, 'S'/'T' stop/start speaking, 'V' says ready, 'W' hard reset; sources: midibox.org's speakjet_control_overview and the SpeakJet manual, the bits of the envelope control register and the ranges not read). The chip emulator (tools/gopher2600-wasm/vendor/github.com/jetsetilly/speakjet, msa.go says there is no SCP) would need it, with a byte-stream write in speakjet-wasm/main.go, the AtariVox peripheral of the vendored gopher2600 (a sixth patch in tools/gopher2600-wasm/patches, and both wasm builds: gopher2600.wasm and speakjet.wasm), tools/stellerator/atarivox.js and public/js/speakjet-worker.js, which now send the chip commands of 1 or 2 bytes. Forms asked about: blocks only (set oscillator frequency and volume, distortion, envelope, master volume, off), synth sound cards on the Sound tab, or emulator support only. A bonus: SCP 'R' is a serial command that clears the chip's buffer, which #33 said did not exist, so 'stop sending speech' could clear it too (only if the chip does that; to check on the manual).

#62. [ ] Singing: notes changing the pitch of speech (asked 2026-10-30). Done in the first form (see the Singing and speech with notes entry): one PITCH per note, at the start of the phrase of the note, so every word of the note has that pitch. Not done: the form with a pitch change for each sound inside a phrase (a 'sing' instrument that sends a phrase one sound at a time just ahead of the chip, with a PITCH before each sound that starts on a new note: steps at sound boundaries, 50 to 150 ms late at best, no glide), because the PITCH command goes at the end of the input queue of the chip and takes effect only after the sounds already queued. The direct synthesizer control (#54) does not help for speech: the chip's speech sequencer overwrites the registers while it speaks.

#78. [ ] Text tab: the "Pronunciations (AtariVox)" field is hidden for now (v-if="false" in src/views/TextEditor.vue); show it again when the AtariVox pronunciation list is wanted (2026-10-28).

PXE block pass, step 1 (opcode legalizer):
- src/utils/pxe-legalize.js rewrites the asm...end blocks of the generated code for PXE (INY, DEY, CPX, CPY, LDA/ALU abs,Y, TYA not used), through three reserved bytes (pxeA, pxeX, pxeT); hooked in workspaceToCodeOnce. The score project now runs past the logo on 6502.ts (600 frames, no trap).
- Open: the score digits look wrong in that run (555554 then 444444); other unsupported forms are not covered yet (ASL/LSR/ROL/ROR memory, ADC/AND/ORA/EOR abs, LDX abs, STA abs,Y, (zp),Y, PHP/PLP): rewrite when a block emits them. The player1 animation code writes DF0HI/DF0WRITE (DPC+ only), so it needs a PXE form.

PXE block pass, step 2 (first comparison with the DPC+ build of the same project, C:/tmp/vp/dpc_ss.json vs pxe_ss.json, frames side by side from C:/tmp/pxe/run-dpc.js and run-board.js):
- Score works (counts 111111 per step; 555554 at frame 600 is right, it is 14 adds mod 10^6).
- Sprites, colors and the playfield are wrong on PXE: the picture is about half the height of the DPC+ one (stripes end near line 110 of 190), player 0 draws a smaller brown shape than the DPC+ big one, the DPC+ blue balls come out yellow/pink bars. The animation code writes the sprite data through DF0LOW/DF0HI/DF0WRITE; PXEstartup.asm sets DF0FRACINC so the PXE core may emulate fetchers, but the tables behind them (P0GFX, P1COLOR ... in fetcher_address_table, PXE_kernel.asm) have to be checked against what the generator assumes.
- Next: find why the picture is half height (KERNEL_LINES / row height settings the generator emits for DPC+ vs the PXE kernel: PXEbB.h has KERNEL_LINES = 178*76/64), then sprite data and colors.

PXE block pass, step 3 (score project now matches the DPC+ picture):

PXE step 14 (2026-11-02): scrolling backgrounds. Checked with C:/tmp/bbx/gscr_up, gcol_up, gcol_nog, gscr_down (compared DPC+ and PXE). They build and run, but the Background scroll and Background scroll colors blocks are wrong on PXE: the generated `pfscroll n a b` statements compile to the DPC+ coprocessor call (function 32 through CALLFUNCTION), which PXE does not have. PXE scrolls with PF_MODE (cbfo vpww), PF_VER_SCROLL_LO/HI, PF_HOR_SCROLL_LO/HI, PF_WRITE_OFFSET and `pfscroll up/down/left/right n` (see C:/tmp/pxe-forum/8_Way Scrolling_Rev_B.bas), so the blocks need a PXE generator path. PXE step 16 (2026-11-02): AtariVox on PXE, tested with C:/tmp/proj/speech_dpcplus.json plus enableAtariVox (builds C:/tmp/pxe/b/vox_d.bin and vox_p.bin; decoder C:/tmp/pxe/pvox.cjs, the 6502.ts AtariVox line decoder with CartridgePXE; the speech starts when framecounter+59 is a multiple of 256, so run 330 frames, plus 181 on PXE for the logo). DPC+ decodes [183,131,145,164,5,254]. PXE builds but traps at once in `_avox_step`: `lda (temp3),y` (opcode $b1) is not in the PXE 6502, and `lsr temp1` (memory shift) is not either. The bit loop is cycle counted (62 cycles a bit), so a replacement must keep the cycle counts (`lsr temp1` is 5 cycles, lda/lsr/sta is 8: shorten the sleep by 3). Ideas for the indirect read: a 4 byte routine in RAM (`lda abs` / `rts`) with its address bytes written first, if the PXE 6502 runs code from RAM (not tried), or the DPC+ ROM copy function. The memory chip (I2C) blocks and the sound effect blocks are the same kind of cycle counted code and are not looked at. Needs a PXE variant of the _avox_step generator (generators/bbasic/avox-memory.js, soundfx.js).

PXE step 19 (2026-11-02, asked "do it", uncommitted): the Memory blocks and the High scores blocks on PXE use its save data, without an AtariVox (the speech blocks still need one). `set save_data 128 128 $0300 $0380` (utils: PXE_SAVE_STATEMENT in generators/bbasic/avox-memory.js) makes two slots in the extended RAM; the generated `_avox_mem` subroutine has a PXE variant (registerMemorySubroutine(..., pxe)): the I2C parts are replaced by `_mem_save` / `_mem_load` on $0300,x with every bit flipped (never saved reads 255 like a blank AtariVox memory), and a job that changes the memory ends with `jsr write_savedata_0` and `_1`. PXE step 20 (2026-11-02, uncommitted): (a) memory/high score data now survives a page reload (the worker keeps it in IndexedDB 'vcsgm-pxe-save', keyed by size and a hash of the ROM: a new build is a new game); the key was wrong before (it read the bus map, not the ROM) and is fixed. The Memory blocks moved to a Save/Load toolbox category that shows with the AtariVox or on PXE; Vox keeps the speech blocks only. A compile note says the save file number of a block is ignored on PXE. (b) Playfield box collision on PXE (Collided ... use box): the 6502 can not read the playfield columns through the data fetchers there (DF0DATA gives 0; the columns live in buffers of the ARM program), so the box test asks pfread (ARM function 24: A is 0 when the cell is lit) for each cell (generators/bbasic/pf-box.js pxeScanLines / pxeReadCell, pf-box-sub.js pxeRead). Checked on the gscr project with a 12 row, 16 line configuration: 147 hits in 150 frames. Found: when the project's pfres does not match the rows of the background (the gscr test project says 96 rows, its background has 12 rows of 16 lines), the box rows are worked out from the project's setting on every DPC+ build too, so the box lands on the wrong rows (a DPC+ issue, not looked at). Open: the user asked 'remove old forks' (not done: unclear which, asked).

The save data uses $0300 to $03FF, so the 1024 bytes of extended RAM are all taken when both are used. PXE step 22 (2026-11-02, uncommitted): Playfield draw horizontal/vertical line now draws a pixel at a time on PXE (the ARM program does nothing for pfhline and pfvline; background_change_hv_line in generators/bbasic/background.js); checked: hline, vline and the diagonal line (pfpixel based) are read back lit with pfread. Data streams on PXE (point, write, read): pointers in the last bytes of the extended RAM ($06F8 to $06FD, so index 122 to 127 of Display RAM area 5 are not for the project's data when streams are used), three subroutines per direction reached by gosub; checked: three bytes written in area 1 from index 4 and read back in order; a stream past $06FF writes into the palette at $0700. PXE step 23 (2026-11-02, uncommitted): AtariVox speech on PXE is not possible, and the keypad neither. Found by building a PXE variant of the speech step (pages of 256 bytes read with `lda page,x`, the shift done in A with the sleep in the bit loop 3 cycles shorter; this ran, 6 phrase bytes were sent, but no write reached the RIOT): the PXE ARM program talks to the 6507 only through the bus library of its ROM (vcsLda2, vcsLdx2, vcsLdy2, vcsSta3, vcsStx3, vcsSty3, vcsStx4, vcsWrite5 [LDA # and STA zero page], vcsRead4 [LDA absolute], vcsJmp3, vcsNop2/2n, vcsStartOverblank, vcsEndOverblank, and the NV store calls); every write it can make is to a zero page address (the TIA), so SWCHA and SWACNT ($0280, $0281) can not be written. Made so: the AtariVox is off on PXE (avoxEnabled false, a note in the build log, no Vox category in the toolbox, no AtariVox instrument watch in music.js) while the Save/Load blocks work through the save data; the keypad blocks answer 0 with a log note (the keypad rows are written to SWCHA). The memory blocks and everything else needs no RIOT write. Also: the SLEEP macro on PXE uses BIT, not the illegal NOP, for odd counts (`const NO_ILLEGAL_OPCODES = 1` after `set kernel PXE`): before, an odd SLEEP trapped the PXE 6502; the Playfield offset block says in the log that it does nothing on PXE (needs an ARM program). The all-blocks builds of Input, Playfield, Sprites, Data still run. Not possible, so: Playfield offset, AtariVox speech and sound effects, keypad, DPC+ chip audio, title screens and Text (until the user decides to port them).

PXE step 24 (2026-11-02, uncommitted): the AtariVox option is not shown when the kernel is PXE (Options checkbox, the Vox tab and its menu item, the AtariVox choices of the Music, Sound effects and Text editors; importing something that needs the AtariVox does not turn it on there).

PXE features still to add to the app (asked 2026-11-02: list every remaining feature the PXE kernel supports; none of these is a block or an option yet; sources: the PXE templates in C:/tmp/pxe-forum, the includes in public/bb19/includes (PXEheader.asm, PXEbB.h, PXEfooter.asm), the author's thread https://forums.atariage.com/topic/381582-pxe-pronounced-pixie-new-elf-based-bb-kernel/ and https://forums.atariage.com/topic/385318-pxe-kernel-templates-tutorials-and-documentation/):
 P1. Wide playfields (40 columns done, step 34; 80 and 120 not): PF_MODE width ww = 1 (40 columns, 160 pixels), 2 (80 columns / 320 pixels; the newest includes say 8 columns of 32 pixels, 256 pixels) and 3 (120 columns, 480 pixels), five, ten or fifteen column buffers of 256 rows; `playfield:` loading by pfsetup1 to pfsetup15 (N columns at once) with PF_WRITE_INDEX (the first column written) and PF_WRITE_OFFSET per column. A Playfield tab / background option for the width, a way to design maps wider than one screen (the author's Paddle-Scrolls-PF and 8 Way Scrolling templates; marty and Mike_at_AEI asked for a multi screen world editor).
 P2. Horizontal scrolling (done in step 35 except wrap, fine mode and the editors' viewport marker): PF_HOR_SCROLL_LO and HI (pixels, with the 40/80/120 column widths), `pfscroll left|right [n]` and `pfscroll up|down [n]` as blocks (a block per direction with an amount), fine scrolling (PF_MODE bit p: -128 to +127 scanlines) with PF_VER_SCROLL_LO/HI per column, wrap around the map.
 P3. Per column control (PF_MODE bits c b f o v): separate PF_FRAC_INC, PF_WRITE_OFFSET and PF_VER_SCROLL per playfield column and for the playfield colors and the background colors (parallax: a column that scrolls faster than the next), or one set for all.
 P4. Reading the scroll position (PF_VER_SCROLL_LO/HI, PF_HOR_SCROLL_LO/HI) as value blocks, so a map knows where it is (the 8 Way Scrolling template does it by hand).
 P5. Playfield to 48 pixel sprite: `simple48` / SIMPLE_48_START and SIMPLE_48_END (turns a 48 column playfield into a 48 pixel wide sprite with the playfield colors: titles, big letters; used by the 8 Way Scrolling title).
 P6. GFX_MODE (the newest includes): 0 normal multisprite kernel, 1 the 48 pixel sprite mode, 2 no score at the bottom, the top kernel fills all 188 lines. An option on the Options tab (and "Show score at bottom" on PXE could use it).
 P7. The sprites mode of the newest includes: SPRITE_0, SPRITE_1 to 16 (the picture a sprite shows), CEL_0 / CEL_1.., INK_0 / INK_1.. (the color of a cel), CEL_TOP_OFFSET and CEL_BOT_OFFSET, `___SPRITE_DATA`: sprites made of cels (tiles of a picture) rather than the DPC+ style pointers; to look at in the includes before deciding what a block would be.
 P8. Four joysticks with the Quadtari: joy2 and joy3 (and their fire buttons) as Input blocks and in the preview (Player 2 port controls of players 3 and 4), flicker advice (3 scanlines between virtual sprites).
 P9. Four paddles: Paddle0 to Paddle3 as variables and PaddleRange0 to PaddleRange3 (0 off, else the range 0 to n); input blocks for paddles (the standard kernel's paddles blocks only know two); the paddle sensitivity of PXE is about twice the standard kernel's (the author says it is expected: a scale option).
 P10. Palette shifting / color cycling: the 256 byte PALETTE table at $0700 (NTSC or PAL colors copied at start) can be changed while the game runs (a block: set palette entry, rotate the palette, load a table).
 P11. PXE sprite masking: bit 6 of NUSIZ (the lower 8 virtual sprites have it on, automatic horizontal masking of players 1 to 16 for every NUSIZ option); a "mask" choice for a sprite and the player 1 to 8 / 9 to 16 difference (the author leaves the top 8 unmasked only in his demo; all 16 can be masked: done, step 31).
 P12. Mini kernels (miniKernelCount, mkMultiSprite, mkScore, mk48Pixel in the header): extra kernels stacked on the screen (a score bar, a multisprite band, a 48 pixel band), the framework the author is building (his backlog: tiles, a driving kernel, a pinball template); to follow.
 P13. Save data in full: two slots (`set save_data size0 size1 start0 start1`, `writesavedata 0|1`, slot 0 up to 127 bytes and slot 1 up to 127 bytes), a block to write a slot of the project's variables, load on start (the Hi Score template: 50 bytes of table plus one byte of game state); wear levelling is the cartridge's job. Today only the Memory and High score blocks use it (256 bytes in one file).
 P14. More variables: var0 to var60 (done), the 1024 byte extended RAM at $0300 to $06FF (`autodim init $0300 $0700`, `autodim byte name n`, `autodim` arrays: a block or an Options setting that puts user variables there instead of running out), the 8 free zero page bytes and 188 bytes of stack page.
 P15. Reading the playfield from the 6502 (pfread, ARM function 24) as a fast "is this cell lit" with the PXE fixes (pfread bit order, inverted result: A is 0 when lit), pfpixel / pfclear (done); pfhline, pfvline are not in the ARM program (done by pixels in the app); the playfield row heights per column group (DF0FRACINC to DF3FRACINC, DF4 and DF6 for the colors, all the same value in PXE mode 0).
 P16. NTSC / PAL detection by the console (no `set tv`; the palettes in PXEheader.asm): an option to keep a project to one standard, a block to read which one the console is (the kernel knows), and PAL60.
 P17. More CPU time: the program runs on the ARM during the blank lines with more cycles than a 6502 would have (the author: 6507 code is emulated on the ARM; the console only reads controllers): measure the time a frame of blocks takes (the offset block, area blocks) and show it on the Options tab like the cycle display of the other kernels.
 P18. Export: the ROM is an ELF `.bin` of 105,406 bytes for the beta 46 includes (the thread: it works on Stella, Gopher2600, PlusCart, UnoCart, and the Chameleon Cart): a download dialog that says which devices take it; the Chameleon Cart `.elf` of the package's pxebin2ccelf (PXE_CC_pre.arm and PXE_CC_post.arm, with a vendor and a game GUID); the ROM size line of the Options tab (a PXE ROM is not 4k to 64k banks); the ROM capacity display (variables, RAM, extended RAM).
 P19. Emulators: the preview runs PXE only on 6502.ts (CartridgePXE); gopher2600 does not run it (the setting must fall back or say so); PAL frames are not compared; nothing is checked on a Chameleon Cart or PlusCart.
 P20. The templates the author gives as examples to ship as example projects when PXE is offered: 17 Sprites, Playfield Indexing, 8 Way Scrolling, Hi Score template, Paddle-Scrolls-PF, Starfield Glitch POWERLINE, zombie_chase and pxe_full_playfield (from his fork's samples).
 P21. Options entry for PXE and the existing project conversion (kernel list shows "PXE (beta)" only when every block works, per the standing condition in memory: not offered until all blocks work), a note in the wiki and the About text (only when told).
 P22. Kernel state of the app items done for PXE that still need a look: the Playfield offset block (done, see step 25), Title screens, Text minikernel, score fonts (DPC+ score fonts are off), and the DPC+ fast math (the stock routine is used).

PXE step 28 (2026-11-02, uncommitted): checks that were open. (a) Playfield offset fallback tested: with Display RAM filled by fill blocks the working bytes were 3 in Display RAM and 3 variables, then all 6 variables; both give the same result as the free case (C:/tmp/pxe/offtest.py, env FILLS=area:count,...). (b) Run-time collision of Players 10 to 16: the box test (Collided block, box ticked or not) answers 1 for overlapping and 0 for apart pairs of Players 10 to 16, and 14 pairs at once (two flag bytes of boxHitFlags, so families over 8 bits work) all answer 1 when overlapping (C:/tmp/pxe/coltest.py and coltest2.py). A pair with Player 0 or Player 2 was not a fair test in that project (Player 0's y moved to 104 by itself, Player 2 was off screen), so the pair is not shown to work.
(c) A user variable named like a single letter (the usual "i") was declared as `dim i = a` and failed to assemble with EQU: Value mismatch on every kernel (2600basic.h defines the letters); the letters a to z are now reserved words of the variable names (generators/bbasic.js), so such a variable is called i2 and so on. The Math category builds on PXE again; on the Standard kernel the mismatch is gone.
(d) Rainbow colors on Players 10 to 16: not applicable, the Player rainbow colors block only offers Players 0 and 1 (PLAYER_OPTIONS in blocks/sprites.js). (e) Still open from the checks: the scene editor with Players 10 to 16 in the browser (the user tests in the browser), the DPC+ pfres-mismatch bug (a project whose pfres differs from its background rows puts the playfield box rows on the wrong lines), flag families past 8 bits for the other flag kinds (only the box hit pairs were run).

The size variable could not hold it: bits 4 (animation does not loop), 5 (finished), 6 (paused) and 7 (colors loaded) are all used. PXE step 35 (2026-11-02, uncommitted): P2 horizontal scrolling and the 64 and 120 column playfields. Options "Playfield width" now offers 32, 40, 64 and 120 (utils/pxe.js PF_WIDTHS, pfWidthOf, pfIsWide, pfMaxScrollOf, pfBuffersOf; PF_MODE $39, $3a, $3b = ww 1, 2, 3 with f, o and v set). Facts found: PF_HOR_SCROLL_LO and HI (6502 address $0802 and $0803) are 16 bit pixels, 4 to a column, and the kernel shows the playfield from that pixel (the furthest is (width - 40) * 4); a 120 column background compiles to pfsetup15, a 64 one to pfsetup8; with f set the kernel takes the row step from the registers PF_FRAC_INC, PF_FRAC_INC_PFCOL and PF_FRAC_INC_BKCOL (PXEheader.asm, $0804, $0840, $0844), not from the DFnFRACINC statements, so utils/pxe.js fixFracIncs sets them to the same values (wide only); the vertical pfscroll then takes column 0 only (pfscroll of 0 to 4 queues became 0 0 plus the color registers: scrollColorsForPxe), otherwise it would scroll once for each column. New blocks in the PXE category: scroll left/right by N pixels (stops at the edges), scroll to column, scroll column (getter). The sprite to playfield column maths (find pixel, collision pixel, box test against the playfield in pf-box.js, missile and ball bounce, sprite to pixel and pixel to sprite blocks) add the scroll (pfScrollColumns, pfScrollFraction in utils/playfield-coords.js; 16 bit in the box test). Backgrounds of the wide playfields have no separate rows for groups of 8 columns (backgroundGroupPfres returns one number), and a width change keeps the picture where it was. Checked in the emulator: a 120 column map scrolls (right 12 pixels, then 100, to column 77, left 20 and right 255 stops at the last column; the columns on screen match: C:/tmp/pxe/hscroll.py), 64 columns stops at column 24; box tests against a wall at map column 12 with the playfield scrolled by 10 columns, and by 10 columns and 2 and 3 pixels, give the same hits as the unscrolled wall at column 2 (shifted by the pixels: widebox.py 64 10 2); pixel to sprite, sprite to pixel and the scroll getter give 39, 35 and 10 for scroll 10 columns and 2 pixels; the existing vertical scroll tests (up, down, left, right, color scroll, no groups) give exactly the same frame at 40 and 120 columns as at 32 (gs.py).
