PXE step 40 (2026-11-02, uncommitted): P3 and P4 finished. P3: the option is now "Each column group has its own scroll and row step (parallax)" (pxeSeparateScroll; PF_MODE with f and v clear: $11, $12, $13). Found: with f clear the first four groups take their row step from the DF0FRACINC to DF3FRACINC statements the program runs before every drawscreen (so a register write does not last), and groups 4 and up from PF_FRAC_INC_n; so in this mode the DF statements take their value from four variables (pxeGroupStep0 to 3, with a flag byte that runs the start values once: utils/pxe.js mirrorGroupSteps) and the new block "column group G row step S" writes the variable (groups 0 to 3) and PF_FRAC_INC_n. Test (colscroll2.py, 40 columns, stripes every 4 rows of 16 lines): step 8 gives 32 line rows (stripe 17 to 48), 32 gives 8 line rows, 64 gives 4 line rows, for groups 1 to 4, group 0 left alone. pfscroll (the background scroll blocks) does not move separate groups in step (with every column scrolled the picture came out blank or torn), so in this mode the playfield columns are scrolled by the group blocks only and the colors still scroll with the background blocks. The tile loader writes the write offset of every column (dpc-tile.js columns). P4: new block "column group G top row" (PF_VER_SCROLL_LO_n) gives the row set by the group scroll block (7 and 33 for groups 2 and 4 set to those; group 0 unset 0). The Playfield scroll position block did not build on PXE or DPC+ with the tall scrolling (it used a row variable that only the old scrolling has): it now reads the rows of the background less the offset the scroll follows (generators/bbasic/background.js): three scrolls down read 3, then one up 2, at 32 and 120 columns on PXE (C:/tmp/pxe/b/sp32.json, sp120.json). Not tried on DPC+; a background that does not wrap (taller than 255 rows) returns the offset, not checked. Not done: separate write offsets and colors per column, the background scroll blocks with separate groups.

PXE step 41 (2026-11-07, uncommitted): the sprite pool on PXE. The pool's swap code used CPX, CPY, TAY and LDX with an absolute address, which the PXE core does not have (see the list under "PXE block pass"): on PXE the exchange of two bytes goes through the stack (lda, pha, lda, sta, pla, sta) and the exchange of a flag bit uses two pushed bits and CMP #0 (generators/bbasic/sprite-pool.js). Compared with DPC+ by building each pool test as a DPC+ and a PXE ROM (C:/tmp/pxe/batch.sh with the projects C:/tmp/vp/pool*.json made by C:/tmp/shots/mkvp.py; C:/tmp/pxe/b/cmp_pos.py gives the position of each colored sprite in frame 300 of both; PXE draws 4 lines lower): pool1, pool6c and pool6e come out the same; pool2, pool3b, pool3c, pool4, pool4b (a pooled sprite at x 79 y 53 on DPC+, x 118 y 42 on PXE), pool4c and pool4d (x 64 against 56), pool6 and pool6b (a different sprite color is left), pool6d and pool6f (x 79 against 114) differ. Not found yet which blocks make the difference (those tests mix Lock, Seek, Inertia, Gravity, Bounce, Distance, collision pixel); the swap opcodes were not it (same positions before and after the change). Next: one block at a time in a pool, DPC+ against PXE, starting with Lock and Seek; the opcode audit (C:/tmp/pxe/audit_ops.py) of the pool's generated code needs the main.asm the app makes (build.mjs misses the picture data symbols), so a hook that saves it from the app is needed.

New request (2026-11-02): a block that sets, clears or flips the playfield pixels in a circle: the user gives the center (x, y) and the radius in playfield pixels. To do after the compile problem of Bricks of Olympus (found: the project builds at commit 02f99ae and not at 6fd5e7a, so not from the PXE work; the cause is in src/generators of 6fd5e7a: restoring the collision, pf-box, pf-box-sub, sprites and background generators did not help).

Circle block on DPC+ (2026-11-02, uncommitted): the block is now done by the coprocessor (function 96 plus 0 set, 1 clear, 2 flip in public/bb19/includes/custom/armprog.c, circle(); built four ways with arm-none-eabi-gcc 10.3 -Os -mcpu=arm7tdmi -mthumb -fPIC, with and without -DGROUPS and -DNOCIRCLE; the old two hex arrays in offset-arm.js come out byte for byte from -DNOCIRCLE): every row in one go, no screen roll, no variables (the numbers wait in temp1 to temp6; a block with more than six numbers that are not plain numbers goes the 6502 way as before), and the inline code per block is about 20 lines instead of 120 (8 circle blocks overflowed a bank on the 6502 way). Checked: same playfield as the 6502 way on a 24 row playfield with pads on every side, set/clear/flip, radius 15; separate group rows draw without trouble. Programs with the circle are about 310 bytes bigger, so only a project with the block gets them. PXE and the standard kernel still draw it on the 6502 (a row at a time): still open to speed up. Not done yet: the ball collision bugs (see above).

Ball and missile playfield columns on DPC+ (2026-11-02, uncommitted): measured with the hardware collision flag (a one pixel object swept over one lit cell, on the plain DPC+ test project and on Bricks of Olympus 0.0.17, columns 3, 10, 20, 28): the ball touches column c for ballx 17 + 4c to 20 + 4c (a ball of width w from w - 1 less), so the column is (x - 17) / 4, where the code had (x - 20) / 4 (wrong for 3 of every 4 positions: the check of the cell the ball is in while it moves with Check playfield while moving, and the Find playfield pixel collided with block, both looked at the wrong column, so a lit cell was missed or the pixel found was not the one hit). A missile: (x - 14) / 4, and its top line is y + 1 (the ball's is y). The wide ball no longer shifts its left column by one under DPC+. Counted in the real game with a paddle that follows the ball: 56 of 95 collision frames had a lit cell under the ball by the old column and no hardware collision (the ball crawls through a brick), 0 with the new one. PXE keeps its old numbers (not measured); the standard kernel too. Still using 20: the ball and missile box test of the coprocessor (armprog.c box(), kind 3, groups build only). Open: the rest of the pass-through (the cell check is skipped while the bounce stage bits are set, and only the top left cell of the ball is looked at). The test projects: C:/tmp/pxe/geo.py (sweep), C:/tmp/inj4.py (counters in Bricks of Olympus).

Ball near a wall (2026-11-02, uncommitted): the cell check of a fired object with Check playfield on was skipped for the 3 frames of a Bounce stage whatever the object did, so a paddle turning the ball round next to a wall let it through the wall. Now bit 7 of the stage byte is set at the start of the frame only when the object is inside a lit cell (sprites.js buildPlayfieldCheckedMovement), and only that skips the check. Version 0.53.13 (package.json). Undo of a circle block: its number blocks are made without undo events. Paddle flicker seen with the circle on the coprocessor (Bricks of Olympus 0.0.17): it depended only on where the bank packing put things (a zero filled 256 byte data subroutine: fine; 600: the paddle never drawn; 900: drawn every other frame; the 900 layout puts Player 0's Paddle in bank 2 and the rest of Player 0 in bank 4). So a player whose pictures are split over banks by the relocator can draw wrong: not fixed, the user saw it gone with the current build. Open: find why a split player draws wrong (the color table and picture pointers of an animation in another bank than the default frame).

DPC+ cycles of a ball hit and the paddle flicker (2026-11-02, uncommitted): (1) A hit frame of Bricks of Olympus cost about 1,000 cycles more than an idle one (the idle frame is already 264 to 266 lines, so the first hit rolled): the 6502 routines _findpixel_ball_16 (about 350 cycles) and _bouncepix_ball (about 900) are now functions 99 and 100 of the coprocessor program (armprog.c findpixel()/bouncepix(), the same cells in the same order; sprites.js buildPixelReflect and background.js buildFindPixelBody call it when DPC+, not PXE, a Fire heading without Inertia, one row height, no scrolling and a power of two row divisor, else the 6502 routines stay). The program has four variants for the features (circle 'c', pixel 'p') and the same with groups: offset-arm.js PROGRAMS, built by C:/tmp/arm/build2.sh (-DNOCIRCLE, -DNOPIX). Checked: the 6502.ts emulator with the same input over 1500 frames: the zero page is the same every frame in both ROMs (the stack bytes and one scratch byte aside); in the app, frames with a hit were 20064 or 20216 cycles like idle ones (before: up to 20672, 71 heavy frames in 2460, now 6). The 32 DPC+ coprocessor programs (public/bb19/includes/custom/bin) are made from main.c with C:/tmp/armbuild/bv2.sh (-Os -fno-tree-loop-distribute-patterns, arm-none-eabi-gcc 10.3; all 32 shipped ones came out byte for byte from the current main.c before the change below). (2) The paddle drawn every other frame: the text minikernel keeps a sprite of no height (player 3) at the line of its row, 153, and checkswap() of main.c counted it as overlapping the paddle (y 142, height 6: 153 - 142 - 5 = 6 is not more than 6), so the two took turns being left out. checkswap now never calls a sprite with no height overlapping (it only keeps the order): main.c, all 32 bins rebuilt. The flicker then looked like it depended on the layout of the ROM, which it did not: the 6502.ts emulator showed it with the old ROM too. Checked in the app: the paddle is drawn on every frame. Not done: a hidden player (frame 255) draws a blank picture of 1 line and still counts as a sprite, so it can still take a turn with a sprite near its y.

DPC+ coprocessor programs by function and fire/bounce on the coprocessor (2026-11-03, uncommitted): (1) The programs are now files public/bb19/includes/custom/prog/<letters>.bin, one for each set of the functions o (offset block), b (box test), c (circle), p (pixel finding and bounce), f (fire movement), then g for groups: 62 of them, made from armprog.c by C:/tmp/arm/build... (arm-none-eabi-gcc 10.3 -Os -mcpu=arm7tdmi -mthumb -fPIC with -DNOOFFSET, -DNOBOX, -DNOCIRCLE, -DNOPIX, -DNOFIRE, -DGROUPS). The generated code has a ';ARM_PROGRAM key rows' line and hooks/rom.js fills it with the bytes (fillArmProgram in offset-arm.js) after each generation; the Generated tab shows the line only. A project with the Undo block and the circle and the ball needs about 1.8 KB of it instead of 2.5 KB, which is why Bricks of Olympus with an Undo last move block compiles again. (2) The bounce stage code of a Fire object (function 100) and the Fire movement (function 101, only when two or more objects are fired: the program part is about 840 bytes against about 560 for one object's 6502 code, and each object then only has a wrapper of about 130 bytes) run on the coprocessor under DPC+ (not PXE; Fire heading without Inertia; one row height; no scrolling; else the 6502 code stays). Checked with the 6502.ts emulator and the same input: the ball, a 16 direction missile and an 8 direction one (900 frames, 845 different states) and Bricks of Olympus (1500 frames) have the same x, y, direction, stage and fractions in every frame as the 6502 code. The thumb emulator of the cartridge has no signed loads (ldrsb traps the program): tables in armprog.c are bytes with an offset. Not done: the 6502 wrappers of find pixel, bounce stage and fire could share one routine for the objects (missile0, missile1 and the ball have their x, y and height in neighbouring bytes), and the sprite count of a project with players fired is not measured.

Reset playfield scroll block (asked 2026-11-03, done, uncommitted): background_scroll_reset in blocks/background.js (toolbox: after Set playfield scroll to row). Standard kernel: the same as Set playfield scroll to row 0 (scrollToRowCode in generators/bbasic/background.js); DPC+ with a tall background: the queues are scrolled by what is left of the way back (rows less the offset byte, or the offset for a scroll that stops at its ends) and the offset byte (and the ones of colors-only scrolls) is put back, so the picture is the one a background load starts with; without a tall background on DPC+ it only says so in a comment. Checked on DPC+: a scroll up and a reset in the same frame leaves the playfield as it is without any scrolling (the sprites aside). Not done: PXE horizontal scroll and the parallax rows (the PXE scroll blocks have their own scroll to 0), sprites that follow the scroll are not moved back.

Inertia (2026-11-03, uncommitted): Accelerate's direction dispatch (a line for each of the 12 or 20 directions, each compared in turn, per object, for 8 way, 16 way and Fine) is a table of 16 entries for x and for y (_inertia_table_x and _inertia_table_y, made once in generateInertiaChecks: bit 0 moves, bit 1 subtracts, bit 2 half rate; an 8 way direction is the entry of twice its number) and about 40 bytes of assembly an axis (axisCode in sprites.js), on every kernel. Checked on DPC+ in the 6502.ts emulator against the old code (kept for the test behind a localStorage key, since removed): player1x and y in 700 frames of a direction that changes every 6 frames, 8 way (170 positions), 16 way (307) and Fine (661): equal in every frame; the standard kernel builds. Not measured: the bytes and cycles (the emulator's per frame cycles do not show the game code, the kernel waits for its timer): estimated at 100 to 200 bytes an 8 or 16 way object and 150 to 250 cycles a frame while accelerating. Not done: the max speed clamp and the Decelerate step (assembly already, about 30 bytes each axis), the position add, and moving the whole inertia step to the coprocessor (it would pay from the second object like the fire movement).

Inertia on the coprocessor (asked 2026-11-03, tried, not kept): a function of armprog.c for one frame of Accelerate, Decelerate and the movement (14 bytes in, 9 out: x, y, the velocities, the sixteenths of Fine, the direction, the rates, the max speed, the flags, the frame counter) compiled to 742 bytes of program (the same tables and clamps in C, written to match the 6502 code, which has since been table driven). The 6502 wrapper that hands an object's numbers over and takes them back is about 170 to 190 bytes against about 250 for the code it replaces (the dispatch is a table now, the clamp, Decelerate, auto stop and the move are assembly), so it would save 60 to 80 bytes an object and pay the program back from about the tenth object, and the call itself (23 byte writes and reads) takes about as many cycles as the 6502 code does while it accelerates. Dropped: no gain in ROM or cycles for any number of objects a game uses. A single call that does every object (the numbers of each in an array) would pay, but the velocities are separate bytes for each object spread over the variables.

Fire movement, hidden players, ball box offsets (2026-11-03, uncommitted): (1) The 6502 Fire movement is table driven (FIRE_TABLES in sprites.js, 7 tables of 16 bytes made once; the step of the main and the other axis, the sign and which amount an axis takes): about 90 bytes for the plain movement and about 40 for each step code of the playfield check, the ball's movement 564 -> 384 bytes including the tables, and no handler or compare chain per direction (checked against the old code over 1500 frames of Bricks and 846 states of a ball, an 8 and a 16 direction missile). (2) The coprocessor does the movement of an object that checks the playfield (function 101, program letter f) only when nine or more objects do: about 85 bytes less each and 150 to 300 cycles a frame less at speed 2 or 3, against a program of 720 to 840 bytes; equal to the table code over 846 states (gate lowered to 1 for the test, since put back). A plain object stays on the 6502 (about 100 cycles, less than the call). (3) A hidden DPC+ player (frame 255) has height 0 so the sprite sort does not count it. (4) The coprocessor box test of a ball or missile takes its column offset in the sixth byte of the call (17 ball, 14 missile) and missile 1's top line is y - 1 with one more line (objectBoxGeometry, measured). Headroom of Bricks of Olympus 0.0.17 with the Undo block absent: it builds with a 2400 byte padding subroutine and fails with 3500 (the number the Project tab calls bytes free is the display data region's zero padding, not space). Variables of Bricks: 19 generated bytes (waitFramesCounter, 2 animnext, _ctrlpf, 3 flag pools, rainbow color, fire dir, fire fraction, bounce stage, 2 collision pixel, function result, score bk color, 3 text scroll, title text flag) and 11 of the user's; no packing found in the generated ones (they are already shared bits). The user's brickMin/brickMax could be read from a table and bounceCountMax is a constant per stage.

Gravity block for fired objects (asked 2026-11-03, done, uncommitted): object_gravity (Missile 0/1, Ball): Start with pull (256ths of a pixel a frame, each frame), max fall speed (pixels a frame) and what a bounce keeps (100/75/50/25/0%); Stop. It uses the vertical speed of the object's Fine inertia (not the Jump block's eighths), adds up on top of Fire's movement, and Bounce (screen edges or playfield pixels) turns the fall speed around and scales it (buildGravityKeepAsm); a fired object heading straight along x that is falling is bounced by the pixels around it. Gravity block reworked (2026-11-03, done, uncommitted; asked: player sprites, fewer variables, other RAM): works on the players too (the dropdown has all objects); no longer built on Fine inertia. An object has one signed byte for its fall speed (pixels a frame, in the read/write RAM pool: Superchip RAM or DPC+ display memory when there is room), the Start block's numbers (frames between speed-ups as a mask, max fall speed, bounce keeps) are constants when every Gravity block of the object has the same number blocks, else bytes in the same pool, and one shared flags byte. The fall speed grows by 1 every 1/2/4/8/16/32 frames. A Bounce takes back the last move of the fall speed and turns it round, keeping 0-100% of its size. Checked on the standard kernel: a missile fired along x falls, bounces on a playfield floor and settles; DPC+ and the player objects compile. Gravity does not look at the playfield itself.

Fired object pinned in the wall (2026-11-03, done, uncommitted; DPC+ coprocessor Fire option not equivalence-checked): an object that starts the frame inside a lit playfield cell (the paddle pinned it against the wall) used to creep through the wall a pixel a frame (it stopped on the first step in a lit cell every frame). Now it may move in the cell it started in, but a step into another lit cell is taken back (buildPlayfieldCheckedMovement, and firemove in armprog.c, all prog/*.bin rebuilt). One shared byte (read/write pool) for the cell, no longer the stage bit 7. Checked on the standard kernel with a Bounce called every frame: the ball stops in the first lit cell and stays.

Gravity at rest, fall-through, cost (asked 2026-11-03, done, uncommitted): a Bounce that leaves the fall speed at 0 now turns Gravity off for the object (done; the ball rests instead of falling through the floor on DPC+). Cost of the pinned-ball check (done, uncommitted): an object with a Bounce block looks at the cell it starts in only when it ended its last frame in a lit cell (bit 7 of its stage byte, which the stage masks and the coprocessor's bounce now keep), so normal flight pays nothing; an object without a Bounce block still looks every frame. Checked: pinned ball stops in its first lit cell (stage byte 182), normal flight leaves bit 7 clear (54); DPC+ gravity ball rests at y=140 instead of falling through. Gravity step, gravity bounce and the cell check were not moved to the coprocessor: the call (writes to the data fetchers and the read back, about the cost of the 50-cycle step itself) costs more than they save; with the Fire coprocessor option on, Fire and the cell check already run there.

Main loop room (asked 2026-11-03, done, uncommitted): the long per-frame checks of the main loop (Fire with Bounce, Seek, Lock, Inertia with Gravity, Jump) are subroutines when they are 700 characters or more (wrapChecks in generators/bbasic.js: _fire_checks, _seek_checks, _lock_checks, _inertia_checks, _platformer_checks), so a build whose main bank is full can move them to another bank (about 100 cycles a call then, about 20 in the bank they are in). Not checked: wide pictures (16 pixel sprites with a partner).

Standard kernel frame length at 28 and 32 rows (2026-11-03): with a score, one text row and the two players on screen the frame is 262 lines at 24, 28 and 32 rows, with and without pfcolors (C:/tmp/shots/bands.cjs, PFRES, PFC); the 268-271 lines measured on the Bricks project (2026-10-20) could not be reproduced here, so nothing is changed; it needs that project's settings.

Text minikernel leftovers (2026-11-03, checked, uncommitted): (1) Title screen text is centered on both kernels: a 12 letter message under a page or in a text card spans x 57 to 102-104 on the standard kernel and DPC+ alike (center 80, the 48 pixel graphics are 56 to 104; harness C:/tmp/shots/ttcenter.cjs); a shorter message looks off center only by its justify padding (PRESS FIRE: ink 61 to 96). (2) The background color block's extra cycle: the text of the same message with and without the block (color 0) is pixel for pixel the same on the standard kernel, and on DPC+ only the player sprite differs (it moves between runs); nothing to compensate. (3) The one text row's reserved lines: on DPC+ at most 1 line is left over (the waited out line below the text, 1 row with the score) because the picture timer only moves in steps of about 2.4 lines; nothing more can be given to the picture. (4) Row 2 on a tall title page cannot be shown (the picture shows about 200 lines): the compile log warning is all there is to do.

Ball and walls, lean angles (asked 2026-11-03, done for the 6502 Fire, uncommitted): measured with a ball fired at all 16 angles from the middle (C:/tmp/shots/bounce.cjs, state machine of blocks; logs of C:/tmp/bbx/gravlog.cjs, dpclog.cjs; analysis bounce_an.py, barrier_an.py, stuck.py): in an arena with walls 8 and 4 pixels thick and speeds 1 to 7 the ball never left the arena on the standard kernel and DPC+ (6502 Fire and the coprocessor Fire alike); it ends a frame up to the speed's worth of pixels into a wall (2 to 8 lines on the standard kernel at speed 5 to 7, 4 on DPC+) before the Bounce turns it. A staircase of cells touching at the corners across the arena (the worst case for a ball at a lean angle) was crossed at speeds 5 and 7 on the standard kernel: the Bounce looks at the collision a frame late, and in a frame where an object that had just entered a wall was still counted as stuck on an earlier wall (the stage of the Bounce) it moved on through the cell. Fixed in buildPlayfieldCheckedMovement and firemove: an object that comes into a lit cell from a clear one resets the stage (the next Bounce is a first one); in the cell it started in it only crawls a step a frame until a Bounce has turned it; an object stuck for all the stages of the Bounce is let move on (the freeze along a wall seen with the coprocessor Fire). After the fix no crossing on the standard kernel (speeds 4 to 7, both slopes) or DPC+ with the 6502 Fire. Open: with the coprocessor Fire option a diagonal staircase can still be crossed at its corner (110 to 150 frames in 2000 right of it at speed 6; plain walls are fine): the ball is reflected in one axis only there; not looked at further.

Ball and walls, second round (2026-11-03, uncommitted): the stage reset and crawl rules of the first round were completed after the pinned ball test (a Bounce called every frame with the heading aimed into the wall) ran on through the wall again: (1) an object in the cell it started in crawls a step a frame until a Bounce has turned it (stage not 0); (2) a step into another lit cell is always taken back, except for an object held by all the stages of the Bounce whose heading is no longer the one the stage byte kept (that was the freeze along a wall in the coprocessor Fire runs and the standard kernel's corner), so an aimed-at-the-wall object is held; (3) the start cell is looked at every frame: the bit 7 gate (only after a frame that ended in a lit cell) made the pinned ball tunnel again for a reason that was not found (a trace showed the Bounce's stage byte changing between the two halves of the probe), so it is not used (about 150 cycles a frame for an object that checks the playfield and has a Bounce). Checked: standard kernel, speeds 3 to 7, plain walls, thin walls, a staircase barrier of both slopes: no crossing, no freeze; the pinned test holds at x 26. Staircase corners (done, uncommitted): a staircase of cells that touch only at their corners was crossed on DPC+ (112 and 147 of 2000 frames at speed 6): the reflect stepped back out of the wall along the axis the heading mostly goes along, which can land on the far side of the corner. It now looks at the pixel one step back along the heading first: if only the column changed the object came in along x, if only the row did, along y, and that axis is stepped back first (the heading's guess stands when both changed). In the 6502 reflect (buildPixelReflect, temp5 bits 5 and 6) and in bouncepix of armprog.c (62 prog bins rebuilt). Checked: DPC+ both slopes at speed 6: 0 crossings (was 112 and 147), standard both slopes at speeds 5 and 7: 0, no freeze, the pinned test holds at x 26, plain arena at speeds 3 and 7 stays inside the walls.
Untested spots, tried (2026-11-03): (1) AtariVox mute: a project with the AtariVox on and a Say block, 500 frames in 6502.ts: 792 writes to SWCHA with Mute in-game audio off, 0 with it on, on both kernels. (2) Standard-kernel text with Squish and Squish Custom: builds, frame 262 lines (259 of 259), the text sits 3 pixels higher than with the default font. (3) Gravity with the coprocessor Fire: the same trace as the 6502 Fire (fired straight along x, playfield floor: slides along the floor). (4) NOT working, found: gravity on a fired object with a diagonal heading and a playfield floor (8 or 16 directions, 6502 or coprocessor Fire): after the first floor hit the ball rises at a steady rate with no gravity (the bounce leaves no fall speed, so gravity is switched off as at rest, and the stage code then turns the old heading round again), and with a full bounce (keep 4) it hovers at the floor with its x turning over and over. A heading straight along x is fine. Not fixed. (5) The reflect's new pixel-back rule is not used for objects with Gravity (their fall speed moves them too). Not tried: a title Text card with Show text beyond the tt4/tt5 projects, Gravity with 16 directions plus Accelerate.
Bricks_of_Olympus_0-0-20 would not compile (2026-11-03, fixed, uncommitted): (1) "Too many variables: 42, 41 available": the byte the Fire movement keeps the cell it started in (fireCell, added in the walls work) took a slot; it now shares the playfield box test's hit byte (pfboxhit) when the project has the box test (the two are never live at the same time). (2) The project sits at 98% of the banks (27,740 of 28,364 bytes, bank 1 full) and the bank search failed at 64 attempts with small additions to the 6502 bounce routine (7 bytes were enough). Gravity objects now use the coprocessor's Bounce under DPC+ too (bouncepix takes a gravity flag and the fall speed as arguments 9 and 10, reflects by the fall speed's vertical way, and gives bit 7 of the stage byte back when the vertical way was turned; the 6502 then bounces the fall speed): the 6502 reflect routine _bouncepix_ball (several hundred bytes) is not generated for it. The staircase pixel-back rule in the coprocessor is cheaper (column only, 24 bytes instead of 80). Checked: the project builds; DPC+ staircase both slopes 0 crossings, plain arena in bounds, gravity keep 2 and 3 rest on the floor and stop (8 and 16 directions, any angle tried).
Gravity at rest (asked 2026-11-03): an object that a bounce leaves no fall speed stops all its movement (its Fire stops too, not only its gravity); no sliding along the floor. The root of the floor trouble was found: the reflect used the heading's vertical way (a ball fired up and along x that gravity brings down onto the floor was taken to have hit what is above it, so it turned both ways), now the fall speed's vertical way is used (6502 reflect and coprocessor bouncepix). Left: a full bounce (keep 4) still goes through the floor after some bounces; Accelerate on a fired gravity object keeps pushing it (inertia moves it without a playfield check), so it does not rest and can go through the floor.
Full bounce (keep 4) falling through the floor (checked 2026-11-03, not a bug): the test arena had a floor and no side walls, so the ball bounced along the floor to its right end (x 146, the last playfield column) and fell off it. With side walls, DPC+, 900 frames, keep 4: 8 directions angle 1 and 3, 16 directions angle 3: max y 142 to 143 (the floor is at about 140), x 23 to 137, never through the floor. The note above that keep 4 goes through the floor is wrong.
Accelerate stops at rest too (asked 2026-11-03, done, uncommitted): when a bounce leaves a gravity object no fall speed (the rest that also stops its Fire), its inertia velocity (and the fine fractions) goes to 0 and its Accelerate and Decelerate actions stop (buildGravityBounceAsm, 6502 reflect only: an object with Inertia does not use the coprocessor Bounce). Checked on DPC+ with walls: 8 directions, Fire angle 1 with Accelerate toward 2: lands, rests at (108, 140) and stops. Not solved: 16 directions Fire angle 3 with Accelerate toward 6 (down and right) keeps going through the floor and wraps round: the Bounce turns only the Fire heading (the inertia velocity of an object that has Fire is not reflected, as before), so the Accelerate pushes it back into the floor every frame and the fall speed never comes to 0.
Accelerate velocity reflected with Fire (asked 2026-11-03, done, uncommitted): the reflect of a Bounce turns the Inertia velocity of an object that also has a Fire heading, not only the heading (velocityFlip in buildPixelReflect). The earlier note that an object Accelerate pushes toward a wall goes through it was wrong: the test arena had no side walls (the harness grav3/grav4 only drew the floor; with WALLS the walls are there). With real walls 8 pixels thick, DPC+, Fire plus Accelerate plus Gravity, 8 and 16 directions, max speed 2, 6 and 8: the object stays inside the walls and on the floor, with the plain unchecked Inertia move. A playfield-checked Inertia move was built (a pixel at a time, stopping at the first lit cell) and tried: it let an object through the left wall in one case that the unchecked move held, so it was taken out again; not needed.
